the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. - i know . mr president, ladies and mr president, this debate covers ten special reports by the court of auditors on matters of such a diverse nature and relevance that they range from a reflection on the principle of additionality in structural measures to a concrete and rigorous analysis of the community's cereals policy. let me therefore confine myself to this last subject, beginning by congratulating our colleague mr mccartin and the court of auditors on the excellent work they have both done, and by recalling the final points of the court's analysis. from 1993 to 1997, overcompensation to the cereals sector amounted to more than eur 13 billion. each european citizen contributed more than €37 to the cereals budget in 1997. arable crops accounted for 43% of agricultural expenditure but only 10.7% of final output. almost 40% of the grants go to 3% of farmers, and 57% of the beneficiaries receive 4.5% of the grants. from this analysis, the court of auditors concluded that the current system of support for farmers' income, based on elements such as price, production or area under cultivation, should be replaced by a standard net income per farm or work unit, with agricultural support being triggered to compensate for any loss of that income. the cap for arable crops is the most important aspect of a cap that needs to be reformed. the deep political reshaping of the structure of the german federal government is a clear sign that, even in the heart of europe, where this cap was conceived and where it has always found its great support, it is increasingly unsustainable to maintain an agricultural policy model designed to defend an industrial and large-scale farming logic. a rural policy guided by regional criteria and equity between farmers must be designed to preserve europe's cultural heritage and the environment. that means reforming the present cap.

in writing. - (pt) since the unesco general assembly adopted the convention for the protection of the world cultural and natural heritage in 1972, which has already been signed by 158 countries, including the 15 member states of the european union, 630 properties have been inscribed on the world heritage list, of which 188 (30%) are located in the countries of the european union and 10 in portugal. it is obvious that it is not enough to classify the properties as world heritage. we must take all measures to support their conservation, including financial support, as we have done with the proposals tabled in the community budget. as the rapporteur points out, there is a disproportion between european countries and the rest of the world in terms of recognised natural and cultural sites, so that third countries must be helped and encouraged to complete the list of natural and cultural heritage, in a broad perspective, so that they can benefit from protection. it is also important that member states comply with existing directives, in particular the eia directive on the assessment of the impact of public and private projects on the cultural and natural heritage, as stated in the report.
in writing. - (pt) this report is deeply contradictory in its assessment of the various aspects of the cap. for example, it adopts positions which reveal a strictly budgetary concern when it welcomes the cap reform on reducing agricultural prices and surpluses and insists on a greater reduction in compensatory payments, without saying a word about the consequences for farmers' incomes. it also advocates the principle of cofinancing, i.e. the renationalisation of the cap's costs, a principle which we reject. it also welcomed the commission's decision to undertake a broad evaluation of the milk mco, following closely an opinion of the court of auditors which tends to call into question the quota system and its increases in agenda 2000, forgetting that, despite certain injustices in the allocation of quotas, as in the case of portugal, this system still guarantees, to a certain extent, price and income protection. as for the pig sector and animal diseases, it finally considered and supported the proposal to create funds with the contribution of producers or other types of insurance schemes, which is a step backwards from the opinion already expressed in the garot report, which defended these funds co-financed by the community. however, i agree with the rapporteur when he raises a very important problem with the arable crops regime, namely the concentration of aid on the large producers and the most prosperous regions. it is necessary to improve the situation of small producers by calibrating support according to size. it is difficult to understand how the rapporteur intends to support small farmers with the measures he is advocating.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in the field of the environment and public health the main problem with european legislation is not that it fails to produce adequate legislation. the problem is, on the one hand, the slow transposition of directives into national law and, on the other, their lack of application. the environmental directives under discussion, on pcbs, nitrates and habitats, are examples of this. it was of little use to us to adopt the habitats directive and to set up the natura 2000 network to protect sites of community importance when the commission has been waiting since 1995 for all the member states to submit the definitive list of these sites, together with their plans, rules and management mechanisms. the same applies to the pcb directive. it was of little use to define in terms of the protection of public health the prohibition of pcbs and the decontamination of all equipment containing pcbs and pcts when, after five years, the overwhelming majority of member states not only failed to implement the directive but also failed to do the simplest thing: pcb inventories and decontamination plans. we are therefore faced with a general picture of non-compliance and for that reason these directives have not produced the environmental results that were expected. but we also face a certain impunity. the slow pace of community litigation and the infrequent application of economic penalties to member states which have failed to comply have sent two negative political signals. those who do feel no incentive to continue, those who don't feel no pressure to change their lives. it is therefore essential that the commission, as guardian of the treaties, monitors the whole process of transposition and application of community legislation, publicising the performance of all member states and ensuring that those who do not comply are punished by example.
- mr president, commissioner fischler, i would first of all like to congratulate the committee on agriculture and rural development and in particular its rapporteur, neil parish, on the timely initiative of bringing this subject before the european institutions. it is now 32 years since sicco mansholt, the commissioner responsible for agriculture from the commission's inception until 1972, presented his plan for reforming the common agricultural policy, the mansholt plan. i believe that the european institutions have not yet paid due tribute to this great figure in european politics. i am re-reading this classic today and i continue to find in it many of the necessary answers to the problems facing young farmers, as well characterized by this report by neil parish. we have set up a heavy common policy based on subsidies per hectare, per head of cattle or on the production obtained; we have limited production rights and established quotas; we have imposed complex mechanisms for standardising production; we have set up intricate systems of intervention on international markets. the results were mixed, with some positive aspects but many other negatives. the effects of this policy on the establishment of young people in agricultural activity are particularly striking. the valuable study produced on this subject for our parliament confirms that land remains the biggest investment for european farmers. and that the difficulty of access to it is the main problem facing young farmers. according to this study, for each percentage point increase in the wheat subsidy, the price of land increases by 0.4%. this effect is significantly enhanced by the production quota and cap systems. we cannot therefore face the imperative of protecting and supporting the modernisation and rejuvenation of agriculture without thinking about changes to be made to the current cap. the alternative to this model of agricultural policy is necessarily to have a policy which is geared to the farmer and his income, and not to the product, the land or the livestock, through which it is obtained. it is a policy of regional rather than sectoral agriculture, and it gives priority to the environment, rural development, vocational training and early retirement. finally, i would suggest to neil parish and the committee on agriculture that this report should not be a point of arrival but rather a starting point for a thorough reflection on the renewal of agriculture and the reform of the common agricultural policy with the involvement, in particular, of the associations of young farmers. the celebration of the mansholt plan would be a good starting point.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, when we realize that 55% of european farmers are over 55 and 23% are under 44, we cannot help but conclude that the future of agriculture in the european union is in danger. the situation is even more serious in my own country, where those figures are 66% and 15% respectively. since the mid-1980s, the cap has started to develop measures to strengthen direct control of production in order to avoid surpluses and to ensure price and income stability. however, the downside of this option for stability was the creation of rigidity in access to agricultural activity, which particularly penalises young farmers, firstly by the difficulty of access to quotas, production rights or premium rights, and secondly by the increase in the price of land due to the added value created by the quota schemes. on the other hand, it is clear that many member states have not implemented measures which respond in the least to the concerns of young farmers. i give the example of my own country, where, after an initial period of enthusiasm following european accession, the activities of the most representative young farmers' organisation have been discredited and even penalized in recent years. it is in this sense that some fundamental measures must be taken urgently, in line with what the rapporteur is proposing, namely the creation of special reserves of quotas and production rights exclusively for young farmers, the increase in support for first-time planting and investments in farms, the introduction of a special subsidised credit scheme complementing the other investment support schemes, the creation of conditions for the proper functioning of a system of early retirement for older farmers to speed up the renewal process and, of course, the adoption of training and information measures. the commission must now urgently submit proposals for action and the council must approve them. if we do not do this, we will be acting irresponsibly and endangering the continuity of agriculture in the european community and all that it means for our civilization. in conclusion, i would like to thank our colleague neil parish for the magnificent work he has done on this report.

the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, i would just like to ask for your understanding that, before i go into the subject of my report, i should be allowed a minute to speak on the question of morocco; i would like to thank mr fischler warmly for his efforts in the negotiations for the renewal of the fisheries agreement with morocco, and i should like to extend this applause to the commission's directorate-general for fisheries. since the beginning of the negotiations we have been confronted with an intransigent stance on the part of the moroccan negotiators, who have used the arguments of 'sustainability of fisheries resources' with us to reduce the possibilities of access to the european union's fishing fleet. however, we know that these were not the real arguments, because they did not use the same criteria with other third countries with which they concluded agreements and in private agreements with various operators, some of them from the european union. given the importance of the moroccan agreement to the european union's fisheries economy, this issue should have been given top political priority at the very top of the european council, the foreign affairs council and, of course, the fisheries council. and the impression i get is that i saw mr fischler and his services practically alone in this fight, with the president of the commission making an important but sporadic intervention. it is hard to believe that the european union, which is home to so many millions of moroccans and which grants morocco so many trade facilities for its products to access our market, has shown such a lack of reciprocity towards our attitude of openness and cooperation. there were therefore important political assets at stake for the community authorities at the highest level, with the help of the member states most involved. but we found that those assets were not played. why would you do that? we shall see why in due course, but in any case i very much hope that a reasonable negotiation can be reached quickly. let me now turn to the subject of my report: guinea-bissau. the protocol governing fisheries relations between the european union and the republic of guinea-bissau was initialled in june 1997 and entered into force in december of that year, with a validity period of until june of this year. following the armed conflict in the country in 1998, the commission submitted a proposal for a decision to the council and parliament calling for the suspension of the agreement. in the girão pereira report at the beginning of 1999, parliament considered the commission's proposal to be justified and legitimate, and gave its favourable opinion, thereby authorising the suspension of the agreement for the duration of hostilities, after which the suspension would be lifted and aid granted to the government of guinea-bissau for ad hoc measures to support the local fisheries sector, infrastructure and control activities. following some procedural and legal problems raised by an earlier commission proposal, the commission now presents a new proposal. the purpose of this new text, as in the previous one but with a different legal wording, is to grant the legitimate authorities of guinea-bissau an amount equivalent to the unpaid part of the financial compensation provided for in the current agreement, which amounts to some eur 6.5 million and is intended exclusively for supporting local fishing activities, strengthening control measures and rebuilding the fishing infrastructure that has been damaged in the meantime. the commission, on the basis of an action programme submitted by the government of guinea-bissau, will pay 50% of the financial compensation provided for and the remainder when the government of guinea-bissau has submitted a detailed report on the implementation of the planned actions and the results obtained, which must be submitted to the commission by 31 may 2001. however, the payment deadline should be extended, since guinea-bissau has had enormous problems with the administrative organisation of its government following the armed conflict. the committee on fisheries of this parliament has therefore approved the deadline for processing payments as 31 may 2003, instead of the original proposal of may 2001. so, since the commission's proposal is basically the same as the one we agreed to in march 1999, since the financial amount is exactly the same as that in the minutes, and since the proposal has precise objectives, i believe that parliament should give a favourable opinion on this proposal.
- mr president, commissioner, i would like to draw your attention to a point which seems to me to be an omission in the common fisheries policy. it would be important for it to be taken into account in the reform from 2002 onwards, since it relates to the situation of fisheries in the outermost regions. it was important that the common fisheries policy should also read the amendment to the treaty of amsterdam and take account of the potential which this situation offers - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to make three brief comments on the prospective revision of the common fisheries policy. point one: access to water and to domestic resources. the european union must be concerned about the maintenance of the traditional activities of each fishing community. it is therefore essential to preserve at least the reserve of access to the so-called 'territorial sea', particularly in view of the fact that small-scale fishing activities are based in a decisive way on the coastal resources of each member state. secondly, the principle of relative stability and the full use of fishing opportunities outside community waters. the principle of relative stability is a fundamental pillar of the common fisheries policy. however, the application of this principle to fishing quotas obtained by the union through financial compensation, either in agreements with third countries or in waters managed by regional fisheries organisations, can lead to adverse situations. the community does not make full use of the quotas it acquires for a fee in certain third countries' waters, although some member states are interested in using them. thus, while agreements with so-called 'southern countries' provide that, after the member states have used up their fishing opportunities, the unused part is made available to all the member states concerned, agreements with so-called 'northern countries' do not apply this mechanism. the european union does not fully use the fishing quotas it obtains on the high seas under the regional fisheries organisations, although there are member states interested in catching the quantities not fished by the 'contractors', so to speak. the commission has therefore decided to initiate proceedings under article 93 (2) of the eec treaty in respect of the following cases: the third point is the role of the european union on the international scene. the union as a whole is a world fishing power and one of the largest markets for fishery products, and it is important that it should claim and assume a role commensurate with its importance in the framework of international organisations and specifically regional fisheries organisations. i am very grateful to the commissioner for his intervention and i ask him to use the strength of the 15 member states in the negotiations with morocco and not to be just the lawyer of portugal and spain.
- mr president, the socioeconomic importance of the fisheries sector is well known, not only in terms of the direct and indirect jobs it creates but also in terms of the economic development of many fishing regions and communities, including small-scale coastal fishing which ensures the supply of fresh fish and the preservation of the traditions and cultures of the european union. however, as was confirmed at the regional seminars, there is a deep-seated criticism of the common fisheries policy, both in the tac and quota system and in the system of tacs, which have been discriminatory and have not brought much benefit to the sector, requiring that member states which have met the objectives should not be penalized with further reductions in fishing effort while the other countries do not meet the objectives set in the previous tacs. it was also clear that most fishermen have doubts about scientific advice on the management and conservation of fishery products, although they do not question the need to conserve species. a thorough review of the common fisheries policy is therefore needed, involving fishermen and other stakeholders in the decision-making process so that their experience and capacity can contribute to the establishment of a more functional and realistic system. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fisheries sector is not left behind. it is therefore necessary to take appropriate structural measures to ensure the survival of the communities dependent on fishing and to take socioeconomic measures to improve the living conditions of fishermen, who must not be harmed by any measures to protect the species, as has happened in northern portugal with the sardine fishermen. the specific nature of the outermost regions should also be taken into account. and it is particularly important, as stated in the gallagher report, that we are moving towards extending the exclusive zone to 24 miles, which has already been decided on in this parliament. as regards the negotiations currently under way within the world trade organisation, it should be stressed, as is done in the varela suanzes-carpegna report, that the community's fisheries activity cannot be analysed from a purely economic point of view of the exchange of goods. fishing in the european union has a multifunctional character, contributes to the consolidation of the social and economic fabric of coastal areas, constitutes a lifestyle and a factor of cohesion in vast coastal areas and a guarantee of food supply. i would also like to make a - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, mr javier solana, it has rarely been so important that the truth of the facts, the whole truth, should come out in a parliamentary debate such as this one. the - mr president, the european parliament has always spoken of the need to increase the european union's non-military intervention capacity. we therefore welcome the council's proposal and support the report by our colleague, mr newton dunn, which introduces proposals to improve the text. these are obviously matters falling within the first pillar. however, it is known that under the second pillar a military rapid reaction device is being built which is intended to perform petersberg-type tasks. the commission will also be able to provide information on the progress made in the implementation of the programme. but how? in what cases? what are the terms of this relationship? what's the coordination? i therefore believe that it would have been useful if the document had also dealt with this aspect. and as a member of this parliament, i would like to see some of the barriers that separate areas of competence from each other removed so that there can be greater cooperation within the european union.
in writing. - (pt) the situation and prospects of young farmers depend to a large extent on the situation and prospects of eu agriculture. but as everyone knows, agriculture and the countryside are in decline, largely because of the common agricultural policy, which favours the concentration of land, the intensification of production, the gradual vertical integration of agricultural production and which has not been without effects on desertification and rural exodus, as evidenced by the decline in employment and farms, by almost 22% and 20% respectively, since 1990. young farmers are the renewal of farms, they are essential to ensure the future of agriculture in the different regions of the eu. the 28% decline in the number of farms owned by farmers under 35 years of age over the last decade is evidence of the negative outlook. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the cap is not simply a system of farmers' incomes, but also a system of farmers' incomes. the parish report, with which i agree in general, despite its fragmentation, does not, however, stress the need for a change in policy. it is part of the existing framework. nevertheless, three amendments which i felt were important and which i tabled were accepted, namely: - the need to focus on sustainable and high-quality agricultural production, not only by strengthening existing mechanisms such as designations of origin, support for organic farming and support for indigenous breeds, but also by creating concrete support mechanisms for regional products of particular quality, in order to contribute to ensuring safe agricultural products with high added value; - the need to increase the amounts allocated to rural development and the structural funds to support and create extensive public service networks, create infrastructure and boost local markets in the rural world; (ii) the need to simplify and consolidate the legislation concerning young farmers.
in writing. - (pt) we voted against the joint resolution because we cannot accept that there is still a desire to deny the obvious - the serious health consequences for the populations of the areas where weapons containing depleted uranium have been used. since the beginning of the nuclear age at the end of the second world war, the dangers of uranium and other radioactive substances - highly toxic (from the chemical and radiological point of view) when inhaled or ingested, as well as genetically dangerous - and the precautions to be taken in this regard have been well known. the use of such weapons by the us and other nato countries during the gulf war, in bosnia and kosovo, has already caused the death of many soldiers, the suffering and death of the inhabitants of the bombed areas and the pollution of the environment in the affected areas, with medium and long-term consequences not only for current generations but also for future generations, which is prohibited by the geneva convention and which clearly shows that there are no "clean wars". in view of the scale of the crime committed against entire civilian populations, there is an urgent need to investigate, assess and convict those most responsible for this criminal act against humanity and to immediately prohibit the manufacture, testing, use and sale of such depleted uranium weapons; we must not just approve a moratorium.

- the next item is the report (a5-0373/2000) by mrs karamanou, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities, on the report from the commission to the council, the european parliament and the economic and social committee on the implementation of council recommendation 96/694 of 2 december 1996 on the balanced participation of women and men in decisionmaking (com (2000) 120 - c5-0210/2000 - 2000/2117 (cos)).
- mr president, the participation of women in decision-making is not only a necessary condition for the rights and interests of women to be respected, but also an essential basis for the achievement of justice and the development of democracy. the reality is that, despite the various recommendations and resolutions, this is an area where major imbalances persist with a huge underrepresentation of women in decision-making bodies, both in the spheres of economic and social activity and in political life, which contributes to maintaining existing inequalities and discrimination. the rapporteur's work is welcome, but she stresses that courageous steps must be taken to promote women's participation in decision-making - which also implies that men and women should assume an equal share of responsibilities in the public and private spheres - and new anti-discrimination practices must be encouraged, including in political parties and in public and private administrations, with the aim of eliminating the psychological and practical obstacles to women's participation in decision-making structures and in political life, putting an end to setbacks, such as the recent case of portugal, where the government abolished the ministry for equality. we must change this situation!
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating our colleague, mrs anna karamanou, on her excellent report. i'm not doing this for a mere formality. i do so because it is fair and for substantive reasons. this report is not only excellent. the explanatory statement is a study of the greatest interest to those concerned with equal rights for women and men and will certainly constitute a reference text for the future. and this clearly demonstrates the peregrine nature of the decision of the bureau of our parliament to reduce the statement of reasons to one page. this house doesn't produce paper by the meter. we are dealing with politics here and the policy must be justified. your report, mrs anna karamanou, is a good example of the importance of the political texts drawn up in this parliament. i shall concentrate my intervention on the electoral system. this is a crucial problem because it is a prerequisite for political participation itself. women must be given the opportunity to be elected so that they can prove what they are capable of. there are electoral systems which make it even more difficult for women to participate. these are, as it is proven, the systems that use the uninominal circles. this system has been penalizing women. it is not so much the electorate that does this, but the structures of the parties themselves that create enormous difficulties by means that are often not legitimate. so when there is a movement to try to change this electoral system by uninominal constituencies, where processes are created as in france to correct and to achieve a 50% quota for any of the sexes, in portugal the government announced the presentation of a new electoral legislation to partially replace the proportional system by the uninominal one. it is a very worrying initiative and it must be borne in mind that in this case women who want to take an active part in politics are being objectively harmed.
in writing. - (pt) we agree with some of the points made in this report, in particular the need to develop transport infrastructure in the sense of environmental sustainability, the preservation and strengthening of the right to mobility of all citizens and the accessibility of transport services of public interest, although we felt that these points deserved greater attention throughout the resolution. we cannot, however, but oppose the establishment of community criteria which treat all transport users equally and do not take account of the different levels of infrastructure existing in the member states. for these reasons we shall not be able to vote in favour of this report.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the figures and images coming from el salvador are frightening, both in terms of the hundreds of dead and the thousands of wounded, as well as the hundreds of missing persons and the approximately 26 000 displaced persons. the aftershocks are not stopping, either in el salvador or even in nicaragua. this situation in central america is not new. as mrs maes and mr poméz ruiz have already said, the consequences of hurricane mitch in november 1998 were dramatic, both in terms of people and material resources, in that part of the world. central america is one of the poorest regions in the world. some 40% of the population live below the poverty line and europe therefore has a particular responsibility towards this part of the world, not only because it is a region which has experienced very serious conflicts and which still has tensions, particularly at the border, but also because it is necessary to help consolidate it from a democratic point of view. fortunately, today all the organs are democratically elected, but there are still very undemocratic habits in these central american countries. in addition, the external debt is large. and only two countries are included in the initiative that has been launched. the idea we have when we speak to the main authorities in the central american countries - and we had the opportunity to receive the president of the republic of honduras on tuesday - is that of a people who are suffering and who, when they see a little light at the end of the tunnel to rise up, to face their problems and their history, are faced with dramatic situations caused by these natural disasters. europe has acted quickly in terms of emergency aid, both in november 1998 and now. but the problem is - and my two colleagues have rightly said so - that of more structural aid. it is essential that the commission implements the reconstruction programme for central america. what we are proposing, mr poul nielson, is that there should be a rapid redefinition of this programme, with more human and financial resources to meet this objective of helping el salvador as well. and on 26 march this year, in guatemala, a new meeting of the san josé group with the european union will take place. it would be a good way to conclude this meeting of the san josé group to proceed with the implementation of this programme for the reconstruction of central america with this new form of aid to the victims of el salvador.

- madam president, i would like to join my voice with those who have raised the problem of the tragedy that took place in portugal last weekend. to get a sense of what happened, just keep in mind the following: last year's winter was the driest of the century, and the last december and january were the wettest in living memory. so all the interventions made in the territory to control the waters have been ineffective. on the other hand, it all happened so fast that unthinkable deaths and flooding resulted. it is therefore a matter of making a much greater effort than one would think, because the situation was truly exceptional.
- madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in recent days several areas of portugal, especially in the north and centre of the country, have been affected by severe weather conditions with torrential rains which have caused loss of human life, demolition of houses, evictions of families, damage to houses, enormous losses in agriculture, fisheries, industry, trade, destruction of communication routes, closure of bars and ports. of course, not all the damage has been fully assessed yet, but it is estimated to be in the hundreds of millions of dollars, which means that extraordinary community aid is needed to deal with the economic and social problems arising from this destruction. and so, in addition to expressing our sincere condolences to the families of the victims and our solidarity with the people who have been displaced and have lost their possessions (and i am sure that all members and this parliament will agree with this), we ask, madam president, that you call on the commission to ensure that, in collaboration with the portuguese government and the local authorities, emergency aid is provided to support the most affected populations in the recovery of economic activities. i would also ask, madam president, that the next conference of presidents of the parliamentary groups should include this issue in the list of urgent matters for the next plenary session.

the council. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. after this very interesting debate on the important and difficult question of the situation in the middle east, i would like to make a very brief reflection. the theme running through most of the speeches by the honourable members is that the european union must play a greater role at the political level. the major role we already play in economic cooperation with this conflict-affected region must be complemented by a greater political role. i would like to say with all humility that the union is playing a greater political role than it has ever done before. why do i make this claim? yes, in part as a response to the honourable member morillon. i think there is a greater desire today than in the past for what mr morillon calls a harmonization of policy towards the middle east. there may still be degree differences yes. however, there is a very strong common will, which the presidency sees in its contacts with all the other member states, to work together towards a peaceful solution. i would like to say that there is no issue which has occupied the presidency of the council, the swedish prime minister, more than the situation in the middle east during the first 30 days of the swedish presidency of the council of the european union. nor is there any issue that has occupied the president of the general affairs council, the swedish foreign minister, more than the situation in the middle east. i think the same applies to the high representative, who can certainly give an answer himself. the union is therefore also very closely involved in the political process, but we are still waiting for the concrete results. i share the cautious optimism of the high representative, mr solana, but we must of course remember that we are dealing with a fundamentally very difficult political conflict in the middle east. if we are to achieve any success, it is indeed necessary for the union to coordinate closely within itself, to work closely with the united nations and its secretary-general and to work closely with the united states, which has shown unflagging strength in its attempts to resolve this conflict. if that happens, then i believe that our optimism can also be crowned with concrete results.
high representative for the common foreign and security policy. i would like to clarify a few points, without entering into a great historical debate, because i do not think that this is the time to do so or to throw ourselves at each other's suffering. ladies and gentlemen, some of you have visited the region recently. i have to tell you that i have been visiting her since 1991, practically non-stop. we have seen so much suffering. what we want, those of us who have seen so much suffering, is to try to find a peaceful and lasting situation. that is the commitment that we wish to reach as those of us involved in trying to find a solution at the moment. i want to say that nothing at this moment suggests an appeal to wild optimism. it would be foolish to do so, but i do believe - and i do believe this sincerely - that we are at a point where the connotations of the problem have been better defined and we can therefore get closer to a solution. why do i say that? we all know that to find a solution to this very serious problem, we must define the territory and be willing to talk about it, we must define the security conditions and be willing to talk about it, we must define the future of jerusalem and be willing to negotiate about it, and we must negotiate about the refugees and be willing to do so. at this point, for the first time, the four or five major parameters have been defined, both sides have accepted them as the major parameters to be defined and accepted, and have begun to negotiate seriously, deeply, about them. this is a historic step. the sad thing is that i appeal to despair rather than hope, because time has unfortunately been mismanaged. if we had a little longer period of political calm, i sincerely believe that at that time one could be optimistic. but now i'm not so much because the timing, unfortunately, is conditioned by the electoral and political calendar, and there's little we can do about it. but anyone who wants to negotiate, in this case with the european union, has to put on the table the challenge of reaching agreements on the basis that two states in the area must be accepted, capable of defining the four parameters to which i have referred: territories, security, capital and refugees. the european union has proposed possible solutions to the four issues mentioned. do not think, ladies and gentlemen, that the people involved in the various presidencies, in this case the swedish presidency, have not put suggestions and ideas on the table for moving forward. the thing is, it's not always the miracle of coming up with exactly the right solution at a given time. but on jerusalem, mr morillon, i can tell you that you can be sure that many suggestions have been put on the table. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. i will also tell you that on settlements the european union has had a brilliant and generous position. a position which indicates that a settlement policy such as the current one cannot be accepted. we have said it, we will continue to say it, and i can assure you that, from my mouth, the same sentence will always be passed. there will be no final solution without a solution also to the settlement issue. we know this and so does anyone who has been involved in this terrible conflict. my concern - which i have - and the cause of my deep sadness - which i also have - is, as some of your honourable members have said, that the oslo process is beginning to lose the political support it had from both sides a few years ago. it is true that the oslo process - not peace - is not accepted by all parties today, with the same intensity and in the same way as it was a few years ago. for some it has not offered the security they wanted, and for others it has not brought the change they hoped for and desired in their daily lives. hence the frustration of some and the lack of security of others. and that is where a big part of the great difficulty of the current debate comes from. i want to tell you that the european union will do everything possible, with your support, with the support of governments and parliaments, to continue to move forward, but it is true that what we can do together to promote the process is not inexhaustible. the parties directly involved are the ones who have to reach an agreement, and the rest of us can only try to establish the economic, social and political conditions so that such an agreement can be reached. my own experience over the last few months is that the parties have never spoken more openly and generously, and have never come closer to resolving the problems. and that was my hope. unfortunately, perhaps you are right and this hope has been in vain. i would be so sorry.
commission. – mr president, i hope this will not seem like an anti-climax after the excellent speech that we have just heard in response to the debate, but there is one point which has come up in a previous debate and in two speeches this evening that i would like to deal with once and for all. at least one of the honourable members who made the point today is in the chamber and perhaps i can deal with it now, for the record, and we can regard it as having been sorted out. it is the question of textbooks, which i know has been of concern to a number of honourable members of the house. i want to make it clear that the commission rejects any attempt to use the educational system to promote intolerance or hatred. it is worth noting that the main focus of the commission’s assistance has always been to promote a culture of peace, tolerance and human rights in the middle east. for example, in recent years we have financed several projects, worth about eur 4.5 million, to foster dialogue between israeli and palestinian youth. i want to make this point absolutely clear. the commission has never allocated funds for the development of a new school curriculum nor for the printing and distribution of school textbooks. the development of school curricula and the preparation of textbooks are the responsibility of the palestinian centre for curriculum development. it was established in 1995 with the assistance of member states through unesco. since then the centre has undertaken the charge of harmonising the hitherto separate educational systems – the egyptian for the gaza strip and the jordanian for the west bank – and of organising a new curriculum that meets the needs of the new situation. only last year did the palestinian authority issue their own textbooks for grades 1 to 6. no sign of anti-semitic content has been found in these new books. books used prior to that were, i am advised, the official textbooks in jordan and egypt and were authorised by israel for the west bank and gaza until 1994 at least – after they had been subject to some editorial control. i hope that has made clear the commission’s position on this issue. the commission has had nothing to do with textbooks and we deplore any promotion of intolerance particularly among young people and particularly through the education system. it was worth making the point at this stage, because it has been raised before.
- thank you, commissioner christopher patten. - thank you very much. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 11 a.m.
- the following oral questions are now scheduled for joint debate: - doc. b5-0002/2001 by mr miranda, on behalf of the committee on development and cooperation, to the council, on the plan for peace, prosperity and state building, known as the 'colombia plan'; - doc. b5-0003/2001 by mr miranda, on behalf of the committee on development and cooperation, to the commission, on the plan for peace, prosperity and state building, known as the 'colombia plan'.
mr president, i would first of all like to thank the council and the commission for their willingness to discuss today a problem of particular importance, timeliness and gravity, such as the situation in colombia, which the committee on development and cooperation is following and will continue to follow with the greatest attention and concern. it is clear that the european union cannot remain indifferent to the situation in that country and cannot - or should not - take a wrong position on it. the situation is well known. there is no shortage of documents from a wide variety of sources which show the tragedy in colombia and the real risks of a worsening of the conflict, with serious dangers of even regionalizing it. i would remind you of the concerns expressed at the meeting of representatives of the highest level of several latin american countries in rio de janeiro on this issue in the middle of last year. and the key element in the current situation is undoubtedly the 'colombia plan' agreed in september 1999 between presidents clinton and pastrana, and it is essential that the council and the commission take a stand and distance themselves from it even more clearly. firstly, because of its eminently military character, but also because it is not the result of any concertation process, but rather arouses widespread opposition inside and outside the country, because it puts the serious phenomenon of paramilitarism on the back burner and, consequently, because it calls into question the negotiating process itself. and because this plan, which focuses on eliminating the cultivation and trafficking of drugs (through the attack on producers, especially by the use of chemical and biological fumigation), not only threatens colombia's rich biodiversity but will tend to accentuate the plight of the displaced, who may even become refugees in neighbouring countries. we are aware of the statement by the general affairs council of 9 october last year. we obviously and essentially follow the aims set out in it and even take a distant view of them in relation to the 'colombia plan'. we believe that this is the position of the vast majority of the governments of the member states. but it is equally true that some people want to interpret this council statement as complementary to the 'colombia plan'. and so we think it is essential to clarify the community's position, to prevent it from being interpreted in a way that we would like to believe is wrong, even if this is contrary to the opinion of one or another government in the member states, particularly the one which has publicly committed itself politically and financially to the plan. we also believe that the european union - and the european parliament itself - should not support an initiative which is clearly the result of a bilateral agreement between the colombian government and the us administration. moreover, we do not think it should be the european union's task to heal the wounds caused by the actions of others. in essence, we believe, and we express this in the motion for a resolution adopted by the committee on development and cooperation, as we have already expressed it in a previous resolution of this parliament, that the european union's intervention must be accompanied by its own strategy, not a militaristic one. it is in this spirit that we hope the european governments will take their stand with the colombian government on 30 april in brussels. we also believe that this european union strategy must start from the understanding that the armed conflict affecting colombia is far from being confined to the drug problem. it is, in fact, much earlier than that and is rooted fundamentally in the deeply negative political, economic and social phenomena that are occurring in the country. that is why we consider it essential and a priority to reform the way in which wealth is distributed, and in particular the way in which land is distributed, by implementing a process of land reform. we also believe that the problem of drug cultivation and trafficking requires a global approach based on shared responsibility and international cooperation between the producing and consuming countries, starting with the fight against money laundering. we believe that the european union's strategy must also bear in mind the need to put an end to the phenomena and the impunity with which paramilitary groups, often in conjunction with the colombian military, are acting and are mainly responsible for the mass killings of the population and, in particular, of european aid workers. how can it not hide the trauma that remains in colombia as a result of the persecution and massacre of countless leaders and militants of the patriotic union, which took place precisely as a result of their participation in an electoral process. in short, the european union must make every effort to promote a genuine peace process, which must not be confused with - and even opposed to - the 'colombia plan'. it is necessary to continue and strengthen cooperation with colombia, and this should mean, as a matter of priority, an effective commitment by the colombian government to implementing structural reforms and involving the people and their organisations in solving the enormous problems affecting them and the country. i hope that is also the view of the commission and the council.
- mrs ghilardotti, the bureau takes note of your suggestion that the president of the european parliament should send a letter and will inform the president of your request.
commission. – i only need to respond to a few of the speeches. to mr howitt i would say, referring to a press statement half a year ago, that the position of the commission is the one i have stated tonight. we have our own plan. we have our own philosophy and we will cooperate with all parts of society in colombia to strengthen the role of peace. we do not need to relate to plan colombia. what we are doing forms no part of it. to mrs sauquillo i would clarify what the planning is on an indicative basis and how we hope be able to spend the eur 105 million from the commission: eur 40 million to projects in the sphere of social and economic development and combating poverty. this includes a series of decentralised initiatives in the areas of health, education, development of agricultural and traditional production, and coastal fishing. eur 30 million in support of alternative development, eur 25 million in support of administrative and judicial reform and eur 10 million in actions to support the promotion and defence of human rights. over and above this we are envisaging a level of around eur 10 million per year, part of which we expect to be echo activities. i mentioned the displaced persons whose numbers keep growing, so we have a clear commitment to continue echo’s presence there. we are expecting to stay at the level we have been at in recent years as far as ngo cofinancing is concerned. this has been at a level of eur 2.4 million; it could be a little more or a little less. we have activities amounting to eur 1.3 million over recent years for programmes to protect the environment and tropical forests. many of these activities will be carried out in cooperation with or through different ngos. it is essential for us to do this if possible with colombian ngos and i will be asking european ngos when they engage in these tasks to find partners in colombia as in other cases and create partnerships. this will in fact strengthen the message that we are trying to get across. some other activities aim to stimulate the government to do what we think is right: to concentrate more on poverty, to take more seriously the social aspects of these conflicts. here the government of colombia is the partner we need in our attempts to shape a peaceful development there. it is not just an issue of whether or not we are using ngos. this will depend very much on the subject and activity from case to case. but the transformation and the strengthening of a peaceful civil society, which is part of the solution and an element in the solution, can only be stimulated by a stronger involvement of ngos.

- mr. howitt, i'm not going to reopen this debate. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 11 a.m. (the sitting was suspended at 8.45 p.m. and resumed at 9.00 p.m.)
the aim of the european council meeting in brussels on 21 and 22 june is to establish a european area of freedom, security and justice, in which the security of european citizens and the fight against organised crime must be strengthened. following the tampere agreement, there is an urgent need to approximate national legislation on criminal offences and penalties in the field of organised crime, i.e. it is imperative that we concentrate our efforts on adopting common definitions, charges and penalties in the area of approximation of legislation in criminal matters. this mechanism of mutual evaluation, to which mr ferri has just referred and which is the essence of the excellent report he has presented to the committee on civil liberties, was set up on the basis of the action plan against organised crime adopted in amsterdam in june 1997. this is already considerable progress, reinforced by the creation of the european judicial network and other initiatives to modernise and strengthen the effectiveness of the system for preventing and combating crime. in the context of this evaluation system, although it is foreseen that the council will adopt the necessary measures after a full cycle of evaluations - that is, three years, five countries per year - the decisions taken by some of the member states to anticipate the reforms and improvements considered most urgent in their own legal order taking into account the results of these evaluations are to be welcomed. and we all look forward to receiving the results of the evaluations as soon as possible for the other countries, including my own country, portugal. i welcome the fact that the subject chosen for the first round of evaluations was that of delays in the operation of the mutual legal assistance system and of urgent requests for the seizure of property, with particular attention to cases relating to organised crime. the problem of delays is particularly welcome. we are concerned about the increasingly widespread delays in judicial proceedings and we know how harmful this is. justice that is delayed or delayed always ends up being the denial of justice itself.
- mr president, the current situation of the multiplication of bse cases in the european union makes it clear that it is not enough to make declarations of intent with regard to cap reform, but that it is necessary to get to the bottom of the problem and move quickly towards a real reform of the current common agricultural policy. the budgetary and community constraints cannot be an excuse for not taking the measures necessary to solve the problem, while at the same time supporting farmers who raise cattle. the reform of the cap which is urgently needed must not be aimed at budgetary savings but at rebalancing the distribution of aid between producing countries and production, taking into account the specific regional characteristics and the reality of each member state. new needs must be matched by new means, and it is therefore necessary to review the current financial framework agreed in berlin. finally, a reference to portugal, to ask the commissioner about the injustice of maintaining this single export embargo in a context where there is a significant drop in cases, while in other countries they are constantly increasing. it's a pointless position.
- mr president, i would first like to congratulate mrs barbara o'toole on her timely report. and i would like to say this about this one: in these times it's not just the concept of the book that's changing. the concept of literacy is also there. the ignorance or inability to master the new technologies in the area of the written word and knowledge will inevitably lead to the emergence of new forms of illiteracy with all the corresponding human, social, economic and cultural costs. (noise in the chamber) the euphoria which has been dominant at international meetings, with its peak at the lisbon council, over the role of new technologies in the development of the union and the fight against unemployment in fast-moving terms must therefore be tempered, at least methodologically, and it is important that the european institutions give serious thought to matters such as those dealt with in this report. but it is not just a question of reflecting on the issues of the book, electronic publishing, printing on demand and related problems. we believe it is essential that the work of the various parliamentary committees, the various directorates-general and the various european and national officials with a say in these matters should find ways of useful and effective coordination. it will not make sense to adopt divergent or even contradictory measures on e-publishing and book, copyright or e-learning policies. the definition and coordination of the guidelines for such policies increasingly require at least two assumptions to be verified: the growing support of qualified experts, enabling politicians in a field which they do not usually master well, and the choice of legal provisions which can be adapted rapidly to technological and market developments. we believe that electronic publishing should not be treated in the same way as traditional books from the point of view of taxation and vat. it lacks a specific regime and it must be borne in mind that the traditional book incorporates a different type of value and is a different product by nature from the simple digitized file that is the electronic book. the proposal is to designate the european year of the book and literacy and to proclaim 23 april each year as european reading day. these measures are part of the fight against illiteracy, of the policy of safeguarding the cultural and linguistic diversity of the peoples of europe, of stimulating the acquisition of knowledge and of a framework for deepening citizenship. for that, they deserve our applause.

- mr president, commissioner, the most traumatic industrial restructuring, because of its unpredictability, is that dictated by aggressive market behaviour and concentration. technological mismatch and market trends have been extensively reported but never measured in their social consequences. do we know how much they have contributed to the impoverishment of people and regions? how do those who do so recover from depression, and at what cost? how and to what extent do they contribute to longterm unemployment among people over 40? how and to what extent do they contribute to the fact that the actual retirement age in the european union is between 55 and 57? the lisbon resolutions sought to respond to these points by giving priority to employment, the viability of social protection, the fight against social exclusion and also by preventing changes in the economy. we do not know how much they contribute to the increase in the social security effort and we do not know how many people become more dependent on social protection systems. who pays for the social restructuring? the market or the taxpayer and the worker? the social partners say that prevention and fair and balanced change are impossible, and they have just signed a pre-agreement document to implement the european society directive on information and consultation. we have also heard, commissioner, that the market has no natural laws and that the political forces must intervene through legislation, institutional action and social dialogue. social insecurity and the increase in crime are taking root in post-industrial society and affect both traditional sectors and the so-called new economy. the portuguese, french and now swedish presidencies have raised expectations by pushing forward with hitherto neglected dossiers. but the commission's programme for 2001 does not envisage such a move.
mr president, 'the harvest is still ripe when the baskets are washed' is a saying in my country, which means that there is still hope for authors and artists that a shock of conscience will pass has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are not naive, mr president. the creation and dissemination of information are economic values, they have always been, and hence the basis of intellectual property and broadcasting rights. unfortunately, the commission proposal and the council's common position are laden with a criticable degree of frivolity. in the name of freedom of consumption, art consumption is opposed to artistic creation, just as it is acceptable to assume that creative freedom is an obstacle to the knowledge and cultural enrichment of citizens. the new communication technologies are not the enemy of intellectuals and authors, but it cannot be assumed that in the cyber world in which we live, protecting the weakest party, that is, protecting the author, means defending the monopoly. we must not sacrifice the rights of authors in the name of consumer rights, and above all, the original sin - mr president, as an author and member of the portuguese society of authors, i have a personal interest in a subject in which the cultural aspect of european construction itself is also at stake. it is absurd for europe to proclaim the essential value of its cultural and linguistic pluralism and at the same time to make it meaningless. i do not know a single author in europe who agrees with the compromise amendments on private copying. this means something. and i would also say that it would be much better if the directive gave satisfaction to the authors now and then came back in 18 months to be reviewed. most of the exceptions to copyright are a cancer-like process, giving rise to the uncontrolled and rampant proliferation of abusive uses. the more exceptions are provided for, the more the right to regulate is weakened, the more optional exceptions are provided for, the more the standardization and coordination of national organizations in this field is undermined. limiting the scope of exceptions is of the utmost importance. it is not enough to rule out the possibility of using the copies for commercial purposes, directly or indirectly. they are often used for atypical purposes, barter, commodity, supply, etc. these are all causes of the uncontrolled proliferation of other copies, with very serious damage to authors and publishers. if the concept of private use does not force the domestic sphere to conform or if the authorization of copying is given only by the author, the directive will continue to encourage more rampant piracy rather than solving the main problems. i would like to end by quoting a passage from a message i received from several portuguese authors, including the nobel prize winner josé saramago: "as artists, we are particularly concerned that unauthorised mass copying could seriously undermine the livelihoods of all those in the creative community. those who have nothing to do with the creative process would consume the fruits of others' labor without productive reinvestment, unless private copying effectively maintains its status as private copying".

- mr president, the last few years have presented us with a completely new picture: the conversion of pharmaceutical companies into biotechnology companies, the explosion in the number of companies developing genetically modified organisms, the allocation of ever more public and private funds to support research in the field of genetics, the legal race for patents on gmo seeds and even patents for the manipulation of the human genome, the merger and splitting at a truly astonishing speed of biotechnology companies, and even the emergence of new political frontiers and a new discourse on ethics, agriculture, public health, the environment, hunger and poverty. in short, biotechnology is bringing about a real revolution in science, economics and politics. a revolution of hope, but also of risk. so fundamentalisms of the 'just because' or 'no because' kind are going nowhere. the answer lies in the right balance between freedom of inquiry and the imperative of protecting public health and the environment. i believe that this work on the revision of the directive on the deliberate release of gmos into the environment respects this balance, because on the one hand it does not restrict research and experimentation and on the other hand it defines clearer and more restrictive rules on the release of gmos into the environment, protecting the consumer and biodiversity. however, the revision of this directive will only be complete when the commission has put forward legislative initiatives which will give concrete expression to the principles of environmental responsibility and the labelling and traceability of products containing gmos.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, our position on turkey's membership of the european union is well known. we made this clear at the november plenary session of this parliament when we discussed the report on the progress made by this country in preparing for accession. we have nothing against the great turkish nation or its close relationship with the european union, quite the contrary. we simply note that the traditional reservations about turkey's candidacy have changed without a substantive political debate to justify it. have the reasons for this reservation disappeared or been diluted? of course, there is the geographical problem: i would like to ask the commissioner whether he has not already taken the initiative to make a statement on the situation in turkey. what different doctrine on european space can be used to frame this new concept of external border resulting from the inclusion of turkey in the union? will europe have to have a new frontier, not just a non-european one but one of war and conflict? ladies and gentlemen, just think of the possibility that we might have neighbouring countries such as iran, iraq or syria... what about the conflict with the democratic model common to europeans caused by the numerous restrictions on the exercise of fundamental rights which, unfortunately, continue to be observed in turkey? and how can we reconcile this country's accession with the military dispute it has with greece over cyprus? what about the kurdish question, which remains unchanged? what about the regime's military tutelage, which persists? finally, does no one question the impact of turkey's demographic size on the economic and social cohesion of an enlarged europe? have all these issues changed or attenuated significantly? we don't think he did. we even think we're not alone in this opinion. the most optimistic of us will say that we have not overlooked the positive developments that have taken place. is that so? let us see what the latest commission report on the turkish situation tells us. it is true that it recognises considerable progress in stabilizing the macroeconomic situation and in reducing the most glaring imbalances in its economy. that's probably what counts, we're not naive. turkey's economic importance to the european union is considerable today. in 2000, for example, the european union accounted for 52.9% of turkey's imports and 53.1% of its exports. how can you ignore that fact? but this does not prevent us from strongly recommending the reading of this report on the respect for freedom of expression and association, which the commission says remains limited, the situation with regard to economic, social and cultural rights which the document says has not changed compared to 1999, the judicial system whose functioning has not yet been modified, corruption which remains at worrying levels, the military protection of the regime, torture and ill-treatment which are far from being eradicated in all respects and which lead the commission to conclude that, quote, 'compared to the previous year' - 1999 - 'the situation on the ground has not improved and turkey does not regularly meet the copenhagen political criteria. " mr president, the proposal for a council regulation which will be voted on tomorrow creates the legal framework which will enable turkey to benefit from a pre-accession strategy, like the other applicant countries. it provides for a kind of suspensive clause which will be triggered when, in particular, it is found that the copenhagen criteria are not sufficiently met. parliament wants to be involved in this monitoring task. - very good. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. or we will have to conclude that what the commission is proposing to turkey is to give the accession partnership with one hand and suspend it indefinitely with the other ...
mr president, the proposal for a council regulation on assistance to turkey in the context of the pre-accession strategy and the report by mr swoboda are another significant moment in the direction of a great historic objective. the degree of economic interpenetration between turkey and the european union is already high, with more than half of turkey's imports coming from the union and more than half of its exports going to the european union. turkey is also a member of nato, within which there is a tradition of mutual understanding with the european countries that are members of the same alliance. in addition to other aspects, particularly cultural aspects, there are thus grounds for making progress towards turkey's future integration into the european union. the proposal for a regulation will now provide the legal basis for the implementation of an accession partnership, so as to facilitate turkey's compliance with the copenhagen criteria. not only the deepening of the customs union but also the promotion of economic and social development are essential aspects of an effective partnership. we therefore support the rapporteur's proposals, which are in keeping with the spirit of the commission's initiative and in particular the inclusion of turkey in the support provided by the pre-accession structural instrument and the special programme for agriculture and rural development. however, in the development strategy we advocate, the path to economic and social development must be compatible with political development, that is, the exercise of democracy and human rights. it is in this context that we underline and support the content of article 4 of the proposed regulation. we also believe that a more effective role for the european parliament in this process can be both a contribution and a guarantee.
