the report by mr macsharry, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, security and defence policy, on the situation in macao, was discussed twice in the committee on foreign affairs and was then unanimously adopted by the committee on foreign affairs. in fact, i had earlier incorporated some of the conclusions of the opinion drawn up by the committee on industry. since december 1999, that is, just over a year ago, macau has ceased to be a special territory administered by portugal, peacefully and by consensus for 450 years, and is now a special administrative region integrated into the people's republic of china in accordance with the chinese rule of 'one country, two systems'. i take this opportunity to point out that there is a serious error in the report in the explanatory statement in paragraph 9, when it says, repeating this rule, 'a two-country system'. now, what the chinese mean is exactly the opposite, "one country, two systems". it is a mistake that still needs to be corrected. as a portuguese citizen and having had direct constitutional responsibility for macau, i can testify here that the transfer of macau from portuguese to chinese administration was done by consensus and with great cordiality in accordance with the 1987 joint portuguese-chinese declaration, which is still in force today and will remain in force for another 50 years. the past year was equally peaceful and consensual, according to the testimonies i have from various sources and in particular from the portuguese and the maasai who continue to live and work in that territory. i have already informed the committee on foreign affairs of this. the policy of the people's republic of china towards hong kong and now towards macau is, as is well known, aimed at the rapprochement and future integration of taiwan into china. and although there are and have been small and inevitable integration problems with regard to hong kong, a territory much larger, much more populous and much richer than macau, with a historical past, however much more recent, it is generally known that the transition of the two territories - hong kong and macau - has gone on well overall. as i pointed out, macau was never a colony, it was a territory under portuguese administration by agreement between portugal and china. after the carnation revolution, when i first came to the un in 1974 as foreign minister, the chinese foreign minister said, "be careful, don't decolonize macau, because macau is not a colony, and never has been". mao tse tung, when he took power in 1949, said: "portugal remained in macau for 400 years without ever making war on china". and that went to macau, and it's, an important point. although less wealthy than hong kong, macau has developed in an extraordinary way in many areas over the last decade. it used to be just the gambling empire; today, it is a region where there is an intensive production of tourist nature and also of toys, of various objects and many other industries, among them textiles. relations between hong kong and macao have always been excellent. but it is good to know, and i remind this parliament, that each territory has its own peculiarities. they are different and should therefore be seen by the european union as separate, albeit correlated, issues; shall not be treated in common, in particular with regard to the representation of the european union in that territory. portugal has excellent traditional relations with the people's republic of china, which continue to intensify at various levels - including several visits by chinese ministers to portugal and portuguese ministers to china this year - and these relations also continue to be very intense between portugal and macau. the current governor of macau, already appointed by the chinese government, mr edmondo, has this year officially visited portugal. it was, in fact, his first visit abroad. in accordance with the lugo-china joint declaration, the people's republic of china has undertaken to respect for the next 50 years the economic system and the political legal order established by portugal when it created the macao special administrative region. a founding member of the world trade organization, macau has signed the international covenant on civil and political rights and the international covenant on economic, social and cultural rights. it has a kind of delegation - i say 'kind', because this is not allowed by the european union - to the european union's economic and trade office, the macao economic and trade office, since the responsibility for external relations and defence lies with beijing. and in principle, the european union should appoint a permanent representative to macao. it's important that you do. i think you already have. portugal left macau with a whole western-style democratic legal system, with well-functioning political institutions, with a chief executive appointed by beijing, who replaced the former portuguese governor, a 23-member legislative assembly, independent courts and a court of last resort, as well as an efficient police force. the lugo-china joint declaration defines civil, political, economic, social and cultural rights, which have been respected. there's no death penalty in the territory. portugal has excellent relations with the people's republic of china, as i said, and it continues to have a very significant presence in macau at the level of investors, companies, civil servants and the professions. there is a portuguese school in macau, which is working well, where portuguese is still taught. for the european union, macau can and should be of great importance as a centre for business and investment, not only in the territory but also because of the transactions that are traditionally carried out there with china and the other countries of asia. as a special administrative region with its own cultural identity and a mixed population, although largely chinese, the european union's presence in macau can only be a two-way street. i therefore urge the european union to send a permanent representative to macau and to strengthen economic, political and cultural relations between the european union and macau. the european union must not lose interest in macao, because what happens in the territory with special jurisdiction over the next 50 years depends in part on the european union's surveillance of macao.
mr president, with his special knowledge of macao and the attention he has been paying to the development of the special administrative region created there, mr mário soares has given this house a document of great importance and timeliness. the analysis of the institutional and regulatory framework established for the region and the potential for modern democratic structuring that derives from it is the starting point, while bearing in mind both the cultural specificity and the particular historical, social and economic ties on which macau's relationship with portugal and europe, on the one hand, and with the people's republic of china, on the other, is based. the horizon is thus set for the expectation that the positive development of the special administrative region will be confirmed, although it is known that not as many concrete data as desirable can be taken into account as regards the way in which these first twelve months actually passed, a very short time, moreover. if, as is expected and already indicated in the soares report, these positive aspects are largely confirmed, we cannot fail to see in this the result not only of the careful preparation of the transfer of sovereignty by portugal and china, but also of the special commitment of the current macau authorities, and also, it is important to recall, of the extraordinarily relevant action of the last portuguese governor of macau, vasco rocha vieira, on whose merits parliament had the opportunity to hear the specially authorized testimony of commissioner christopher patten in plenary in december 1999. this action on the ground has had the merit of ensuring, among other things, the respect of fundamental rights in the long term, and it has been a form of calm and convinced struggle for them, without the drama that has characterized the struggle of those who have been awarded the sakharov prize, but with similar effectiveness and repercussion. the very positive aspects highlighted should not, however, lead the european union authorities to follow further developments less closely. some points, perhaps explained by the difference in attitudes, justify this. it is known that there has been a change in the commercial companies code which may possibly conflict with the rules on transparency in public tenders, and it is also known that the portuguese language is being used less, both in the official sphere and in the media and in schools. there also appear to be indications of changes in the electoral law, in certain aspects concerning the public administration and the classification of civil servants, the health system and higher education. it is hoped that these will be superficial aspects which will not result in a move away from the european union and the impoverishment of macao's autonomy in favour of practices in other countries in the area or a less acceptable dilution in china itself. however, the soares report reiterates the need for full respect for the statute and legal framework adopted for macao, both as regards fundamental rights and the other rights and principles of democratic legality which derive from the sino-portuguese joint declaration of 1987 and the basic law of 1993, the articulation of which is very usefully recapitulated. the need to encourage cooperation between europe and macao in all areas was also stressed, and the need for the rapid appointment of a european union representative to the special administrative region was made more than clear. for all these reasons, and while i would like to congratulate mr soares, i would like to end by expressing my agreement and my applause for his report.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i am speaking for two main reasons. the first is to congratulate the rapporteur, our colleague mário soares, on the excellent report which this house has produced and to highlight three points in that report. firstly, the need for europe to keep a watchful eye on the autonomy and specificity of the macao special administrative region, secondly, the need for the european union to have a physical presence with a representative and, thirdly, the need for reinforcement and dialogue with that territory, as our colleague mário soares has very well said. as regards the second reason, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, my speech is intended to mark a significant moment in the debate on this report, because the undersigned of this report, in addition to being a ranking member of this house, is a former president of the republic of one of the member states of the european union with a career which honours us all, not only his party colleagues, not only his fellow portuguese citizens, but all europeans. and this report, with its stamp and signature, gives credibility to the european parliament. it is a fact that not only does it make a contribution to this document, but also the active and persistent way in which it has always fought and continues to fight in this house for the defence of the values that are most dear to the european project itself.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, macau is a unique case, a culturally specific case and a landmark in the historical relationship between europe and china through portugal. preserving and enhancing these features in the new framework launched in december 1999 is a relevant objective for us. the european union is the second largest trading partner and the third largest investor in macau, with a trade and cooperation agreement dating back to 1993 as well as other specific agreements between the eu and macau in various other areas. finally, the portuguese administration has made a great effort, especially in the last two decades, to modernise the territory and its infrastructure; the commission has also been able to establish in macau an institutional legal framework of the greatest interest for a european perspective and for our particular view of asia, the pacific and our relations with the great nation of china. the first merit of this report and the motion for a resolution we are debating is that it has met the timetable, with rigor and timeliness, and has not let macao fall into oblivion. i do not thereby diminish the importance of the report; i would like to say that i am very pleased that the commission has taken the initiative to present this report, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his work. from the looks of the room, it seems that this is a portuguese debate, but what we want to reaffirm is that the 1999 transition also marks that macau has become a european issue of the first magnitude. the timeliness of this parliamentary work by our colleague mário soares exposes the commission's main weakness, namely that it has not done the same, which deserves clear criticism. firstly, it is wrong that a year has passed without the commission publishing the promised and due annual report on eu-china relations. and secondly, it is also wrong, as we know, that it has not yet appointed the person responsible for bilateral cooperation for macau, contrary to what was announced more than a year ago in the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament. the reasons why we should maintain and deepen bilateral cooperation with the new special administrative region are well known. there's no point in going on about it. we would like to congratulate dr. mario soares on his excellent work. we hope that the commission will now be able to make up for lost time, to intensify and better organise cooperation with macao, to respond more actively to the challenges we face, to re-read and to implement its own 1999 communication on time.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, our starting point is the need to combat illegal immigration and, in particular, the forms of exploitation which it entails. this situation is worsening not only because it has proved to be highly profitable and less risky than other forms of trafficking, such as drug trafficking, but also because this activity is favoured by the legislative and operational inadequacies of the member states and by everything that allows smugglers to enter and remain in these territories. the proposal we have before us, which aims to strengthen the fight against the entry and residence of illegal immigrants, must pay particular attention to two aspects: the question of the responsibility of employers and the consequences that may follow from the use of illegal labour - we must look at the situations within our own member states with regard to the illegal labour market - and the question of trafficking in human beings, which must be fiercely combated, with particular attention to the trafficking of children and women for the purpose of sexual exploitation, which represents one of the most odious aspects of this type of trafficking. dramatic situations such as that which took place in dover last june or those which have come to light following the successful police operation cathedral are situations which no democratic society can tolerate. we must fight against the aid to illegal immigration. it is essential that each member state takes the necessary measures to ensure that the intentional facilitation of the entry, movement or illegal stay of migrants is considered an offence subject to effective, proportionate and dissuasive sanctions. there should be a common policy in the member states on immigration and asylum appropriate to the demographic, economic and humanitarian requirements of the member states.
- mr president, commissioner, the so-called hard-shelled fruit, to which it would also be fair to add the carob, is one of the most typical products of southern europe. the only specific aid they received, and are receiving, is limited to the co-financing of quality and marketing improvement plans, which were due to expire at the end of last year and which the commission now proposes to extend for another year. the commission's proposal stems from the recognition, which it itself makes in the preamble to the proposal, that the other measures for which the sector is also eligible were not appropriate or immediately applicable to nuts, and it promised to submit new proposals in a report to be published by the end of 2000, which has not yet happened. it is not worth prolonging for another year the already meagre measures in place and saying nothing more about the future of this sector, especially since it is in crisis due to the very strong competition from third countries. what does make sense is that hard-shelled fruit is treated as a whole-body sector within the framework of the common organisation of the market in fruit and vegetables. it is in this sense that, when the european parliament adopted the proposal for a new common organization of the market in fruit and vegetables, it adopted an amendment calling on the commission to submit proposals guaranteeing the soft fruit sector permanent measures and support as they exist for the other agricultural sectors. that is what the rapporteur is proposing and that is what we hope the commission will correct. and, as soon as possible, until such future measures are in place, it is prudent, given the delays in the chain, to extend existing aid not by one year but by two, as the committee on agriculture of the european parliament wants.
in writing. - (pt) there are several reasons for illegal immigration, but we believe that the main ones are, on the one hand, the flight from the difficult situations in many countries and the search for better living conditions, and, on the other hand, the construction of a fortress europe, which makes it increasingly difficult for third-country nationals to enter the territory of the member states. we therefore agree with the rapporteur that the main offenders in the case of illegal immigration are those who derive economic and financial benefits from it. we therefore necessarily agree with the establishment of a sufficiently deterrent and repressive framework which penalizes not only the smugglers and traffickers of human beings but also the employers who take advantage of this situation. the adoption of these measures must be accompanied by effective control on the ground and measures aimed at the effective integration of these workers, guaranteeing their social rights and conditions, and not the expeditious expulsion of persons in an irregular situation, as is increasingly the case.
in writing. - (pt) we voted in favour because the rapporteur proposes to extend aid to a large sector in crisis, as we had already done in the past when we adopted a proposal in the jové report on the cmo for fruit and vegetables. we also agree with the rapporteur's proposal to extend the extension to two years, so as to give the commission time to prepare a new aid scheme for dried fruit (nuts and dried fruit), and with the introduction of aid for hazelnuts. in any event, the extension should be maintained until the entry into force of the new regime, which should be included in the fruit and vegetables cmo. it is essential that in this new regime the commission takes account of the enormous socioeconomic importance of these fruits for the southern countries, as well as their environmental value in the fight against desertification. if the aid is not withdrawn, the sector will be in danger, mainly because of low-priced imports from third countries.
in writing. - (pt) in portugal, as in various countries of the european union, industrial restructuring has led to the dismissal of thousands of workers, increased job insecurity and innumerable socioeconomic costs, particularly for the countries and regions directly involved, putting their economic and social cohesion at risk. in recent months alone, thousands of workers in portugal in various companies in the metal, shipbuilding, electricity, footwear, textile and clothing sectors have been threatened with dismissal and early retirement or have been forced to terminate their employment contracts amicably, particularly in the companies of the siderurgia nacional group, the abb group, renault, setenave and lisnave, indelma/simens, j.c. clark and vesticom. this extremely serious situation requires special attention from both the member states and the community institutions, which must modify their policies to give special priority to respect for workers' rights, quality employment and the economic and social cohesion of the regions where the companies wishing to close down and/or relocate their industrial units are located. it is also urgently necessary to amend existing directives in this area to make the status of workers' representatives compulsory and binding in respect of collective redundancies in the defence of workers' rights, in particular in all processes of restructuring, merger and relocation of industrial enterprises, and to make community and national aid conditional on long-term commitments to employment and local and national development.
in writing. - (pt) we support the proposals and initiatives aimed at deepening the friendly relations between the european union and macao after the incorporation of that territory into the people's republic of china, which has become a special administrative region following the transfer which we welcome. all conditions are now in place for the intensification of economic and cultural relations, on the basis of cooperation and mutual benefit, between the people's republic of china, in particular through the macao special administrative region, the european union and its member states. however, as regards the report on which we voted, we remain critical of the aspects which seem to show a certain lack of confidence, particularly in respect of the respect of the agreements, because we consider that the reality shows that there is no reason to be suspicious.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for the report by mr mário soares on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament entitled 'the european union and macao: after the year 2000' because it expresses the concerns and initiatives which the european union should take towards macao. the concern is limited to the need to preserve in this special administrative region the freedoms and rights which existed before the transfer of sovereignty, including freedom of worship. the freedoms derive from the principle of 'one country, two systems' which was negotiated with great difficulty by the portuguese and chinese authorities. the initiatives constitute the concrete actions that the european union can stimulate and promote in macao in order to ensure that the region maintains a democratic collective life, where the human rights and individual freedoms of macao residents are respected and prosperity and peace are valued. among the initiatives in the report aimed at preserving macao's autonomy and specificity in relation to china, i would highlight three: the continuous monitoring of the consolidation of the principle 'one country, two systems', the encouragement of european investment in the sar as a corollary of active cooperation at the most diverse levels, and the call for the urgent appointment of a representative of the union in macao with a view to boosting relations between the two regions. portugal administered macau for four centuries. we are not ashamed of our past in this region, quite the contrary. on the one hand, our presence has helped to promote the peaceful coexistence of european and chinese civilisations. on the other hand, we have bequeathed to the people of macao a region with considerable development indicators, legislative, judicial and customs autonomy, where the rights and freedoms of each individual are part of a heritage of civilization which it is our duty to preserve.

the next item is the oral question (b5-0009/2001) by mr von wogau and mrs suominen and mrs doyle, on behalf of the group of the european people's party (christian democrats) and the european democrats, to the commission on the stability plan for ireland and france.
(de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by addressing the question of why we have submitted a request for information on the decisions in ireland. i think that in this case it should be recognized that we have reached a new stage in the development of the european union and the integration of the community economies. twelve member states now have a common market with a single currency, so it is in the true sense of the word a regional market. in this new european regional market, there is an urgent need for economic policy to be formulated in accordance with common principles. we are therefore pleased to see that the european commission and the ecofin council of ministers have acted accordingly. this was the first time that a decision concerning one of these member states had been taken in this way. it is about the stability of the european currency. as you know, my political group, in all the debates in this house, has always given the highest priority to the stability of the european currency, and we will continue to do so. we believe that one of the pillars of the social market economy is a stable currency. we need this also in relation to the european currency. i want to make it clear that this priority will continue to be our priority in the future. i would like to ask, however, whether the european commission and the members of ecofin have chosen the right target in the first decision of this kind. in the case of ireland, he is without doubt one of the exemplary boys of the european union. the question is whether other countries are in a position to reduce their deficits. ireland can make surpluses. other countries continue to have a huge global debt of over 100%. ireland is reducing it to less than 40%, and in this case it is also a model for other member states of the european union. other countries are struggling with unemployment. on unemployment, ireland has made exemplary progress in creating new jobs. other member states are struggling with only slow growth. ireland is achieving growth rates that other member states of the european union can only dream of. however, we must note that this strong growth is overheating the irish economy, creating risks which are also inconvenient for irish citizens. if, for example, we look at rental and land prices in dublin and compare them with those in frankfurt am main, it is clear that these price developments are also having a negative impact on irish citizens. it is therefore necessary to do something about this. but the question is, who will have to do something? is it the european union that will have to intervene, or is it a matter that should and can be resolved by ireland itself, by the irish government, by the irish parliament? this raises the question of who will be responsible for the european economy in the future. in this respect, we must make efforts to ensure that this responsibility is clearly defined. we must know very clearly who is responsible for what, so that if we succeed we can praise them and if we fail we can also say that a certain country or a certain institution has made a mistake. in this sense, a clear division of labour is emerging in the european union. the european union is certainly competent in competition, in competition between companies in the european union. commissioner monti has often shown that the european commission takes this task very seriously. and the european commission is responsible for monetary stability on the one hand there is the strong european central bank, on the other hand there are the finance ministers, who have an obligation to ensure that the stability and growth pact is implemented. competition and monetary stability are therefore a matter for the european union. however, the responsibility for taxation remains undoubtedly with the member states of the union. the same applies to social security systems and training. in this case we have to ask the question: what is wrong with monetary stability, in other words, is this stability in euroland in danger because of what is happening in ireland? let's say we had another situation. let us assume that the inflation rate in germany is 6 or 7%. germany produces one third of the gross national product of euroland. the immediate consequence of this would be an increase in the average rate of inflation in the european union, so that the european central bank would have to act, thus balancing the economy. however, ireland is a small national economy. ireland's gross national product is only slightly higher than that of the west of london. the overheating in ireland has no effect at european level and there is therefore no need for action by the european central bank. ireland itself can no longer contribute to a slowdown, as it did in the past, by raising the interest rate of the irish central bank. this means that ireland itself will have to take responsibility, also towards the irish people, for this overheating to be controlled in one way or another, but i would always leave the decision in the hands of the irish. i would not take it to the level of the european union. and a second question: on 12 january, the european commission took the decision to transfer eur 401 million to ireland. additional funding of over eur 726 million is also to be added. this is money that has been injected further into the irish economy. in this connection, i would like to ask whether there have been talks between commissioners solbes and barnier to resolve this apparent contradiction. in other words, what is happening in germany, what is happening in france, what is happening in italy is giving much more cause for concern about the stability of the euro. i hope the european commission will have the same courage when it has to deal with matters relating to these large countries. (the president cut the speaker off)
i thank commissioner solbes for his report. the debate is closed.
- mr president, i support the haug report and congratulate the rapporteur. i therefore agree that supplementary and amending budget no 1/2001, which provides for eur 971 million for additional costs arising from the bse crisis, should be supported by parliament. i would like to make the following comments on this proposal: - firstly, the eur 971 million now available is necessary but not sufficient. but you will see. - secondly, the bse crisis, the real difficulties facing producers, the breakdown in consumer confidence and the food safety requirements associated with the preservation of public health are not compatible with weak responses, with the postponement of obvious solutions such as conflicts of interest or budgetary difficulties. the sensitivity of the situation warrants a swift and complete response. the climate of crisis must not be perpetuated. this issue has to go, it needed to go quickly off the agenda. let us have the courage to put an end to it, which will coincide with the restoration of the citizens' confidence. the current emergency situation should not be confused with the essential revision of production methods to be promoted by the reform of the common agricultural policy, a reform which is necessary but which cannot be rushed, which would be the case if it were carried out in the midst of a crisis or if it were guided solely by financial considerations or national interests. these would, in fact, be aspects which would condemn her to birth; - thirdly, the foregoing justifies the european parliament's having effective decisionmaking power on all agricultural expenditure, including that in subcategory 1-a, and at the same time the nucleus of expenditure in subcategory 1-b is being enlarged. this will respect the legitimate interest of the citizens;
- mr president, the proposal for a supplementary budget of eur 971 million to increase the financing of measures to combat bse seems necessary and fair. this means that the budgetary margin still available for agricultural expenditure under the cap is practically exhausted. it is therefore natural and necessary to start the debate on the financing of the bse crisis in future years. but this debate must be timely and methodical, incorporating an overall assessment of european agriculture, the current state of the rural world and the way in which the cap has responded to this reality. in my view, the cap will have to be redesigned to give adequate answers to what is now called the 'european agricultural model', and it will even be necessary to change the wording of its objectives in the treaties. because it is true that the cap is now largely outdated in its foundations, objectives and instruments of implementation. but what i find unacceptable is that the bse crisis is used as a pretext to force reforms on the basis of individual convenience. this is what seems to be the result of the amendments now tabled by the commission, which provide for or propose to amend the basis for co-financing of measures to support the beef and veal sector. we all know that some member states want to renationalize the cap. i think this is a terrible mistake which will set the european union backwards rather than forwards, and will particularly penalise the poorest countries whose farmers will be treated less favourably than their community colleagues. but i'll take that debate. but such a debate should be conducted in the context of an overall assessment of the cap and other community policies, and not in this opportunistic way, taking advantage of a crisis to make national selfishness prevail when what is required is a solidarity response by the community to those in difficulty.

- doc. a5-0059/2001 by mr gemelli, on behalf of the committee on development and cooperation, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on the development policy of the european community (com (2000) 212 - c5-0264/2000 - 2000/2141 (cos)).
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. mr president, my brief speech is only intended to underline some of the points i have already mentioned in the opinion of which i was rapporteur for the committee on external affairs. firstly, this commission document is welcome, not only because it aims to correct specific aspects of the implementation of development and cooperation policy, but also because it seeks to place this action within a more general framework which has a significant scope in accordance with what was established at the helsinki european council. the report by mr gemelli has deepened the content and brought more consistency to this objective and therefore deserves to be approved. secondly, i would like to draw attention once again to the advantage that would be gained from greater intra-community coordination of development policy and foreign policy. we all know that europe's contribution to developing countries is considerable and that we are the world's largest donor, but this is not sufficiently acknowledged, both in terms of the financial implications, particularly in terms of the commitments and expectations created, and in terms of the responsibility it implies for political influence on the development processes of the countries concerned. a common strategy to assert the european union's international role, legitimised by the principles of cooperation and the defence of human rights, can only be of benefit to those countries which wish to move from the status of the vulnerable or even of victims to that of partners. thirdly and lastly, development policy must be made more effective, particularly by means of a more reliable system of monitoring, control and evaluation of programmes and their results on the ground. this may possibly require the creation of new management bodies, but it will certainly require better use of existing resources, in particular delegations, and a genuine reform of the community's external service.
- mr president, commissioner, the proposal for a council regulation providing for certain technical conservation measures for large migratory populations now incorporates into a single legal framework measures which were scattered over several regulations. the proposal is therefore of positive significance in that it creates better conditions for the effective implementation of one of the most important aspects of the common fisheries policy, which is aimed at fisheries species subject to a high fishing pressure due to their high geographical mobility and their high commercial value. but for reasons unknown to me, i must say that the rapporteur is speaking on the original commission proposal and not on the compromise proposal which emerged from the december fisheries council, and on which i think it would be more appropriate for parliament to give its opinion. i will not repeat here points already made by the two rapporteurs and especially by my colleagues from the epp, but i would just like to emphasize two important points: firstly, the issue of the discipline to be required of vessels operating under a flag of convenience, a fundamental aspect which we must discipline; and secondly, the fact that experimental fishing vessels should be included in the quotas of their respective fisheries. finally, i would like to point out that the european union must work in the various international bodies for the coordination of fisheries management in which it participates to ensure that all the fishing fleets in the world, especially those of the major fishing powers, respect exactly the same conservation rules as the european union. that seems to me to be a fundamental point. finally, i would like to thank the two rapporteurs for the excellent work they have done and i hope that their reports will be adopted with the amendments tabled by the committee on fisheries.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are indeed faced with an agreement of great importance both for equatorial guinea and for various regions of the european continent. but to talk about tuna in the north atlantic means that we must also take account of what is happening in other regions, such as the autonomous region of the azores, where we have just recorded the second year of a practically zero tuna harvest in 2000. our fishing fleet of about half a hundred vessels has been virtually at a standstill for the last two years. in these circumstances, we cannot help but wonder whether certain fishing gear, such as the hook, could be putting the sustainability of tuna stocks in the north atlantic in question. and in this connection we would like to know whether the commission has already carried out any research into the consequences of using this kind of art. secondly, we would also like to know whether the commission is aware of the crisis in the tuna fisheries sector in the azores and madeira and whether it intends to take any action on the matter. finally, i would also like to point out that there are tuna fishing gear such as those used on all the islands of micronesia (jump and stick) which do not put dolphins and turtles at risk as do other far more aggressive practices and techniques, and we would like to know whether the commission would be willing to take this into account.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to congratulate mr salafranca and mr marset campos on their excellent reports, which allow us to discuss today the mandate for the negotiations on two agreements, as has already been mentioned here: one between the european union and mercosur, the other with chile. i would stress that the agreement with mercosur may become the first worldwide agreement between two customs unions. the importance of these should therefore be duly emphasized by this parliament, not only because they will contribute to strengthening relations between the european union, its member states and the countries of the southern cone of the american continent, but also because they will allow for a more balanced development of cooperation relations in latin america, counteracting the growing influence of the movement of the free trade area of the americas in the region, nafta. in this context, the agreements are strategically important and deserve our political support. two additional notes, mr president: the first, to say, as my colleagues the rapporteurs have said, that i too regard as negative the council's decision to make the conclusion of agreements conditional on the conclusion of the wto negotiations. we therefore welcome the fact that portugal and spain agreed at the recent spanish-portuguese summit in sintra to join their efforts to separate the agreements from the conclusion of the wto negotiating round. the second note is to note that the negotiating mandate also covers the political and cooperation aspects, in addition to the commercial one, as is natural. i would not be surprised - and we are not opposed to that - if the agreements were to take the form of association agreements, or until the council had clearly defined in its mandate the legal basis which would determine the nature of those agreements. the question is not new, even in terms of the greater or lesser power of intervention of the european parliament with regard to its final content, as the other rapporteurs have pointed out. in any event, it should be borne in mind that these agreements will always have to be ratified by the national parliaments of the member states, giving them the necessary democratic legitimacy. in addition, to my knowledge, no agreement between the european union and third countries has so far been refused on the pretext that the legal framework which has been applied is inadequate. so do not create new obstacles where they do not exist. the important thing is that effective agreements are concluded and that they are mutually beneficial for the union, for the states and for the people to whom they are intended.
in writing. - (pt) this amending budget is insufficient and only covers temporary measures, although it responds to the urgent need to finance the costs of the bse crisis, which is why we voted in favour. it should be noted, however, that commissioner fischler himself admits that 'the crisis may be deeper than we think' and that the cost estimates, which amount to €3 billion, presented by farmers' organisations indicate that the consequences of the bse crisis are underestimated, including at budgetary level. however, since we believe that it is not the farmers who have to pay for a crisis for which they are not responsible, but the common agricultural policy, budgetary constraints cannot be used as a pretext to prevent the adoption of all the measures necessary to curb the bse crisis and to defend the interests of farmers. we need to re-evaluate the financial perspective. the aim of the cap reform, which has been urgently needed for a long time, as we have been arguing, should not be to make budgetary savings but rather to protect agricultural production, respecting food security, defending food sovereignty, promoting regional specificities and economic and social cohesion, guaranteeing farmers' income through fair prices, safe and quality agricultural products for consumers. we voted against the amendment which aims to put the question of co-financing, i.e. the renationalisation of the cap's costs, on the agenda, because we do not agree with this measure.

the first point to be stressed is that negotiating an agreement on agri-food trade only makes sense in the context of a global agreement that integrates other sectors of the economy and other dimensions of society. the second point to be stressed is that the european union must be open to further efforts to reduce domestic support for market access and export refunds. however, this effort should require transparency of rules and equivalent action by our trading partners. thirdly, it is essential that the peace clause and the special safeguard clause be renewed in order to maintain the stability of agricultural markets and farmers' incomes. the fourth point concerns the need to preserve the european agricultural model, which is characterized, as mr konrad schwaiger has said, by the predominance of family farms and the multiplicity of functions that agriculture plays for society. but we must bear in mind that we must preserve the primary function of agriculture, which is production. otherwise, if we do not take this into account, our rural area will be, in the short or long term, a mere agricultural museum. fifthly, we must emphasize the initiative for the least developed countries, but we must create the conditions so that this measure is not misused by those who are not entitled to it. the european union is already the world's largest importer of agri-food products, with a deficit of over usd 16 billion and therefore running a deficit. in contrast, the united states and all the members of the cairns group are highly surplus. in view of this situation, it is legitimate for the european union to have the right to preserve its agricultural productive fabric, which is the basis of the economic vitality of its rural area. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by expressing my full support for the position taken by mr deprez, namely that the portuguese initiative proposal and the finnish initiative proposal should be rejected. these two proposals call into question article 202, which lays down the general principle that the commission is reserved the power to implement acts adopted by the council, although the council may reserve the right to exercise implementing powers directly in specific cases for which there are grounds. the justification put forward that the council intends to reserve to itself the right to exercise those implementing powers for a transitional period of five years in view of the fact that the member states play a more important role in visa policy, which reflects the political sensitivity of the matter, is not, however, in itself sufficient to infringe a general principle of the community legal order. and it is not relevant, since it is contrary to the principle contained in the protocol integrating the schengen acquis into the framework of the european union which explicitly transfers these matters to the institutional legal framework of the union. as regards the frahm report, i think the rapporteur's position to reject the portuguese initiative should also be supported. the reason for this is that it would lead to a lack of consistency in the rules governing the movement of third country nationals within the territory of the schengen states. the schengen convention covers three categories of third-country nationals who are entitled to move within the territory of the member states: those who are subject to the visa requirement, those who are exempt from that requirement and holders of a residence permit. this initiative covers only the second of these categories, which fragment the right of movement of third country nationals and does not indicate to what extent these rules will repeal, replace or amend the schengen acquis. the nassauer report also proposes, and rightly so, the rejection of the french initiative. in view of these three proposals, which we intend to reject, we should appeal to commissioner vitorino and the commission to take the initiative in this area and to ensure the overall coherence of the community structure of which it is the guardian.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the initiative we have in hand to set up the european crime prevention network is intended to meet the growing need for crime prevention. the need for greater and better international cooperation in this field, particularly at the level of the european union, has been felt. we know, however, that this cooperation is hampered by differences in definitions, procedures, structures and so on. the need for comparable data on crime, victimization and criminal justice in the european union arises from studies, statistics, criminal policies, structural factors, etc. the overall aim is to assess the various actions, seeking to identify, in priority areas, the key measures which are desirable and possible to take, improving the european union's response capacity and the effectiveness of crime prevention policies, both at national and international level. these were the political guidelines that emerged from the tampere european council and led to the conclusion at the high level conference held in the algarve, portugal, in may 2000 that the question of crime prevention should be addressed in a multidisciplinary and partnership-based framework. it is essential to ensure the safety of the european citizen who wants to live in peace and that increasingly implies not only the effective suppression of crime but the adoption of intelligent prevention policies which also imply the ability to understand and react to the psychological and sociological phenomena that cause or increase the feeling of insecurity. such prevention, which is necessary in order to reduce both the quantity and quality of crime or the causes of crime, should cover all types of crime, including organised crime, not forgetting the areas of urban, juvenile and drug-related crime. this is an important and ambitious project, because crime knows no borders. the network thus aims to promote cooperation and the exchange of information between member states, to collect and analyse information on actions taken and on existing best practices in the field of prevention, with the aim of contributing to a reflection on future decisions to be taken in this field. the committee on civil liberties was somewhat split into two groups: one which wanted to reserve to this network the right to intervene in areas directly related to the european and international dimension, and another which focused exclusively on micro-crime. i believe that the compromise proposal that has been adopted was a good solution, not excluding any of the aspects of crime prevention. we know that the phenomena of organised crime and micro-crime are two different realities, which require different responses: the first is one of the priority objectives of the union and its member states, while the second is a primary responsibility of local, regional and national institutions. to prevent this, the union's role should be limited to encouraging the exchange of information between national authorities in order to provide an exchange of best practice and research in the name of the principle of subsidiarity. the people of the european union have the right to demand that the union effectively address the problem of the growing threats of crime against their freedom and rights.
in writing. - (pt) we agree, on the whole, with the rapporteur's position, which rejects the main part of the commission's proposal, particularly as regards the reduction of sugar quotas to 115 000 tonnes. we regret, however, that our proposal to correct an injustice by the commission in respect of the quota allocated to portugal was not accepted. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not undermined by the fact that the community is not able to meet the needs of the developing countries. the economic viability of the only portuguese beet processing plant on the mainland, in coruche, could be jeopardized if this plant cannot process nearly 100 000 tonnes. that is why we are proposing an increase of 30 000 tonnes. this correction would ensure fairness for portuguese farmers and the sustainability of beet production, which, moreover, could and should be increased when the alqueva dam is operational. similarly, with regard to the azores, we consider it essential to ensure the viability of the only existing plant on the island of são miguel, whose operation is essential for this outermost region.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's legal system is not undermined by the fact that the member states are not able to take the necessary measures to ensure that the community's legal system is not undermined. it did so not because it was prejudiced against the expediency of governmental or state initiatives in the context of the creation of a common judicial area, as any less attentive interpretation might suggest from what happened this morning. on the contrary, i believe that outside the fields of immigration and asylum, and in the end outside the framework of the free movement of persons, initiatives such as this are naturally welcome. what they cannot do, and this is what concerns parliament, is to replace the inconsistencies of the member states which generate the legislative quagmire by each one shaking the "water from the hood" with part-initiatives to his own taste and in his own way, leaving out a true vision of the community as a whole, a common responsibility and a serious and coherent legislative body, as the commission is proving that it is possible to do - and i am looking at commissioner antónio vitorino - in one of the most sensitive areas which is that which concerns the european model of coexistence and its corresponding fundamental freedoms, in this case the people. that said, and particularly in the context of this report, where the functioning of the internal market implies the deepening of the european social area, anything that appears, even in the member states, to eliminate and reduce conflicts and disputes with cross-border roots is naturally welcome, as i believe tomorrow's vote will prove. what is this german initiative all about? the court held that the court of first instance had not been entitled to take a decision on the question of the interpretation of article 85 (1) of the treaty in the light of the provisions of the treaty on the freedom of movement for workers. until now, international cooperation in the production of evidence has been governed by the 1970 hague convention, which has a limited scope for european countries in that, to my knowledge, it has only been ratified by eleven member states. in the absence of a legal instrument covering the 15 member states and those which follow, not all citizens who have recourse to the courts enjoy the same facilities before the law, depending on whether their country or the country to which they have applied has ratified the convention. with this new regulation, all courts will benefit from the same law and, of course, citizens will be equally protected, since this community instrument favours direct contact between courts of different nationalities. the obstacles to the existence of a european judicial area are essentially removed either by introducing a standard european form to be used by the courts of the requesting states in the language of the requested courts in order to facilitate communication between them, or by creating a central body in each country to receive and distribute requests from another state for production of evidence from a national court, or by establishing the principle that every request has a reply, obliging the european courts requested to produce evidence to do so within six months, after which, if they do not do so, they will have to give explanations to the requesting court. in my amendments to this proposal from the federal republic of germany i introduced, among other things, the obligation for the central bodies to draw up guides to legislation on official languages recognised by each court in each member state and to disseminate accredited legal translation offices. finally, in view of this innovative framework which greatly facilitates the speedy functioning of the courts in all the member states - and this seems to me to be one of the fundamental concerns of the german initiative - i proposed that only exceptionally the direct contact between courts provided for in the regulation should be disregarded in favour of the traditional consular procedures which, in my view, are naturally cumbersome, bureaucratic, somewhat distant in action and very responsible for the delays in justice in these matters when there are conflicts or actions of a cross-border nature. this last point was not understood by some of the members from other groups who voted against this amendment, the scope of which, in my view, is merely to emphasize a certain conception of europe which i have outlined: a community of its own right, of community law, more than a mere diplomatic community or a community of international law. i therefore put this amendment back in the hope that this detail, which is not so much a detail, will be understood by the majority of my colleagues.
- mr president, commissioner, the 1999 annual report of olaf is naturally dominated by the dismantling of the criminal organisation for the counterfeiting of butter and the extortion of public money with its headquarters in naples and branches in central europe. in addition to congratulating the rapporteur on his excellent report, i think it is entirely justified to pay a special tribute not only to olaf but also to the italian and french judicial organisations whose joint work has made it possible to dismantle this organised crime network. according to the latest information available, the butter adulteration scheme set up by some families in the neapolitan camorra has produced 130,000 tonnes of totally or partially fake butter. this, i suppose, will make this the biggest case of fraud ever committed against europe in the framework of the common agricultural policy. the first question that arises here is how is it possible that a policy which is supposed to serve rural communities and farmers ends up systematically financing large agro-industrial complexes and that these often prove to be so unscrupulous in respecting the community budget, consumers and the farmers on whose behalf they are financed? the second question that arises here is how is it possible that no fraud of this magnitude, meticulously planned and carried out over three years and involving astronomical sums of community subsidies, has been detected, that the commission has not been able to detect anything at all? after the persistent revelations of fraud in the use of subsidised milk powder for calf feeding, of fake butter exports and of the falsification of milk products, we now have this mega-scandal without the commission seemingly being prepared to make a thorough review of its entire system of intervention in milk products. the pan-european nature of this fraud, which has involved large dairy companies in countries such as belgium, france, italy and germany, makes it unacceptable to leave the policies needed to combat food counterfeiting to the member states on a permanent basis. it makes it all the more urgent that the commission take direct action to combat the adulteration of products such as olive oil, wine and milk products. the commission has refused to disclose the names, to place them under special surveillance or to take preventive measures against the numerous dairy companies involved on the grounds of secrecy and the presumption of innocence. i cannot help but note, however, that the commission's discretion in this case is in stark contrast to the way it has dealt with the widow of an adviser to a former commissioner who is now being asked to repay 18 months' salary to her deceased spouse. if there are dairy companies that are unable to distinguish tens of thousands of tons of mistletoe made from animal waste, oils and chemicals from real butter, how can consumers trust their competence to ensure the quality of their products? the question is also whether the commission considers us all to be on an equal footing, or whether, when it comes to large companies and large interests, some are clearly more equal than others.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating mrs eija-riita korhola and the committee on the environment on the improvements they propose to the text before us. and i congratulate them also on having accepted most of the contributions we approved in the committee on civil liberties, where i was the shadow rapporteur on the work of mrs ludford. parliament has been - and rightly so - at the forefront of the trend towards greater openness and transparency in the activities of public authorities and access to documents. access to environmental information should be regarded as one of the essential pillars of an effective environmental policy because it contributes to a greater public awareness of environmental issues, because it thus improves the protection and quality of the environment, because it allows more effective monitoring of whether community environmental legislation is being applied fully and effectively and, finally, because it allows citizens to participate more effectively in environmental decision-making that may affect their lives. with this initiative we propose to replace the 1990 directive, mainly with the aim, among others, of adapting the directive to the evolution of information technologies by creating a second generation directive that reflects the changes in the way information is produced in our society today. in conclusion, i would like to stress two points: the first is the question of the time limits within which public authorities are obliged to provide the requested information as soon as possible and at the latest within one month. and the second is that we have limited the scope of the exceptions: while it is acceptable that, in order to protect legitimate interests, there must be provisions which exclude certain information from being disclosed, it is nevertheless essential that those exceptions be rigorously defined and interpreted in a restrictive manner so as not to weaken the general principle of access and so that the directive can in fact achieve its objective.

authentic national debates, by the portuguese with the portuguese, the british with the british, the germans with the germans, the czechs with the czechs, the poles with the poles, about what they want for the future of the european union. only then will we be able to arrive in 2004 at a decision-making body which is definitely qualified to take decisions for the future, or else we will fall - mr president, i should like to begin by congratulating the undersigned on the oral question which has been tabled on the interest, importance and timeliness of the matter. water is a commodity essential to life. it is also a strategic factor in development and as such is a matter which concerns us all, as has already been pointed out here and as is quite true. this perspective now requires a joint approach to the planning and management of shared rivers. at european level, the new framework directive, and at united nations level, the helsinki convention, and more specifically its protocol on the protection and use of transboundary rivers and international lakes, constitute a framework of international law embodying this new vision and these new principles. as is well known, portugal was actively involved in the drafting of the water management directive, seeking to give priority to fundamental aspects of the protection and sustainable use of water, taking account of the climatic and geographical conditions of the iberian peninsula. it is understandable that he did. portugal and spain are in a rather unequal position, since we are a country of jujube, that is to say, in portugal there are no waters flowing to spain, but in spain there are waters flowing to portugal. on the other hand, our neighbours may take measures in the management of their water resources which affect portuguese interests, but we, on the contrary, cannot do anything in the management of our water resources which directly affects spanish interests. joint management of common river basins within the framework of international law imposed by the directive referred to above is thus one of the fundamental vectors of the conduct of policy-makers in this relevant sector, both in bilateral negotiations and in the multilateral framework. the new spanish water plan, in that it has abandoned the mainly douro and tagus river crossings - rivers which are shared between portugal and spain - is already, in our view, a step in the right direction. the directive is an extremely important safety net, since it coordinates the management of water bodies so that they are not regarded as units defined by administrative or political boundaries. with the aim of integrated water management, optimising objectives appropriate to the specific conditions of the regions of the european union, the rules of the framework directive will enable, now and in the future, and with the collaboration of governments, the implementation of hydrological plans which take account of such important aspects as water quality, the preservation of adequate flow rates, the climate and the protection of biodiversity and the environment.

in writing. - (pt) it is regrettable that parliament has not considered it appropriate to defend before the commission and the council a new type of sustainable development, creating quality jobs and guaranteeing a high level of social protection, as was advocated in the motion for a resolution tabled by our group. the truth is that the guidelines contained in the commission document for preparing the stockholm european council emphasize the priority of completing the internal market through accelerating the process of liberalization, strengthening financial markets through the establishment of pension funds, increasing the competitiveness of enterprises based on reducing wage costs, flexibility and labour mobility. meanwhile, the main legislative initiatives foreseen by the social agenda are not progressing, especially those aimed at strengthening the individual and collective rights of workers and improving their working conditions. furthermore, social and employment policies remain closely linked to the stability pact and the push for liberalisation continues in sectors such as gas, electricity, postal services and transport, which calls into question the objectives of quality employment with rights and the satisfaction of the basic needs of the most vulnerable sections of the population.
in writing. - (pt) despite its title, the von wogau report does not analyse the economic and social situation in the eu, but merely restates the general lines and insists on the single objective of monetary policy, price stability and control of inflation below the 2% threshold set by the european central bank, without taking into account the impact on the real economy, particularly on employment. it stressed the need to speed up the liberalisation of telecommunications, mail, energy and transport, irrespective of the consequences for employment, regional cohesion, working conditions and a quality universal public service. it continues to count on a good economic situation, despite the signs of economic weakness in the community, which the report itself notes, whereas it would be appropriate to reflect on the weakness of the us economy, the economic recession in japan and their impact on the european economy. he stresses the need to make the labour market more flexible, while there are over 14 million unemployed and some 65 million poor people in the eu, gross income inequalities and increasingly precarious work, with new jobs being created in temporary or part-time jobs. it continues to insist on moving towards the euro without creating the necessary mechanisms to reduce its economic and social risks. all in the name of the sacred and holy of competitiveness and the abstract notion of the social market economy. that is why we voted against the report. the economic and monetary policy of the eu and its member states should aim at continuously improving the quality of life of citizens and at improving the satisfaction of their needs.
in writing. - (pt) it is important that the european parliament should decide on the future of the biotechnology industry, not only because it will be a leading industry in the future, but above all because of the ethical and socioeconomic issues it raises, as in the case of gmos, genetics and medicines. unfortunately, the parliament's report, which mr purvis has been given, does not deal with these questions. it is the one-sided and dangerous spokesman for all the interests of the big multinational companies in the sector. it is moving towards a logic of liberalization and generalization of the use of products, in the name of good causes, which have not all known consequences for the environment and human health, and which raise profound ethical questions, such as genetic modification and the patentability of living and nature, which is a heritage belonging to everyone. the logic of profitability and profit, without evaluation, leads industry to want to put products on the market whose safety is not guaranteed and which also create economic dependence among their users, as is the case in agriculture with gmos, all in order to compensate for the high costs of research. in addition, in the area of medicines and their patentability, developing countries and the poorest sections of the population are excluded, as we can see from the actions of multinational companies in brazil and south africa. that is why we reject this report. research in this area should be in the public domain and not managed by private interests, as it is necessary to ensure fairness of benefits and the necessary assessment of risk.

- mr president, the initiatives taken by some portuguese people, including myself, to achieve this joint resolution have already had some positive effects. firstly, the joint resolution itself, which expresses the will of parliament to support it; in writing. - (pt) in line with the adjustment of the financial perspective, the 2002 budget will not exceed the limit of 1.1% of community gnp, which means that we can foresee a new austerity budget for next year. the rapporteur is in agreement with this and sets the level of implementation and the quality of expenditure as the main priorities for next year, with issues such as employment and social issues or the budgetary impact of enlargement and the introduction of the euro being relegated to a footnote. food security is mentioned - in the face of the bse and foot-and-mouth disease crisis - and a long-term strategy is advocated, but there is no assessment of the costs of these crises on agricultural expenditure, no compensatory measures for farmers - as we suggested with the proposal to create a special reserve for bse issues - and no plans to modulate and set ceilings for cap aid, which is essential. we therefore consider that the re-budgetization of the structural funds must take place as a matter of urgency and imperative, in order to guarantee full implementation of the budgeted amounts. we also recall that in this context we refuse to call into question the post-2001 spending target of agenda 2000. finally, we find it particularly worrying that the rapporteur expressly regrets in his report that decisions on financial regulation will only be taken by qualified majority from 2007 onwards and 'forgets' that if this were not the case, the country would already be losing a fundamental instrument - unanimity - for the setting of the next financial framework, including the part relating to the structural funds and the cohesion fund.

- the next item is the report (a5-0084/2001) by mrs hermange, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the communication from the commission to the council, the european parliament, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions - towards a barrier-free europe for disabled people (com (2000) 284 - c5-0632/2000 - 2000/2296 (cos)).
- mr president, the matter we are dealing with is of the utmost importance. not only because it is a question of guaranteeing to disabled people the fulfilment of a fundamental right of access to a life of dignity in all its various aspects, as should be guaranteed to all people, disabled or not, but because it is all the more important in the knowledge that there are more than 37 million disabled people in the european union. and it is urgent to take all the necessary measures to guarantee everyone genuine freedom of movement, including not only the possibility of travelling in suitably adapted transport but also of studying, working, integrating and living with dignity in any of the member states of the european union. it is particularly important to create the conditions necessary to guarantee everyone access to jobs with rights, by promoting their access to training, by creating incentives and by developing positive actions, in order to combat unemployment, which, for people with disabilities, is three times higher than the average in the european union. it is also essential to take account of the need to prevent new forms of social exclusion, in particular information exclusion, and therefore there is a need for an action plan for the effective participation of disabled people in the information society, as well as for the right of access of disabled people to electronic and telecommunications services and equipment, and for a more active role in european research centres in technological research aimed at improving the quality of life of disabled people. the fight against discrimination against disabled people requires concrete measures to support families with one or more disabled people and the establishment of a school for all which ensures the integration of children with disabilities, but also all the necessary support with specialised teachers and specialised techniques where appropriate. a decisive push is also needed in legislation and concrete action to include the integration of people with disabilities and the participation of their organisations in the definition, monitoring and evaluation of these policies in the various policies. we therefore agree with the general proposals contained in mrs hermange's report, which we support and to which we have made our possible contribution, including through a proposal which we are now presenting, and we hope that the commission and the council will recognise the importance of giving the greatest possible attention to this problem and achieve the full integration of disabled people in the various aspects of life, taking advantage of the declaration of 2003 as the 'european year of disabled citizens' to raise public awareness and member states' awareness of this issue.
