in writing. - (pt) the purpose of this proposal for a regulation is to stabilise temporarily the degressivity of the specific cereals aid for portugal by maintaining the same level of aid for the 2001/02 marketing year as for the previous year. this is a decision taken under the portuguese presidency of the eu, which we agree with, and which aims to maintain the co-financing of aid for one year by compensating for the loss of income of portuguese farmers. however, given the cap's pressure on farmers' incomes and taking into account the needs of portuguese farmers, the stabilisation of degressivity should cover the whole period of the current financial framework, i.e. until the 2005/2006 marketing year. in addition, there should be an upper limit to the stabilisation of the co-financed aid, for example double the limit of the simplified scheme, to manage savings that could finance both the extension of the stabilisation, the creation of support mechanisms for protein crop production and the implementation of a programme for the conversion of alternative crops to cereal production for the improvement of land with lower utilization capacities. it should be remembered that many portuguese small farmers cannot receive the aid because they do not market all their production. they should be able to devote their production to self-consumption while retaining the right to aid, for example by starting from farm income categories.
in writing. - (pt) the community action programme in the field of public health 2001-2006 is a good theoretical document, which reflects the philosophy of the eurocrats on public health, although it is timid on certain issues, in particular: regional coordination and funding must clearly demonstrate the importance of human health without undermining national responsibilities; therapeutic and diagnostic means must not be regarded exclusively as commercial and industrial activities. it turns out that this component of public health has been treated as a separate commercial domain, currently controlled by multinational corporations, challenging national and regional interests. there can be no good public health programme if this situation does not change; the programme avoids talking about the determinants of health in the workplace and the health of workers. the direct and indirect damage of the occupational activity of many working conditions cannot be socialized, leaving the capitalists the increased profits of the activity under these conditions. finally, reference must be made to the need to make significant progress in the defence of human public health, as well as in increasing the funding of the programme, as is done in the amendments we have voted for.
- mr president-in-office of the council, commissioner lamy, on the eve of the third united nations conference on the least developed countries, which will take place at the european parliament in brussels in mid-may, we feel it is essential to discuss and state the strategy to be adopted by the european union in this connection and on this occasion. the oral questions to the council and the commission which we are putting to you today have exactly that purpose. and with the motion for a resolution we have tabled we also intend to make a contribution in this direction. we are all aware - i hope - of the dramatic situation in which these countries find themselves. gdp per head is 100 times lower than in the developed countries, real growth is just above zero if we take into account population growth, the fall in commodity prices by about 30% in 1998-99, life expectancy is no more than 51 years, the illiteracy rate is about 50% and the roll could go on indefinitely. and the worst part is, you can't see the light at the end of the tunnel. because many of these countries, about a third of them, are still plagued by endless conflicts that exhaust all available and unavailable resources. we therefore believe that this conference cannot be merely a repetition of the diagnosis so often made, nor a mere indication, more or less exhaustive, of intentions which have been systematically not fulfilled. concrete measures are essential in the context of a global strategy, which is crucial to a new international economic order, and which is aimed at implementing and strengthening the 1986 united nations declaration which recognises the right to sustainable development as an undeniable human right. how essential it is that the european union and each of its member states should take a clear position in this respect, by striving to reverse the current trend towards a reduction in financial aid from the industrialized countries and to set a timetable for its increase by 0.7% of gdp, by acting in such a way that the commitments of rio de janeiro in 1992 are reiterated, by contributing to the definitive cancellation of the debt that clearly suffocates these countries, by making themselves available and acting at the level of conflict prevention, paying particular attention to combating arms trafficking. and finally, to abandon the perspective of focusing everything on the liberalisation of markets and to work towards a fundamental change in the wto rules and provisions which penalise these countries. in this connection it is essential to bear in mind that only 12 of the least developed countries are represented in geneva. and then, as we said in the motion for a resolution, it is necessary to take account of the fact that market access is not in itself a sufficient condition for ensuring economic growth and that economic growth does not in itself automatically lead to equitable development. it is therefore just as or more important than access to markets for these countries that their self-sufficiency and food security must also be ensured. hence their right to the protection of their production and of their rural population. and in this context, a few words on the initiative 'everything but arms' which we shall have the opportunity to discuss in depth at one of the next partsession, even if the council has taken the unacceptable decision not to hear this parliament. (close§) to emphasize that this is a positive initiative, although it is limited in scope and has aspects which need to be limited. and since this is only a first step, as has been said, it must be supplemented by other measures of a different nature, such as those we have been indicating. finally, at a time when so much emphasis is being placed on the need to combat poverty, i think it is necessary to emphasize two specific objectives which it will be important to reiterate: education and health. as we have said in our motion for a resolution, it is essential that the industrialized countries make a decisive contribution to the continuation of programmes in these areas. and in particular, as we recently agreed in this plenary, it is essential to act to ensure that the right of the countries most affected by aids and other epidemics to manufacture and market the necessary medicines, or to import them without the cost of intellectual property, is recognised. it is on these and other points, many of which are dealt with in more detail in the resolution we are presenting, that we now await the council's and the commission's comments.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the council has made considerable progress from the first to the second reading, but it is still not enough. if the european parliament remains firm on the essentials, the council will, i think, eventually evolve on the rest. the continuing violation of the sphere of professional secrecy of lawyers and the like, which is inviolable for the authorities, is unacceptable. the council, on the other hand, being tormented by this obsession with penetrating the restricted field of professional secrecy, continues to move away from what should be the fundamental axis of the revision of the directive, namely the priority fight against international organized crime, which is not given the full importance it deserves. the professional secrecy of lawyers and the like is not a minor issue, it is a fundamental issue of culture and civilization. it is absolutely intolerable in a state of law worthy of the name that the lawyer be turned into a whistleblower by force of law. it is surprising that the council, at a time when so many fundamental rights are being respected, should hesitate on this issue and allow itself to offend against a basic guarantee of the citizens. the new proposal is less bad than the previous one, but it is not enough. the court of justice has already ruled that the right to confidentiality is inviolable, not only in relation to proceedings before the court but also in relation to legal advice. there is no privilege for lawyers, who can and should be prosecuted when they directly carry out criminal activities or are suspected of doing so. what they can't do is have lawyers be hounded by clients who come to them, much less turned into hounders by clients who come to them. the proposal also contains other minor pitfalls, such as the possibility for member states to extend the scope of lawyers' work to other areas, which makes the council's text particularly dangerous and perverse. what the directive has to do is to guarantee the protection of professional secrecy, which is not a guarantee for lawyers but for society, the rule of law and citizens. and you have to separate what is advocacy in the proper sense, including legal advice, from other acts in which the lawyer may act but in which materially the action is not advocacy in the proper sense. there, the secrecy is inviolable, here the lawyer can always be prosecuted when he misbehaves, just like any other citizen.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, as you know, this issue is also of particular concern to portugal and portuguese fishermen, particularly in the sesimbra and algarve areas. and we were somewhat surprised, after the last debate we had here and where we received information that an agreement was possible, by the sudden news that everything had failed. we understand the difficulty of the problem and are shocked by the intransigence of morocco's position. and so the questions we raise are the following: the difference in financial compensation between the union's proposal and that of morocco is, according to the information we have, more than offset by the amount of community aid granted to morocco under various headings, and also by trade compensation or trade counterpart under other agreements. the question we are asking is whether the commission is in a position to devise a comprehensive strategy with morocco. we understand that a country decides to sell its resources more expensive, it has the political freedom to do so, but also that to the extent that it does so it has less need to resort to the help of international cooperation ... and we would like to recall in this connection what was said in the resolution adopted in 1999 by this european parliament: the european union and morocco are working together to develop a common fisheries policy. on the other hand, we would also like to know what measures are in place to support the conversion of the fishing fleet.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, there are three points to be made about this failed negotiation: the first is about morocco's extreme negotiating inflexibility. understandably, morocco wants to exploit its fisheries resources, but the lives of the people and their economic relations are made up of compromises. the european union has made commitments to morocco, opening its markets to many of its products, including agricultural products, and opening its doors to the many millions of moroccan citizens who have made the european union their home. the lack of reciprocity on the part of morocco is therefore incomprehensible. i hope that the european union will draw some conclusions from this history, both as regards future cooperation with morocco and its own common fisheries policy. the second is that the commission's, and especially commissioner fischler's, commitment was in contrast to the passive attitude of the council and the various presidencies of the european union, including my own country. is it because the agreement served only the interests of two countries directly? or have the governments of the two countries not been forceful enough to defend their interests? these are questions that require an answer. thirdly, it is important to prepare for the future, but the future is not prepared only by financial compensation, by inactive fishermen or by compensation for the scrapping of boats. this failure of the negotiations should be a matter for reflection for the european union on the future of the common fisheries policy. there are three main ways of doing this: the first is the need to create new political and financial instruments to facilitate new forms of cooperation and business partnership with countries rich in fisheries resources. the second is that there should be a serious political commitment to the forthcoming agreements with third countries; the third is that there should be a real revolution in the management of fisheries resources. finally, i must say that this refusal by morocco is a mystery to me. is it not possible to know the underlying reasons for this refusal? and if that refusal can be explained away, then why can we not think about a possible new negotiating mandate?

- mr president, in the light of the statements made by commissioner franz fischler, it seems that a process which had been dragging on since november 1999 and which had negative socioeconomic consequences for the community fleet operating there and for the fishing communities which traditionally depended on these fishermen has now come to an end. and the question i leave you with is this: is it still possible to start negotiations on a new fisheries agreement with morocco under acceptable conditions? the moroccan proposals to rewrite the old agreement were unacceptable, and even in the sector there were those who questioned whether a bad agreement was worth having. in any event, with this situation, some 30 vessels and more than 600 fishermen in portugal now have no future in sight. despite some extension of aid, it is imperative that the european union provide compensatory support for the fleet, the fishermen affected and the areas affected, by supporting a programme of conversion of the fleet operating in moroccan fisheries, ensuring its reorientation to other fisheries covered by the community fisheries agreements and, of course, considering a change in the european fisheries policy.

in writing. - (pt) the position adopted in the report represents a step backwards from the previous position. the fight against organised crime is only part of the problem. it is also important to adopt concrete measures with regard to the criminalisation of the economy which is precisely the result of money laundering. it is also significant that there are no concrete measures on the issue of offshore companies, the breach of banking secrecy during the judicial investigation or on the assets resulting from the criminal act of money laundering, in order to prevent the use of financial funds resulting from such an act during the investigation phase. finally, it should be noted that there is a phenomenon of mystification around consultants and advisers, which leads to traps and opportunities for information leakage.
in writing. - (pt) it is important, as the rapporteur points out, that the manufacturer of compound feedingstuffs or the person responsible for labelling provide a detailed list of all the raw materials used, and it is also essential that there is a binding definition of the raw materials allowed in compound feedingstuffs, in view of the bse and dioxin crisis. it is also necessary to ensure that all materials presenting a risk to human and animal health are prohibited. however, this report was placed in the context of a joint discussion on animal feed, in which the commission made a statement on control alternatives in the field of the promotion of protein crops in the eu, in line with the decisions taken at the agriculture council in december 2000, which prohibited the use of animal flour in the feed of farmed animals and stressed the need to replace animal protein with vegetable protein. the commission proposal is based mainly on budgetary implications, forgetting the basic principle of food sovereignty, when it argues that the best and cheapest solution is to increase eu imports of soya by 5%, which would worsen the eu's dependence on plant proteins, and more specifically on soya meal, and could jeopardise food security, with imports of genetically modified soya from the united states. in the name of costs and wto rules, the commission refuses to increase aid for oilseed production, because it goes against the blair house agreement, just as it refuses to support the use of set-aside land, because it is in contradiction with the world trade organisation's blue box, which requires that agricultural subsidies be conditional on measures to limit production. in doing so, the commission refuses to encourage internal production of this unacceptable position deserves our rejection, even if we support the report for which we voted.
in writing. - (pt) we welcome the proposals put forward by the rapporteur amending the proposal for a council decision on a financial contribution by the eu to certain expenditure by the member states, in particular for the implementation of the control, inspection and surveillance systems applicable to the common fisheries policy. since control is important to ensure responsible, sustainable and fair fishing, the community should finance the costs involved and the necessary investments, taking into account that it is the responsibility of each member state to implement the scheme in its exclusive economic zone. the council proposal maintains the continuity of the co-financing scheme, but limits the timeframe, the financial participation and the type of actions financed. the rapporteur proposes to increase community co-financing from 30% to 35% in training and investment actions, with 100% financing of eligible expenditure by member states for control and inspection actions in the context of eu participation in regional fisheries organisations. secondly, we consider it unacceptable that the proposal for a decision also aims to achieve budgetary savings in this area. the budget for these actions is increased from €41 million per year to €35 million per year, which is contrary to the creation of an adequate infrastructure to support control and inspection programmes which may entail an increase in the current financial burden.
in writing. - (pt) the rapporteur improves the commission's original proposal as much as possible and takes account of the emergency procedure, which was part of the set of seven measures to combat the crisis in the beef market. the commission's proposal, which was specific and limited, was intended to provide the possibility for organic farms only to produce pulses on land set aside under the wto. taking into account the community's deficit in vegetable protein, the limitations of the blair house agreement and the ban on the use of animal flour in the feeding of farmed animals, the creation of a genuine internal system of support for the production of fodder legumes and protein crops is necessary. the rapporteur's proposal, which we support, aims to give the same possibility to conventional farms and to farms under an agri-environmental programme with a view to a large complex of fodder legumes, bearing in mind the importance, as we proposed in the case of portugal, of beans, lupine and yellow lupine. however, this proposal lacks a genuine system of incentives for the production of pulses and protein crops, which could be implemented either through aid in the field of set-aside or their partial link to the production of fodder pulses, or through a modulation of the aid for freezing land when producing fodder pulses. it would be important for the commission to present concrete proposals in this respect, without being held captive by budgetary constraints and the world trade organisation.
in writing. - (pt) the foot-and-mouth disease epidemic, which follows the mad cow crisis, is only exacerbating the crisis in the livestock production sector. it is a penalty for farmers and further undermines public confidence in the safety of agricultural products. the liberalisation of agricultural trade and the free movement of goods have increased the risk and spread of animal diseases, with a cap that, in the name of competitiveness and a steady fall in prices, has promoted concentration, intensification and centralisation of production. in the present context, the producers are the most affected, without the necessary compensatory aid or adequate measures being implemented, in this case to curb the foot-and-mouth disease epidemic. this is why we have proposed the creation of a specific budget line in the eu budget to support farmers, as well as measures to eradicate animal diseases in the face of the food, health and veterinary crisis. the spread of the disease justifies the temporary suspension of movements of animals between member states and the strengthening of controls on marketed meat, and it is regrettable that no decision was taken to this effect at the last agriculture council. but beyond short-term measures, we need another agricultural policy, one which protects production and supports changes in production methods. a policy which promotes food quality and safety by respecting the precautionary principle. sustainable agriculture, which promotes regional specificities and diversification of support to agricultural producers, taking into account different levels of agricultural development. agriculture contributing to economic and social cohesion and based largely on family farming and small and medium-sized farms.
in writing. - (pt) despite the devaluation of the results of the stockholm summit by much of the media, progress has been made, reflecting the objectives of the economic and financial groups that dominate the european economy, as reflected in the commission's proposal. the latter has already charted the course. just like the story of the half-full bottle of water, some will say it is "half-full", others will say it is "half-empty". the lisbon strategy of accelerating liberalisation, more specifically through the privatisation of transport, postal services, electricity, gas and telecommunications, was nevertheless consolidated at the stockholm council. on the other hand, the process of financial market integration has accelerated. the question of full employment does not necessarily mean an increase in the number of quality jobs, especially if this increase is accompanied by greater job insecurity. with regard to the so-called 'modernisation of the european social model', the burden on the pension system and the adaptation of social security has been reasserted, with the method of coordination having been opened in this area and this objective included in the eu's annual economic guidelines. it must be stressed, however, that the meagre victories of the lisbon strategy in the social field are due to the struggle of workers and all progressives who demand an economy more oriented towards people's problems and towards sustainable development, as is the case with us.
in writing. - (pt) in this brief statement of vote, which is obligatory, i would like to comment on some aspects of the resolution on the question of 'foreign relations' which was discussed at the stockholm summit and which was adopted today and which i voted against. firstly, as regards the balkans, i cannot help criticising the unacceptable and blatant interference and blackmailing of the federal republic of yugoslavia by the united states, while attempts are being made to whitewash and cover up the responsibility of nato and all those who have waged war against yugoslavia, in flagrant violation of the un charter and in contempt of the security council, by using depleted uranium weapons whose chemical and radiological effects on the population and the environment are still difficult to assess. the current situation in the balkans has complex and deep-rooted causes, but we must highlight the role of the us and eu interference, which, in a significant and sometimes decisive way, have contributed to the deterioration of a process whose consequences for the whole region are far from being forgotten, which is why a strategy of selective and arbitrary criminalisation by certain political leaders is unacceptable. secondly, as regards the very serious situation in palestine, we consider that the provisions of the resolution are very inadequate. the latter do not clearly and explicitly condemn the escalation of israeli aggression, the bombing and repression of the palestinian population by the israeli army, and the tightening of the blockade of the palestinian territories. thirdly, we cannot but be very concerned at the lack of criticism of the current militarisation of the eu, whose common defence policy is the instrument, as the european pillar of nato, and of the us project for a national missile defence system (nmd) whose implementation would help to relaunch the arms race and intensify global insecurity.
in writing. - (pt) although not surprising, the decision to reject the kyoto protocol recently announced by the united states is extremely serious. this is not surprising because the united states, after signing the kyoto protocol in 1997, has since 1998 used various pretexts to block negotiations at the various un conferences to establish the procedures for implementing the protocol. this decision is serious because it shows that national egoism has prevailed. it is unacceptable that, in the face of increasingly dramatic forecasts of the effect of global warming, the main contributor to greenhouse gas emissions should remain outside the only international instrument designed to limit these effects; but, however serious this decision may be, we cannot doubt or hesitate about the need to implement the kyoto protocol, with or without the united states. the need to curb climate change is urgent, and the kyoto protocol (rather than any other type of agreement) is the most appropriate policy instrument to achieve this. the non-participation of the united states reduces the scope of the protocol but does not remove its purpose. the non-participation of the united states makes its implementation more difficult but not impossible. the united states' position and the approach of cop6bis, the conference to approve the modalities of the kyoto protocol, obliges the european union to redouble its efforts and assume effective leadership on this political issue. the eu is also working to improve the quality of its energy supply, both internally by combating the very negative increase in greenhouse gas emissions in all member states, and externally by negotiating the terms of application of the protocol with as many countries as possible so that it can be ratified and implemented in 2002. but the european union must also fight for the application of fair trade rules so that the united states does not derive illegitimate economic benefits from non-ratification of the protocol. for this reason, the european union must now, rather than trying to maintain a deaf dialogue with the united states, implement an action plan to implement the provisions of the kyoto protocol at the global level - that is, with as many countries as possible - and urgently by the end of 2002. an action plan which involves the achievement of all the following programmed objectives: (i) negotiating, even before cop21, the modalities of the kyoto protocol with russia, japan, australia, canada and the countries of central and eastern europe, so as to reach an agreement which can be ratified by more than 55 parties, corresponding to more than 55% of emissions (minimum limit for the entry into force of the kyoto protocol); (ii) the ratification of the kyoto protocol by the european union by the end of 2001; (iii) approval of national plans for the reduction of greenhouse gases by the end of 2001; (iv) the presentation of the directive on the implementation of the european emissions trading system by the end of 2001; (v) the presentation, within the framework of the european climate change programme, of a set of armored guidelines on policies and measures to reduce greenhouse gases in industry, energy, services, agriculture and, above all, in the transport sector - the sector with the fastest growing emissions; (vi) the european commission's preparation of an economic study on the competitive advantages that american products, and more specifically energy products, will illegally obtain over european products, as a result of the non-application of the kyoto protocol; (vii) the european commission's preparation of a legal study determining how to minimise, within the framework of supranational institutions and in particular the world trade organisation and the transatlantic economic partnership, these illegitimate advantages mentioned in the previous point.
- mr president, i would like to support the positions of our colleague jorge moreira da silva, who led our delegation to the hague conference on climate change and was very committed to the compromise that was fortunately reached on this resolution. four short points. firstly, the firm denunciation of the unfortunate and reprehensible unilateral decision announced by president bush to reject the kyoto protocol. secondly, it is not surprising that this is so serious; the european union should have been wary of alternative strategies a long time ago. thirdly, we must not waver or hesitate about the need to implement the kyoto protocol, with or without the united states. fourth and lastly, the european union must do more and take effective leadership, both internally in combating the emissions slide which is now taking place in most of our 15 member states and externally in ensuring that kyoto is ratified by as many countries as possible so that it can enter into force before 2002.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, more than a year ago, on 16 february, the european parliament discussed the problem of counterfeiting the euro, at the time from the point of view of the common criminal framework. it was then that our colleague mrs cederschiöld, with her competence and rigor, drew up the report and introduced our debate. as charlotte cederschiöld used to say: "we cannot allow counterfeiting to weaken our common currency". that statement still stands a year later and it is the essential question: the security of our currency. we have all spoken here about the importance of the euro for our union, its economic importance as a complement to the achievement of a genuine economic area without internal frontiers, its importance as a mechanism that has contributed to budgetary discipline and the transparency of the public accounts of the member states, and also its political value as an emanation of the european construction that we are developing, advancing and consolidating day by day. so, for all these reasons, the security of our common currency is very important. we also know that the security of the currency is also linked to its stability and exchange value, but what we appreciate today is the aspect of protection against counterfeiting. but if all is not well on this other side, we now have all the more reason not to neglect this fight and to strengthen our precautions. in her report, charlotte cederschiöld rightly stresses the global importance of the new currency and the inevitable fact that it will arouse criminal appetites, stating that 'the euro is in open competition with the dollar as the most counterfeitable currency'. i am sure that my colleague charlotte cederschiöld will agree with me that this is not the race we want to win against the dollar. we are aware of the particular fragility of the transitional period, during which the new european currency and the old national currencies will coexist. the lack of knowledge, the lack of familiarity and the obligation on banks to promote currency exchange will encourage the counterfeiting of both the euro and national currencies. we must also fight against mockery and fraud, and we must do more and better in the field of information, especially in my country, portugal, where surveys show worrying levels of ignorance. examples of the pedagogical contribution of catholic priests, especially in the interior of the country, have come to light: the contribution of the church and the local and regional press should be encouraged and supported. i also agree with the approach taken by the commission in its communication and with the four elements identified: training, information system, cooperation - in particular with europol, olaf and the european central bank - and criminal protection. in conclusion, i would also say that i am not in favour of reservations about the national sides of the coins: i think the increased risk is offset by the reinforcement of a sense of belonging and identification, and it also allows for a political response to those who are always quick to criticize over-uniformity. charlotte cederschiöld, congratulations to you!
in writing. - (pt) in this report, the european parliament gives its opinion on the second update of the initial stability and convergence programmes submitted by the member states in 1999. the downward revisions in the rate of economic growth and the slowdown in the world economy, rather than leading to a change in the direction of the current economic trends, are leading to their reinforcement: further and accelerated fiscal consolidation, in particular the reduction of public expenditure and wage moderation in order to guarantee the objective of price stability, to which the european union continues to subject, which is unacceptable, all its economic trends and all its social policies. the rapporteur is following this philosophy closely, which is in line with the lisbon strategy, in particular the objective of making europe the most dynamic and competitive economy in the world within a decade. it is not surprising, therefore, that the report calls for wage moderation and links the establishment of 'compatible wage policies' with tax cuts, endorses 'structural changes to increase flexibility and competitiveness in all factor, product and labour markets' and considers that 'structural reforms and liberalisation can make a decisive contribution' to the lisbon objective. for all these reasons, we voted against this report. however, one positive element must be emphasized, namely the rapporteur's emphasis on investment, in particular on the importance of public investment, which the commission had completely forgotten, as a determining factor in the sustainability of economic growth and job creation.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budget is properly managed. - i know . mr president, commissioner, the committee on budgets has attached the greatest importance to this matter, particularly to the e-learning initiative, because of the contribution it can make to citizens, through appropriate training, to better living and working conditions in the new information society. i would remind you that in the context of the general guidelines for 2002, which were adopted on 3 april last and which are an important instrument of budgetary policy, the e-learning initiative is expressly considered as an important step towards the strategic objective set by the lisbon european council of ensuring sustainable economic growth based on knowledge, with more and better jobs and greater social cohesion. in this context, the commission is asked to examine the possibility of defining a more appropriate legal framework which would effectively complement the action of the member states in this field. the opinion of the committee on budgets annexed to the mauro report is now in this direction. it is reiterated that such a major initiative justifies the creation of a specific programme with its own legal basis. it is therefore not appropriate to use existing programmes such as leonardo, socrates, culture 2000, research programmes and the structural funds as sources of funding for the new initiative, which should also be a stand-alone funding instrument. at the same time, we are following with the greatest interest the implementation of the preparatory action in this area in this year's budget, as from 2001. i would like to end by congratulating mr mauro on his excellent work.

in writing. - (pt) the subject of new technologies and how to approach them in education is of course of the greatest interest and importance. but as you know, it is not enough to equip every school with a computer to solve the problem. it is not only essential to make the use of appropriate technological infrastructures in schools viable, both in terms of curriculum and equipment level, but also to pay the utmost attention to teacher training and to create the conditions for every student to have access to and use these technologies. it is therefore important to emphasise that this commission communication is very diffuse and that it is in practice dependent on other existing programmes, which pursue their own objectives, since this initiative is limited to proposing to monitor progress in each member state, without envisaging actions that could actually change the current situation and influence the future widespread use of the internet. however, for a country like portugal, if the cost of access to new technologies is not fundamentally changed and if the standard of living of the poorest families is not significantly improved, a large percentage of children and young people will not benefit from equal opportunities in access to new technologies.
