- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in recent years much attention has been devoted to the problem of drug addiction in relation to drug use. but, paradoxically, little or nothing has been done about alcohol abuse and alcohol dependence! the paradox is also that reality is far from what is usually believed. alcohol dependence is a much broader and deeper social and public health problem, with statistics in many countries revealing that about 10 percent of the population can be considered alcoholics. alcoholism is one of the silent dramas of our day - it is as if society were repeating that denial which is one of the most striking and characteristic features of alcoholism and other drug addictions and, as is well known, the most serious obstacle to the recovery of the sick. the report and this recommendation are in opposition to this past of indifference and therefore deserve our support and our congratulations. this particular attention to children and adolescents is right. on the one hand, it is known that alcohol consumption has increased sharply among the younger population, with cases of juvenile alcoholism, which were virtually unknown or very rare a few decades ago, even now appearing frequently. on the other hand, because the health information and education efforts directed at young people will also have long-term beneficial effects on the general prevention of alcoholism. many of today's older alcoholics come from generations who didn't know, who thought it was okay because it was always like that. now, we must make the younger people aware of this. to inform, prevent, control, anticipate and avert human and social disaster. today, moreover, we are faced with new challenges in a situation where the market is flooded with products that are suggested as harmless, such as alcoholic soft drinks or synthetic drinks that are aimed predominantly at the youngest, which can be the initiation of abuse and therefore the door to the abyss and whose dangers must thus be exposed. we hope that this recommendation will be successful and that it will be followed up in all the member states.
- the aim of the present proposal from the european commission is to compensate beef producers for the major imbalances which have been experienced by the market in recent months as a result of successive crises affecting the sector. i understand the reasons for this proposal and especially its timing, but i consider it to be highly inadequate, and i would stress the following points: firstly, i consider it unacceptable that the special premium for male bovine animals should be reduced, even temporarily, by penalizing member states which are still net importers of meat, such as my own country, in order to reduce production. secondly, the creation of the special intervention buying-in system for meat from animals which have been traced for more than 30 months is not feasible either from the point of view of consumer confidence or of cost, since in a few months' time there will certainly still be no possibility of disposing of the meat which has now been stored. it would be wiser to buy these animals for meat destruction. thirdly, the promotion of extensive production makes sense, but it should be accompanied by a special premium supplement for small farms which, because of their limited land, cannot benefit from these incentives. once again, the same recipe is insisted on for all, as if all farms had the same capacity to adapt and survive. finally, the authorization of the use of fallow land for forage in the case of organic producers - the subject of our colleague mrs auroi's report - can only be seen as symbolic, at a time when producers are faced with the increase in the cost of feed for their animals. why are farmers not allowed to use fallow land for protein production?
- the previous fisheries protocol between the european union and greenland served mainly germany, which received about 80% of the quota, although on average it used about 12%. the new protocol guarantees fewer fishing opportunities on the grounds that it approximates the reality of the actual catch. however, the fundamental question of guaranteeing fishing opportunities to fleets of other member states capable of taking advantage of the fishing opportunities provided for remains unresolved, preventing a better matching of the cost of the protocol to its economic and social benefit and disregarding the conclusions of the council of fisheries ministers of october 1997 on fisheries agreements and under-utilisation of quotas. hence the amendment we have tabled to ensure that all member states have access to the fishing opportunities offered. the current situation is particularly unfair to fleets such as the portuguese fleet, which has been fishing in the area since the 1930s and was excluded from the protocol when portugal joined the european union in 1986. the existence of a small quota for portugal could reduce the 12 000 tonnes of deficit on the farm, which would help to make the remainder of its long-distance fishing fleet viable. of the 52 factory ships in existence in 1990, only 14 remain.
- commissioner, we are looking at the second largest fisheries agreement between the european union and a third party. in this fisheries agreement, which is in fact of such important dimensions, the first question to be raised is that there is a confusion - which does not seem to me to be acceptable - between what is a fisheries agreement and what is support for the development of a region which, being part of a member state of the european union, has decided not to be part of the european union. we have nothing against this development support, we think it is perfectly justified, but we do not understand why this agreement should be disguised as the development of a fisheries agreement. the fisheries agreement which echoes and claims the principle of relative stability based on historical catches, but which ignores historical reality! portugal will shortly be celebrating 500 years of fishing in newfoundland. in greenland, fishing is a little bit more recent, in fact, it's not 500 years old. it is therefore difficult to find a more historic fishery than portugal's in greenland's seas. and to say that portugal has no place in this fisheries agreement is something that we cannot accept, it is something that is completely contrary to reality, and we cannot support this interpretation of the principle of relative stability.
- commissioner, as the rapporteur has pointed out, the experience gained with the previous fisheries agreement with greenland is very disappointing, with very low catch percentages compared to the quotas initially set. although it did not fish, the european union nevertheless paid an annual average of eur 38 million in due time during that period of the previous agreement. what is most astonishing about this third agreement is that it reduces fishing opportunities for the period in question, but increases the financial compensation from the european union, which rises to 43 million euros per year. in other words, you pay more to fish much less. why, then, has the european union decided to sign a fisheries agreement despite the negative experience of the last agreement? our rapporteur highlights two main reasons: the first is the importance of this agreement for the conclusion of other agreements in the region, in particular with norway and iceland. the second is that it is vital for the fleets of the countries fishing there. for these reasons, and despite all the inconsistencies, the rapporteur proposes that we agree. i understand those reasons and that is why i did not oppose the rapporteur's proposal, although i approved several amendments by several colleagues. it is important, however, to note that, according to the two main arguments put forward by the rapporteur in support of this agreement, other fisheries agreements should follow, which is not the case, as we know, with the one in morocco, although it is essential for the fleets of two member states fishing there and is a key signal for concluding other fisheries agreements in the region, such as with mauritania and other countries south of mauritania. there is no doubt that we are witnessing an incomprehensible policy of double standards, and i hope that the governments of the member states concerned - portugal and spain - will take due account of what has happened and what is happening with this greenland agreement in order to make a similar procedure prevail in the council.
the rapporteur. - commissioner, i should like to begin by congratulating myself on the inclusion of this report in today's sitting, at the very moment when the third united nations conference on the least developed countries is taking place in brussels, at the premises of the european parliament. this is a good opportunity to discuss this measure specifically aimed at these countries, but also to reflect once again on the problems they face and the ways of overcoming them, although we have already had the opportunity to consider and adopt a general proposal on this subject. however, i cannot but regret, and i deeply regret, that the council has decided not to involve the european parliament in the definition and evaluation of this measure, and i also regret that it is not here with us today. this is a position which we consider intolerable and which we hope will not become a precedent. moreover, since this measure is aimed at a group of 49 countries, the vast majority of which are acp, it would be desirable if its definition had also taken care to ensure that our partners were effectively heard, even if, or even because, the main recipients of these measures of exemption from customs duties for products originating in the least-developed countries are essentially those who are not members of the acp group. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and because more than 99% of the community's trade with these countries already benefits from total exemption from customs duties. in a positive sense, because we want to understand it as a first step, but only a first step, in a deeper and more global movement which presents itself to us as indispensable, possible and oriented towards responding to the many difficulties facing the poorest countries of our planet. and not just on a commercial level. i think it would be extremely unfortunate if we were to assume that the problems of these countries will be solved by measures which are mainly geared to what is sometimes called, in a simplistic way, 'integration into the process of globalisation'. it is necessary to take into account that, rather than being a solution to the problems of these countries, this process of liberalization has ended up becoming a real problem for them, such are the weaknesses of their economies and the gap that separates them from those of the industrialized countries. it should not be forgotten, however, that just as important or even more important than market access for these countries is their ability to feed populations who often lack access to the most basic means of food subsistence, which should put their self-sufficiency, food security and, in particular, the subsistence of their family farms, at the forefront of their concerns. in addition, and still in the field of trade, other aspects than the facilitation of exports provided for in this measure will be important to consider: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in substance, the final version of the measure, as adopted by the council, and beyond the general assessment which we deserve in the terms i have just mentioned, nevertheless raises some special considerations. the first concerns the transitional periods finally adopted for sugar, rice and bananas, but we are fully aware of the difficulties which this raises and therefore i do not think that this matter should be the subject of any further controversy. secondly, i believe that it is necessary to ensure that the implementation of the measure is properly monitored in order to ensure that the ldcs and their populations are the final and definitive beneficiaries of the measure, and that the implementation of the measure does not result in a disadvantage to the acp countries. in my view, an appropriate and even broad quota of the exports covered could facilitate the effectiveness and discipline in its application. i would conclude by saying that in any case we consider it essential and indispensable to monitor the actual impact of the measure.
- we must address the issue of transatlantic relations between the european union and the united states in the context of a strategic redefinition by the us administration. it is natural that the elections in that country have brought to power a new president and a new administration, and it is our duty to be attentive to the signals that come to us from the other side of the atlantic. the fundamental feature which in our view differentiates the present administration from the previous one in terms of foreign policy is a return to the priority of consolidating and developing systems of alliances with all those who show a willingness to act in harmony with the americans, replacing the model which consisted of acting on the basis of a multilateralism based on a process of permanent negotiation, with a view to creating and defining rules, norms and multilateral and universal values inspired, of course, by the american institutions. it is not appropriate at this stage to analyse the practical military, diplomatic and economic consequences of this return to more traditional geopolitical conceptions on the part of the united states. but let us remember that the concept of democratic administration induced a voluntary activism which resulted in a greater and greater responsibility and involvement in the details of almost daily crisis management anywhere in the world, a concept which seems to us to be being abandoned. the revision of this interventionist approach leads us to agree with the commission and the strategic direction of its document 'a new impetus for relations between the united states and the european union'. the european union must now take advantage of the new american administration to review the way in which transatlantic relations are managed and set strict priorities so that the new transatlantic agenda is more productive and constructive in terms of concrete results. like the commission, we also believe that the leaders of the union should agree on a more concise number of strategic dossiers. they must also ensure a high level of political commitment to avoid the rationing process becoming too bureaucratic and burdensome. how often does the mere holding of frequent meetings and summits with heavy work orders lead only to the dilution of interests and the lack of clear vision and objectives. let us therefore look to renewed transatlantic cooperation, in which the european union and the united states can effectively engage, together and with mutual benefit, in the various global challenges in which they are inevitably involved.
in writing. - (pt) the world conference against racism, xenophobia, antisemitism and intolerance can and must be the unique opportunity to carry out a thorough analysis of these worrying phenomena and to adopt guidelines capable of putting an end to them. however, it should not be overlooked that many of the intolerant attitudes observed in our societies are induced by feelings of insecurity arising from situations resulting from the mass dismissals of thousands of workers, the increase in job insecurity and the loss of rights. the european union and its member states are therefore cutting budgets for cooperation with the developing countries and, in general, there is no support for their effective development. it is therefore easy to understand that for many men and women in these countries, the only way of survival is to emigrate to our 'rich' countries. the continuing creation of obstacles to the movement of immigrant workers within the european union (a situation which can now be modified to some extent to attract the specialized technicians we lack) and the creation of systematic difficulties for the family reunification of these citizens certainly do not contribute to their integration. we shall vote in favour of this resolution, but we believe that, beyond the expression of politically correct intentions, action is necessary. the european union must set an example in this respect.
in writing. - (pt) the council's common position adopted on 12 february 2001 takes up 21 of the 30 amendments tabled by the european parliament, but it omits the important questions which the rapporteur is trying to bring to the european parliament's second reading and for which we voted. first of all, the introduction of the precautionary principle in the marketing of products and in the measures to be taken in risk management. this is a general principle used by public administrations, which should be explicitly mentioned in the member states' powers and obligations. the council's common position also abandons the notion of a total ban on exports of products presenting serious risks, although it clarifies the concept of partial or non-ban without, however, introducing any selection criteria. it is therefore necessary to return to the original text of the european commission. finally, another important issue concerns safety in the service sector. as the rapporteur says, the commission must come forward with proposals on the safety of services. the commission should identify the needs and priorities for community action in this area, as well as the responsibilities of service providers, taking into account the clarifications in the common position concerning the scope of this directive.
in writing. - (pt) we know that alcohol consumption is high, with all the negative consequences that this implies, especially when it comes to young people. as a recent survey of 15 to 16-year-olds showed, ireland, the united kingdom, and denmark have the highest rates of binge drinking in the european union. these last two countries, along with finland, france and sweden, have the highest percentages of young people aged 13 and under who are already drinking. studies show that there is a strong link between drinking alcohol and antisocial behavior, such as violence, especially among young people. immoderate drinking has been linked to unwanted pregnancies, sexually transmitted diseases, crime, and traffic accidents. in addition, drinking habits acquired during youth can also be detrimental to health and lead to long-term drinking patterns, so that this is an issue not only for health promotion, education and information policies, but also for measures to limit alcohol consumption among children and adolescents, as well as for structural actions in the field of treatment and socialisation. the commission proposal is very superficial and assumes that the entities concerned with the marketing of alcohol will work together to prevent actual consumption by children and adolescents, which is rather utopian, as we know, although the rapporteur goes a little further in the proposals presented, seeking to impose certain rules and focusing on more specific codes of conduct. member states need to pay more attention to this problem.
in writing. - (pt) the beef sector was severely penalised during the last reform of the cap, as the price reduction was not fully offset by direct aids, which only compensate for 30 to 50% of the fall in producer prices. in addition, the bse crisis coupled with the foot-and-mouth disease crisis led to a decline in consumption and lower producer prices. portuguese exports of beef are also subject to an embargo. in this context of crisis, the commission has put forward a plan of 7 proposals to address the imbalances in the sector. but instead of a balanced and coherent proposal, the commission is presenting a dubious proposal whose sole purpose is to achieve budgetary savings. although it does not depart from the logic of the commission, the sturdy report contains some interesting ideas, for example on the discount for calves and the increase in aid. the commission's proposal to strictly respect the limit of 90 animals per holding and to create a future system of individual rights is positive, but it rejects the commission's proposals, which can not only combat intensification but also support the application of a modulation system of aid. in any event, if the present system is to be maintained, it is necessary to ensure that, in the event of overrunning of the duties, the proportional reduction of the premium will not affect producers with a herd of less than 15 head. the commission's proposal to reduce the density of livestock from 2.0 to 1.9 ugb per fodder area will lead to an annual penalty of ecu 300 million (9,000 premiums) in the case of portugal, which will have repercussions, especially on small and mediumsized family farms. the rapporteur goes even further than the commission in this matter by creating new premiums for major reductions in livestock density. for all these reasons, rather than voting for this report, we have opted for abstention.
in writing. - (pt) we voted for amendments nos 1, 10, 2 and 12, not because we agree with a productivist policy within the cap, which will have to be reviewed in the interests of producers, consumers, environmental protection and food quality, but because we believe that dubious restrictions, in this case on the number of male bovine animals benefiting from the special premium for beef, isolated from a comprehensive and articulated reform, are meaningless and create situations of injustice in relation to the premiums fixed for other types of agricultural and animal production, which remain unchanged.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to raise a preliminary question under rule 143 of the rules of procedure, namely whether the report and the rules of procedure, which are now to be discussed, are admissible for debate and vote. and i challenge the admissibility on a strictly legal basis. it is enough, moreover, to quote another report of the committee on constitutional affairs, which is under consideration and which will be before the house at the end of the month, on the treaty of nice, and where it reads, and i quote the english text: the committee on constitutional affairs welcomes the fact that the treaty incorporates the legal basis that will enable the statute for european political parties and rules governing their funding to be adopted under the codecision procedure. but this is not enough, even this is legally questionable. the treaties do not mention european political parties. the treaties speak of political parties at european level and give equal weight to all political parties in their involvement at european level. this is the clear text of the treaties in article 191 before and after nice, and it is also contained in declaration 11 annexed to the treaty. the adoption of a legal regime, and moreover of funding, for a small category of parties, the so-called 'european political parties' and those falling within their scope, would create a discrimination which is unacceptable in the light of fundamental rights and in the light of the treaties. this would not only be discrimination against certain political parties, but above all against citizens, since it is the voters, in accordance with the electoral laws and the way elections are conducted, who determine the weight and representativeness of the parties. i would therefore ask the house to vote against the report and the rules of procedure.
the rapporteur. - ladies and gentlemen, poverty remains a worrying problem in the european union, with new groups, such as immigrants, new threats of exclusion, of which i would stress the info-exclusion, because of the persistence of inequalities in access to new technologies, affecting tens of millions of people, especially women, children and the elderly, but increasingly including workers with precarious ties, atypical work and low wages. it is in this context that it is of particular importance to put on the agenda the definition of a global strategy to combat poverty and social exclusion on the basis of article 137 of the eu treaty. all the specific measures, local and national, are important, so it is essential to launch a european union strategy aimed at coordinating policies and efforts in the fight which must be a priority against this real scandal that we are living in a period of wealth creation and growth without, even today, more than 50 million citizens of the european union having access to fundamental rights enshrined in the social charters and the charter of fundamental rights itself recently proclaimed by the community institutions. however, as is well known, the nice council adopted common objectives to combat poverty and social exclusion, which must be reflected in a coordinated and integrated manner, in a multidimensional perspective, in the national action plans which the member states have undertaken to draw up by 1 june next. as is well known, the common objectives which should be reflected in the national action plans are: facilitating participation in employment and access for all to resources, rights and services; preventing the risks of exclusion; acting in favour of the most vulnerable; mobilising all the actors involved. the european council meeting in december this year, at the end of the belgian presidency, should also adopt common indicators on poverty and social exclusion to allow a comparison of the results expected by the member states in the implementation of the national action plans on social inclusion. it is in this context that the adoption and entry into force, with the greatest urgency, of the community action programme to encourage cooperation between member states in the fight against exclusion is of particular importance. until this programme comes into force, there are no mechanisms for coordination and monitoring at community level of the actions being carried out, which are fundamental aspects of the overall strategy which the council has undertaken to follow. it is not, therefore, to be understood as a refusal by the council to accept proposals put forward by the european parliament and, in particular, those already accepted by the commission. the council's common position of 12 february incorporates, in whole or in part, 25 of the 47 amendments which parliament adopted on 16 november last year, whereas the commission had accepted 31 amendments. the proposals which are now being taken up at second reading, and which the committee on employment and social affairs unanimously adopted on 25 april, therefore take up the positions which the commission accepted but which the council regrettably rejected, such as the establishment of comparable parameters in the context of national action plans and the financing of 90% of the activities carried out by the main european networks involved in the fight against poverty and social exclusion, a percentage of funding which has already been accepted, for example, in the programme to combat discrimination and which has been refused here, it is not clear why! on the other hand, we are returning to the central issue of funding the programme with 100 million euros and not just 70 million as the council and commission insist, although the council has already accepted proposals which involve increased expenditure such as the networking of national observatories and similar bodies to combat poverty, exchanges of experience, preparatory meetings of the annual european union round table on social exclusion with the participation of all the stakeholders, field visits to ensure the participation of the excluded. it also includes issues of comitology, in particular with regard to the committee, taking up the commission's initial proposal, the assessment of the impact of macroeconomic policies in terms of their possible effects on poverty and social exclusion and the financing of innovative projects. it is also worth mentioning the minor technical corrections which were introduced, after the presidency's services had called attention to them, in order to improve the legal quality and visibility of the text voted by the committee on employment and, at the same time, to allow our text to be kept in line with other texts voted by parliament, such as the minor technical amendments to amendments nos 10 and 2 and the new amendment no 18. as for amendment no 3 by our commission, and in view of the technical amendments already mentioned, i do not think it is necessary, but i will of course leave the matter to the house. as for the three amendments tabled by the ppe, i believe that they are purely procedural and that their adoption at this stage would make it more difficult to approve the programme. finally, mr president, i would like to thank all those who have contributed to the preparation of this report and i hope that the programme will be adopted quickly.
- the decisive step for the future of social protection in europe was taken at the lisbon council and laid down in the treaty of nice. the questions which are being put to the newly created committee are vast and require concrete answers in the terms very rightly put by the commission and by the particularly excellent report we are here to assess. i therefore ask the council to reform its mandate for this committee. it is not enough to calculate the financial effort to meet the obligations of the systems, which should already have been done in the framework of the stability pact, because, like employment, social protection is interdependent on macroeconomic policy decisions, plus the increase in the dependency of the elderly, plus the unpredictable increase in changes in the labour market, alternating periods of training with active and inactive periods. we must consider the cost to our societies of the absence of transfers from redistributive systems to citizens in terms of loss of political confidence in europe, social upheaval, increased insecurity and poverty, reduced consumption, economic recession, increased inequalities. this is a nuclear policy question which we must answer and which the council will have to answer shortly. capitalization must have defined boundaries and be part of the solution and not the problem. it must not constitute a barrier to mobility. it cannot because it does not solve the demographic problems and it is not solidarity-based, it has benefits and risks, it stimulates the financial markets, factors which must be weighed up, but it is not and cannot be part of the problem and would contribute to increasing the risk of social protection.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would first like to thank the commission, which, in the context of this report, has made every effort to be present, to answer the questions raised and to justify the choices made, as well as our committee on agriculture, the rapporteur for the opinion on this report, mr vincenzo lavarra, and the rapporteur for the olive oil sector, mr jové peres. a sincere thank you for the contributions you have made and for the attitude of frank collaboration you have maintained with me. a very special note is made of the excellent study on the environmental impact of the common organization of the market in olive oil, which was prepared for the commission's directorate-general for the environment by accredited agro-environmental analysis institutes and which i quote extensively in this speech. the olive oil cmo is based on supporting production, i.e. it stimulates productivity gains as a way of increasing farmers' incomes. the average yield of an olive grove varies between 200 and 500 kg/ha/year in traditional olive groves in the most disadvantaged areas and between 8 and 10 tonnes/ha/year in more intensive olive groves on better soils. with the potential to increase olive yields by a factor of 10 or even 20, a policy based on the promotion of quantity produced has necessarily encouraged the abandonment of traditional olive groves and the most difficult land and has promoted the intensification of production on more favourable land. traditional plantations have a low density of trees, are generally old - centuries-old trees are common - are associated with arable crops and/or sheep or goat pasture, are often planted on stilts and are very labour intensive. their heritage and landscape value in preserving floods and landslides or in increasing biodiversity is invaluable. the study we referred to counts 120 plant species, 70 vertebrate species and 160 invertebrate species in traditional olive groves in the province of córdoba, andalusia. the role of old olive groves and olive trees in the preservation of many bird species is especially important. the value of the production subsidy is too low here and the abandonment of the traditional olive grove is taking place throughout the mediterranean, with the inevitable negative consequences for the environment and rural development. modern intensive plantations have a high density of small trees that are uprooted after a few years of life, use abundant water for irrigation, fertilizers with levels as high as 350 kg. per hectare, pesticides and herbicides to strip the soil of vegetation. these plantations do not use terraces, allowing slopes that are often above 12%. the erosion caused by modern olive groves in andalusia is estimated to reach 80 tonnes per hectare, and the erosion process is considered very serious when it exceeds 50 tonnes per hectare. hence the approximately 1 million new intensive plantations in andalusia are causing 80 million tonnes of soil erosion per year, making it one of the main drivers of desertification in the south of the iberian peninsula. although less studied, the same problem affects large areas of the peloponnese, crete, the aegean islands, and tuscany. in areas such as liguria and many areas of portugal, erosion is caused more by the abandonment of traditional olive groves than by the installation of modern olive groves. this system of production-related aid not only encourages the human and environmental desertification of the south of our continent, but also facilitates the activities of olive oil adulterators who conspire against the community budget, against the consumer's right to buy a natural and quality product and against farmers. the level of fraud and irregularities in the olive oil sector exposed by this report of the court of auditors is too high. it is true that all the evidence points to the fact that fraud is found in olive oil more than in milk only because controls are more stringent and not because there is any greater propensity for fraud. the european institutions must make the fight against food adulteration in areas such as meat, milk, wine and olive oil a top political priority, which necessarily requires a revolution in the way this cap works. the preservation and improvement of the environment and farmers' incomes is the primary objective of the agricultural chapter of the treaty. however, what we are doing now benefits the agro-industrial complex more than agriculture, desertifies disadvantaged rural areas, has negative environmental impacts and facilitates counterfeiting. we must therefore abandon the old system of the cmos, which encouraged physical productivity, and create a new cap, which is focused on protecting farmers' incomes, the countryside, quality and the environment.
- following the latest 'reform' of the common organisation of the market in olive oil and the end of the transitional period for the commission to opt for a change in the support scheme from a production aid to a tree aid, the commission presents its strategy for olive oil quality and extends the current support scheme for two more marketing years. this extension is a positive step, but the commission has failed to address three points which were left unanswered in the 1998 reform: the creation of compensatory aid for small producers, as proposed by parliament; the prohibition of the marketing of olive oil mixtures with other fats and a labelling which is not misleading to the consumer and which reserves the name 'olive oil' for the olive product. the abolition of public intervention mechanisms and their replacement by private storage has not prevented the fall in prices and their consequences for the reduction of farmers' incomes. the report by mr salvador jové seeks to correct some of these essential issues, particularly as regards mixtures, which we support. as for the report by mr paulo casaca, he calls into question the resolutions of the european parliament by using an opinion on the report of the court of auditors to put forward proposals to replace the current system of production aid by direct aid to farming, which opens the door to the commission's option of the tree premium. this intention must be rejected. aid for the olive tree would not only have a negative impact on production and employment (with no olive harvesting), but would also lose a key element of control of olive oil production and quality, increasing the possibilities for fraudulent practices.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this is another contribution to a good atmosphere in the debate on the 2002 budget proposal. the first reaction to the preliminary draft budget is: in an overall assessment, we note the proposal to increase the appropriations for commitments by 3.4% and for payments by 4.8%. the difference between the two seems insufficient given the need to recover late payments. it is worth recalling that one of the european parliament's priorities is precisely to eliminate the gap between the commitments made and the payments actually made in the short term. if our fears are confirmed, the reduction in the balance to be settled will be merely apparent. in fact, if the increase in payment appropriations is insufficient, the payment of new commitments will be postponed. it would then be like a retrofit of the ral and not the solution of the problem. the solution is to solve the past lag, in particular in categories 3 (internal policies) and 4 (external action) and not to generate new lag. we will carefully examine the progress report to be submitted to us at the end of june, as agreed, and we will continue to do everything to improve the levels of budget implementation, which is to say that we want to contribute to a more effective fulfilment of the european union's commitments. we noted that despite the proposed 4.8% increase in payments, they remain at 1.06% of the european union's gdp, whereas the financial perspective would allow them to reach 1.10% in 2002. it should also be noted that we will be making a careful assessment of the level of consideration given by the commission to the priorities defined by parliament and set out in the guidelines adopted in april. in the specialty and in the field of agriculture - category 1 - we are closely following the impact of the bse and foot-and-mouth disease crises on the budget. it will be good to know the exact values involved as soon as possible. however, the existence of the reserve of eur 1 billion proposed by the commission seems acceptable, given the imponderability of the crises. it is, however, emphasized that for us a reserve is always a non-compulsory expenditure and that we therefore want to interfere in the decision on its use. if not before, in the letter of amendment to be presented after the first reading we hope to know exactly what the costs of bse and foot-and-mouth disease are. in this context, i would remind you that we have a commission document on the guidelines for the mid-term review of the latest reform of the common agricultural policy, which is expected to demonstrate the consistency of the options adopted in the meantime in the face of the crisis with a future orientation. the guidelines refer to the date of 15 september for the submission of such a document. the letter of amendment is another possibility to consider for the submission of this document. in the fisheries sector, given the reported failure of the fisheries agreement with morocco and the consequent need for fleet restructuring, it is necessary to carefully assess how this will be financed. in the internal policies (category 3), the margin is limited and there are clear priorities in this area in the guidelines we want to respect. in external action (category 4), the difficulty of ensuring stability in the european union's action persists. the reprogramming has been the rule and we are wary of the possibility of facing cuts again in areas which are important: latin america, human rights or the fight against aids, for example. in the balkans, however, where the european union has also made clear commitments, there is, as far as i understand, a reduction in budgetary resources. it seems that the world bank report, which will indicate precisely the reconstruction needs, has not yet been taken into account. what's going to happen then? and as for the operational costs of defence and security policy, where are we? in the area of administration (category 5), to what extent are the proposed appropriations sufficient to cope cumulatively with the reform of the commission, enlargement, the launching of new policies, the increase of the council budget by 10% and the proposals of the other institutions? the insufficiency of the means decided upon in berlin is obvious. we'll see! as regards pre-accession (category 7), i would point out that parliament has expressed its support for the re-allocation of unused appropriations, as is the case in category 2. i would like to say again that we hope to be able to conduct the debate which has just begun in a constructive manner. consensus with the commission and the council is desirable and possible. to do this, we must be aware of the clouds on the horizon - i am referring specifically to the fact that the committee on budgets voted today on its position on the financial regulation. i would stress that this is a matter for the guidelines. it states that parliament will not hesitate to reflect its position on this matter in its budgetary decisions. it is still possible to guarantee that our position on the revision of the financial regulation will be taken into account.
the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. - i know . mr president, despite everything, something is moving forward in europe with this regulation. first of all, because it legally enshrines european political parties as the main instruments for shaping european public opinion. that is, we get closer to the citizens and strengthen democracy. secondly, we clarify the nature of the resources of these parties by ensuring the transparency of their public community financing, subject to the normal controls of the audits and the court of auditors, while separating it from any promiscuity with the national parties. we then dignify parliament by giving it the responsibility for the legal recognition of european parties and the first political judgement on the suitability of each party to the common democratic values on which the union is founded. this regulation, which follows on from the tsatsos report and takes up the essence of its philosophy, is the first major achievement of the institutional framework on which european democracy is based. it opens the door to the urgent completion of the statute for members and assistants and also to the necessary uniform electoral law for the european parliament. i would say that we are taking the first steps towards the structural arrangements for community democracy, based on european citizenship. as rapporteur for the committee on legal affairs, i sincerely admire the work of the report and the effort made by mrs schleicher, who made it with great openness, receiving contributions from the other committees, including the contribution of the committee on legal affairs, did so with an open mind and ended up, with her attitude, by strengthening this contribution of the european parliament, creating confidence that, as we have already seen, the council will understand us and that we will soon have a definitive status, at least in this legal framework, of european political parties. i am therefore very grateful to mrs schleicher for her work.

(da) mr president, socialist-democratic group and the edd group unanimously reject the proposal to use the 'elastic' article of the treaty, article 308, to finance supranational european parties. the treaty of nice introduces a specific legal basis for support for political parties, thus proving that there is no legal basis for this purpose in the treaty of amsterdam. the court has held that article 308 cannot be applied as an alternative to an amendment to the treaty. the basis of the proposal is therefore clearly illegal as long as the treaty of nice has not been adopted in all member states. its content will also unfortunately be illegal under the treaty of nice, in that it does not cover supranational european parties. it covers political parties at european level, excluding, in a specific declaration, discrimination. the schleicher report provides for the allocation to certain parties of specific support which is not allocated to others. this is contrary to the principle of equality. in a democracy, only voters can decide on representation in parliament and competition between parties should be based on equality. particular advantages cannot be granted to certain parties because they are based on a local or supranational organisation. it is not the task of the majority in parliament to correct the electoral results by forcing all voters to contribute to the financing of five supranational parties in the eu, and it is incredible that the commission, as the guardian of legality, can put forward a proposal which is so obviously illegal, and it is incredible that parliament is prepared today to support a proposal which, according to existing legal practice, can be declared illegal by the european court. i urge the groups to discuss this matter with the legal services of parliament and i ask that the vote be postponed at least until the next sitting.

commission. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i shall be extremely brief, even if i do not want to delay the voting period. the commission shares the general view of mrs gebhardt on the eurojust report and would like to congratulate her on the excellent work she has done. we particularly stress the importance of finding appropriate solutions for the link between eurojust and the european judicial network, which has made a very important contribution to the development of judicial cooperation at european level, and to defining the conditions for the involvement of the commission itself and europol in eurojust's work. at this stage, the eurojust unit has no powers to take operational decisions or to promote, as such, investigations or the bringing of cases to court. but obviously the natural evolution of eurojust is to reach this higher stage of judicial cooperation. that is why the views expressed by mrs gebhardt, which the commission shares, are important, as regards the guarantees of defence for defendants, the rules on data protection and the overall issues of safeguarding the fundamental rights of citizens. we believe that the charter of fundamental rights of the european union is an important guide for the adoption of these solutions. we share the honourable member's suggestions, but there is one point on which i would like to make a reservation: the commission has also proposed that the court of justice should be able to give its opinion on the question of the legality of the infringement. we think it would be better to talk about jurisdiction in the area of economic and financial crime. one of the most sensitive issues is the link between eurojust and olaf. olaf has competence in the field of the first pillar; eurojust emerges in the specific intergovernmental context of the third pillar; we would therefore like to avoid giving the impression that eurojust must always be involved in cases where olaf takes the initiative to safeguard the protection of the community's financial interests. the specification of the different fields of application seems to us to be very important. finally, madam president, with regard to the imaginative and creative figure presented by mr dell'alba, who called eurojust eurosaintjust, i must say for my part that i believe that eurojust is not an instrument of the justice of the saints. it is an instrument of human justice and aware of the difficulties, and even some of the risks; like all human work, it is perfectible. it is not a sacred office, but a first step towards ensuring the protection of fundamental rights and the fight against crime at european level. improved, but a necessary first step. i therefore hope that the house will support mrs gebhardt's report. in writing. - (pt) the report by mrs ilda figueiredo on the programme to combat social exclusion deserves our support. in fact, we must encourage cooperation between the european union and the member states to combat social exclusion and to eradicate poverty - realities which are unfortunately still very much present in some member states, such as my country, portugal - in accordance with qualitative and quantitative objectives agreed between the council and parliament. the commission will be required to provide parliament and the council with information on progress made under this programme each year. stimulating dialogue with partners in this field at european, national and regional level, in order to fight poverty and social exclusion more effectively, and to maximise financial support for innovative projects with european added value, are key factors for its success. in order to enable the programme to be fully implemented, by maintaining on-the-ground travel, enabling the participation of people in situations of social exclusion and poverty and financing the actions of ngos, it is imperative to raise the reference amount to eur 100 million, including technical and administrative expenditure. the basic funding of the main european networks taking part in these actions must also be considered. the preparation of an annual eu round table on social exclusion, organised in close cooperation with the presidency of the council of the union and in consultation with all relevant stakeholders, is another initiative that cannot be ignored. finally, i would like to draw attention to the inadequacy of the financial control mechanisms of ngos and to call for better auditing, which can guarantee the sound and rigorous application of public funds.
in writing. - (pt) draft supplementary and amending budget (sb) no 2/2001 presented by the council aims to continue and accelerate the development of the common european security and defence policy (ceesdp) by creating three new directorates within the general secretariat of the council, recruiting 51 officials, paying allowances to military experts and purchasing telecommunications and data protection equipment. the management structures of the pecsd are thus being created, helping to accelerate the process of militarization of the european union, a process which we reject and which in itself justifies the rejection of this brs and the ferber report. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (emah) is based on the principle of the european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (eahp) and the principle of the european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (eahp). however, certain institutional and technical issues must be respected. by including these operational expenditure as administrative expenditure of the council, on the one hand the nature of the expenditure is confused and on the other hand the possibility of democratic control of this expenditure is eliminated, particularly by parliament, which thus renounces, in practice, its budgetary power. but beyond the questions of democracy and transparency, the pressure on the ceiling for category 5 expenditure - administrative expenditure - is increasing as the european union is being enlarged. the present motion for a resolution tabled by the green group deals only with these last two questions, accepting in essence the position of the ferber report, without addressing the substantive questions of the esdp. that is why we cannot vote against it.
in writing. - (pt) we are well aware of our opposition to the creation of 'european political parties' or other forms of organisations with supranational characteristics, because to go down this road clearly means taking a further step towards strengthening the federalist orientation of the european union and anticipating predictable measures in this direction. although this initiative of the european parties is presented as a way of overcoming the criticism of the court of auditors regarding the financing of the political groups of the european parliament, it appears to be imposed by the dominant forces of the european union, without there being any real support for the creation of these "european parties", since the treaty of nice has not even been ratified by any national parliament yet. finally, it is also worth mentioning the disagreement in principle with the innumerable aspects which are included in the present proposal, particularly as regards the criteria for its formation and the possibilities of financing.

in writing. - (pt) this report addresses a problem of the utmost importance for the future well-being of workers in the european union, who must be guaranteed secure and sustainable pension schemes in the long term. as we all know, there have been many catastrophic visions of the future of social protection, because of the ageing of the population and the increase in life expectancy. although the report did not sufficiently distance itself from the commission's more negative positions, particularly when it refers to the need for greater flexibility in pension schemes, it stresses the importance of increasing employment and public systems based on solidarity between generations and the right balance between the proportionality of contributions and benefits on the one hand and the principle of solidarity to avoid poverty and social exclusion on the other. it also takes up the 1992 recommendation on the guaranteed minimum income to be provided by social protection schemes in order to support initiatives leading to its implementation. however, the report falls short of what should be put into practice in terms of socioeconomic policies, which effectively guarantee sound public schemes and decent pensions. that's why we abstained.

was a missed opportunity for europe to take a solid and extensive step forward in its political and social deepening. the european parliament worked to ensure that the charter of fundamental rights was incorporated into the treaties, the conference approved it but did not incorporate it into the treaties. the european parliament wanted more matters to be decided not by unanimity but by qualified majority, because the situation is already difficult with 15, let alone 27, and the conference has done just the opposite. the european parliament wanted the qualified majority rule for decisions in the council to be co-decision with this parliament, and the conference did not do what we thought was right. nice had the smallest agenda in the history of the intergovernmental conferences and yet it took the most timid step in all the reforms of the treaties. in contrast, the nice conference gave rise to an image of a europe of 'interests' rather than a europe of 'community of values', an image project. but the council was aware of this when it adopted its 'declaration 23', when it said and recognised that it had achieved little in nice. and in a way he recognised that the method of possibility of continuing enlargement and in view of the fact that a debate and a new method of preparing and revising the treaties are opening up, which makes it possible to think of europe in a more global, more serious, more coherent and above all more profound way. we are not saying 'yes' to the treaty of nice, as in maastricht, or 'yes' to the treaty of amsterdam, as in amsterdam, but we are not helping to stop the enlargement process and are thus working in a positive way to make enlargement a reality. in other words, this european parliament does not want to be part of the problem, it wants to be part of the solution. and so we reaffirm here too that in our report we will not subordinate the evaluation of the treaty of nice to the conclusions of the laeken council, but we will take the intergovernmental method failed in nice and if the convention succeeded in drawing up the charter of fundamental rights which the council signed, why not look to the good example of the convention - mr president, madam president-in-office of the council, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to leave nine brief messages. this report is not about the treaty of nice, it is about the post-nice process, the beginning of the political siege of the laeken summit to be held at the end of the year. nice is not here yet, they're already galloping. the so-called crisis of the european union is the result of this federalist obsession. we could focus on reforming the institutions to make them work better. we could start here in parliament, where democratic representation is so weak and mediocre in its authenticity. but no! instead they want to reform everything else. they spend their lives discussing how to change the balance between institutions. and that is where the struggle for power comes from, which is criticized so much later: a struggle between states, a struggle between institutions. it opened the struggle for power. federalists like to invoke the founding fathers, but you'd better read them. robert schuman, in his famous statement of may 9, 1950, said: 'europe will not be built all at once, nor will it be built all at once. it will be achieved by concrete achievements which first create de facto solidarity.' this is enough to dismiss this report, which is full of conjecture and concoctions, and completely ignores the concrete achievements for a long-term de facto solidarity. where is the real cohesion? in the same vein, chancellor gerhard schroeder should be thanked for having dispelled once and for all one of the myths of federalism, the false idea that it serves the smallest and weakest. it is a proposal for the centralisation of power, the erasure of the member states, the destruction of economic and social cohesion. quite the opposite. we are not joining a united states of europe, but a community. we do not want a federation of nation states, but the union of nation states that we are. no more and no less. who are you abandoning? who is destroying the europe that was successfully built? the drafting of a european constitution is another perverse fantasy. if there is no european people, there is no legitimate european constituent power. and if there is no european constituent power, there can be no european constitution. the reform of the treaties is to be done by treaties. there 's an arabic proverb that goes like this: "the word is an invention of man to hide what he thinks" ... - mr president, i disagree with this report for three main reasons. the first concerns its assessment of the content of the treaty of nice, whereby it takes a maximalist position and criticises the positions adopted as timid and insufficient, when it should have been criticising federalist advances which call into question the fundamental interests of some of the present and future member states, since the acceding countries have not been able to participate in a decision-making process on changes affecting them. even in the european parliament it is unacceptable for it to criticise the position taken in nice of increasing the number of members to allow for a more pluralist composition after enlargement, although it then tries to do some justice to the number of representatives from hungary and the czech republic. that's the least of it! the second disagreement concerns the acceleration of the process of preparing the new treaty at a time when the treaty of nice has not even been ratified by the national parliaments of the member states. insisting on a proposal which aims to make rapid progress in preparing the revision of the treaty so that it can be completed by the end of 2003, without even guaranteeing the participation of the acceding countries on an equal footing, is unacceptable. the third disagreement concerns the proposed method of following the draft charter. this method excludes the participation of parties with different sensibilities, thus undermining pluralism and the genuine independent and democratic participation of national parliaments.

- mr president, the reformulation of the financial regulation is a necessarily difficult process. this regulation deals not only with a budgetary framework but also with the presentation and monitoring of accounts and the duties and responsibilities of the main budgetary actors. all these issues have been dealt with in the same article of the treaty, which requires the same procedures for them, but this does not imply that they are necessarily dealt with in the same regulatory texts. the fact that its adoption requires the unanimity of the council makes it even more difficult to amend it. perhaps for these reasons, this process has been dragging on since the commission presented its first proposal for a recast in july 1996. and all indications are that he will yet meet a new stage. the concern to restore coherence to a text which has become a tangle of disparate rules and provisions is therefore naturally the most important concern in the present reformulation exercise. in this exercise of budgetary discipline it is neither possible nor desirable to forget the relevant sectoral regulations, in particular those relating to agricultural expenditure, the structural funds, research and development and external action, nor the content of the important interinstitutional agreement on budgetary discipline. in this respect, since it is not politically or legally acceptable to override existing regulations and agreements by adopting provisions in the financial regulation which are contrary to them, it is necessary to reduce the specific rules in the sectoral regulations to the minimum necessary, and it is also necessary that these be perfectly articulated with those of the financial regulation. it is also necessary to align and frame the interinstitutional agreement with the financial regulation and to consider the need to include the financial regulation in any future revision of that agreement. the second major question concerns the community budgetary system organized in commitments and payments and the time limits for its implementation. the current system has known weaknesses and problems, but it is far from certain that the innovations proposed by the commission (let alone those suggested by other institutions) will help to improve the situation. it is a point where further reflection is essential, based on a thorough and rigorous assessment of the functioning of the present system. we hope that the commission will give it the necessary attention in its future proposals. another point of great importance is that of late payments. the european institutions have recently adopted, on a proposal from the commission, a directive which imposes severe financial penalties on both public and private authorities for failure to comply with a maximum payment deadline of 30 days. how is it possible, then, that the european institutions want to exempt themselves from the rules they have adopted for all the other institutions, private or public, of the union? we would point out in this connection that the commission's communication on this subject is not satisfactory. only the commission's commitment to apply to itself in an exemplary manner the rules it proposes to others is acceptable. it is also a point of honour for us socialists to ensure that the political groups in this parliament follow without restriction the procedures which we are here approving for community expenditure by the other institutions. we will be very attentive to how each of you will deal with this issue in the vote tomorrow. finally, we note the council's failure to respond to our repeated requests for discussion of the amendments to be made to the financial regulation. it would be absurd and unacceptable for the european parliament, on which the adoption of the budget depends in the last instance, to be deprived of any power of intervention in the rules which form the framework of that same regulation. in these circumstances, we have no choice but to postpone a final position of parliament. i would like to conclude by congratulating our rapporteurs, gianfranco dell'alba and michiel van hulten, who have done an excellent job here, and i hope that this work will continue in the same atmosphere of cooperation in which it has been carried out so far.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations. - i know . mr president, the committee on employment and social affairs has considered that, despite the legislative progress achieved, wage discrimination and the segmentation of the labour market persist, with low wages perpetuating in traditional sectors which tend to persist in services and the so-called 'new economy'. 40% of the increase in women's employment is part-time and self-employed, based on minimum wages, which, being an alternative to unemployment and the possible reconciliation with family life, does not fail to constitute a new indirect discrimination underestimated in the statistics. as is the overqualified recruitment for lower-value jobs. the structural funds should have a role in encouraging the social partners to adopt equality pacts. the lisbon council decisions, the employment guidelines for 2001, the social agenda and the new treaty wording define the importance of increasing the employment of women, quantitatively and qualitatively, not only to give effect to a right but above all to economic equilibrium and the sustainability of pensions. the important thing is to write straight even if it's in crooked lines. the committee on employment and social affairs proposes in its texts new formulas for the concepts of positive action, indirect discrimination affecting one or more persons and harassment as a phenomenon of subjection and humiliation, with or without direct sexual abuse. let us put it this way: we choose a conceptualisation which we consider to be more comprehensive and appropriate. the members of this committee were unanimous in extending the protection of maternity, paternity, adoption and pregnant women, and we again recommend, as this parliament has already done, the revision of directive 92/85/ec. the right of return to work provided for in directive 76/207/eec - and we disagree with mrs hautala's report on this - should allow women to take up an equivalent post in the event of the expiry or change of their previous job, in order to protect their employment after maternity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the future of the european union. few people know what eures does, what it already has at its disposal for those seeking work in another eu country or for those who, as employers, accept applicants from another country. few also know what eures may mean in the future. our first recommendation is therefore to promote eures and its database more widely, which should be reflected in the budget, the activity plan and the budgetary implementation, as well as in the various member states, where it should be properly monitored and pursued by the respective public employment services, which are already linked together through eures. eures is a network of networks made up of some 500 euroadvisers, public and associative bodies and partners, and offers a valuable opportunity for direct consultation via the open internet. it aims to promote the transparency of the european labour market by providing information, advice and placement assistance. it covers not only the union but also the european economic area, currently covering 17 countries. in short, it is intended to facilitate the effective exercise of the right to free movement of workers and to promote mobility, which we understand not as a political end in itself but as the need to respond to voluntary and spontaneous requests from people. but it makes little sense if people don't know it exists at the bottom. the first priority, therefore, is to promote and disseminate. moreover, thanks to the growing use of the internet, the effectiveness of eures will increasingly depend on widespread knowledge by the general public and much less on simple improvements in the operation of the bureaucratic network of services and bodies. a special mention should be made of a specific sector of eures, the area of crossborder cooperation in the field of employment and labour mobility, which clearly has a higher level of response: crossborder eures. today there are already 20 specific structures in this field, but there is still a lot to be done here. i would like to mention in particular the case of my country's border with spain, which is still largely uncovered, and i am also mindful of the new challenges and opportunities that the next enlargement will bring in this area. however, thanks to the important virtualities that these crossborder eures structures offer in terms of local development, decentralised social dialogue, specific development of crossborder mobility, being able to work in another country without changing residence, there is clearly another priority for the future. the commission's report, which covers the period 1998-99, contains positive data, which we have noted. the negative aspects that must be addressed immediately are the abovementioned lack of public awareness, the relatively low involvement of employers' organizations, the very marked national imbalance between the presence of the different member states, which is worrying and impossible to accept, certain shortcomings detected on the website, in particular its multilingual nature, in which all european citizens are equal, and the lack of any mention of the outermost regions, so we also ask for special attention to be paid to their specific problems in this area in the future. the commission's own report contains, in our view, some shortcomings which we have pointed out. in particular, there is a lack of diagnosis and an assessment of the situation in terms of the evolution of the service itself and the actual state of labour mobility within the union and the european economic area. future eures reports will be much more useful and much more politically understandable if they present a critical view of the successes and failures achieved, if they include more relevant information, both statistical and qualitative, if they allow us to better assess the fairness of the priorities, the desirability of future targets and strategies, and if they also allow us to make a good parliamentary check on the grounds of the choices made. this requalification of the future biennial eures reports and their content is, moreover, particularly required by the conclusions of the last spring summit in stockholm, in particular those adopted following the commission's communication to the council entitled 'new european labour markets, open to all, with access to all', which we follow, which we welcome and whose articulation we strongly recommend in this context. we also propose a programme for enlargement, a programme of preparatory actions which we want to be accessible and necessary. and since a new legal basis for the organisation of eures is being announced, probably in 2002, the word of this parliament is very simple: parliament wants to be part of that redefinition.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the right and opportunity to live and work in different member states of the european union is an important political aspiration of european citizens. the eures network is based on the existence of this legal right of free movement of workers within the territory of the european union and the european economic area and on the undesirable fact that obstacles to labour mobility still exist. this network, which focuses on the exchange of information and experience in counselling and placement support for professionals, is supported by eures advisers in dialogue and coordination with the public employment services of the member states, as well as by an internet access service which should in future be more widely disseminated, making it easier for the average user to consult and search. eures contributes to a more flexible european labour market and increased mobility, and provides a range of vocational training opportunities in crossborder regions. the positive features of this network which should be strengthened, as the rapporteur, mr ribeiro e castro, has rightly pointed out, and which i welcome, are the ease of access and exchange of information on employment opportunities in this vast area, the multilingual content of this system, which must be promptly and rigorously updated, and legal assistance to citizens, especially as regards tax and social security systems. that said, it is important not to overestimate eures. this network, which is very positive, is merely an instrument for implementing the european employment strategy. in fact, there are specific employment directives aimed at removing the social, cultural and linguistic barriers which hamper mobility in the european union.
in writing. - (pt) this report provides an opportunity to deepen the initial proposals for revising directive 76/207/eec on the achievement of equal treatment for men and women in matters of access to employment, training and promotion, and working conditions, by extending its scope to include equality in all aspects of women's working life, and by trying to make a positive contribution to the abolition of the innumerable discrimination which persists in the world of work. we contributed to the preparatory debate, in particular by presenting proposals from working meetings in portugal with trade unionists from sectors of activity predominantly female, in particular in trade and services, the textile industries, clothing, footwear, electrical and electronic equipment. the report does not contain all our proposals, but some of them are implicitly reflected in the report, particularly as regards the need to prevent discrimination against women workers on the grounds of pregnancy and maternity, the need to take the necessary measures to reconcile family and working life, and the need to increase restrictions on any possibility of discrimination in access to employment. there is also a whole series of positive amendments in the area of combating sexual harassment at work and protecting preventive measures and positive actions in the protection of equality in the event of discrimination. that is why we voted for this report.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in many countries of the union - and in my own country, portugal, in particular - there are today phenomena of exploitation of people from eastern europe who, in search of a better standard of living, are victims of networks set up to exploit their suffering. the exploitation of human beings, men, women and children, is a repugnant phenomenon for whatever purpose: clandestine, sexual or other work. we therefore have a responsibility to combat this scourge and to produce the necessary regulatory instruments. this is what has been done, by seeking to establish a common definition of the elements of these types of crime, by trying to establish lower thresholds for the maximum penalties to be applied and by seeking to define rules of jurisdiction and judicial and police cooperation. i would like to congratulate mrs ewa klamt and mrs anna karamanou and commissioner antónio vitorino on their efforts in this area. in fact, issues related to trafficking in human beings and the sexual exploitation of children should be a priority on the political agenda of the european union. we must strengthen the fight against the sexual exploitation of children and child pornography by improving the provisions of the joint action of february 1997 and ensuring that there can be no safe havens for perpetrators of sexual offences against children when they are suspected of having committed a crime in a country other than their own. it is also intended to complement the efforts made so far by the union and the international community, and it should be noted that the united nations protocol on trafficking in human beings was adopted in december 2000 within the framework of the convention against transnational organized crime, which was signed by 81 states. the adoption of these proposals in this house should be a clear demonstration of our will to take up an effective fight against these unacceptable violations of human dignity and rights. i welcome the fact that the council has reached agreement on the basic elements of the proposal for a framework decision on combating trafficking in human beings, but i regret that there has been no consensus on the level of criminal sanctions.
