- mr president, as mr aparício sánchez has pointed out in the explanatory statement to this timely report, there is not enough data to know objectively the effectiveness of the directive and the regulation on the export of cultural goods and their restitution when they have been illegally removed from the territory of one state to another. this is largely due to the delay in transposing these diplomas into the internal legal systems of the member states. there is every reason to believe that the illicit trafficking of cultural goods, particularly those circulating on the art market, has increased in the meantime. it is all very well for the member states to observe the principle of strict respect for the integrity of national cultural heritage and for preventive and repressive measures against such trafficking to be made more effective. but there's more to it than that. here, in this parliament, not long ago, we all had the opportunity to see an exhibition on the serious waste of cyprus' cultural heritage, perpetrated under the complacent eye of the authorities of a country which, by the way, is also a candidate for enlargement of the union. the measures now proposed can thus make a significant contribution to improving the legal instruments available and the system as a whole. each cultural heritage is of vital importance to national identity and european identity. judicial and police cooperation between member states in this area should be strengthened. the movement of stolen or misappropriated cultural property should be hindered. in this area, illegal trafficking and trade in these goods, giving an update on the current state of the matter, listing and identifying the goods which remain to be recovered and studying the consequences of the - mr president, commissioner, in recent times the greatest fear of europeans was hunger. famine was, along with war and plague, one of the three apocalyptic fears that had pervaded the history of europe since the middle ages. the times have changed radically with regard to hunger, not for the whole world, unfortunately, but for the europe of the fifteen. plenty of food is one of the achievements of the modern age in europe, as is a food of a much higher hygienic quality than was prevalent until recently. nevertheless, food safety and quality have suddenly become one of the main causes of anxiety, fear and uncertainty among sophisticated european consumers. in fact, brutal and relentless diseases against which medicine is powerless, such as bovine spongiform encephalopathy, are the result of the blind and unnatural industrialization of food production. this situation must be brought to an end, in particular by urgent and far-reaching changes to the european common agricultural policy. the legislation and strategy to be adopted in the field of food safety are vital for the european union and its credibility. they should be one of your highest political and legislative priorities. the proposal for a regulation under consideration is the main pillar of a legislative edifice designed to guarantee a high level of health protection and to restore consumer confidence. the cornerstone of this legislation is the creation of a functioning european food authority with high scientific capabilities and a high degree of independence and autonomy. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a fire, and on the proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a fire. hence the need for a prestigious and reputable food authority. the commission's proposals are well formulated, it must be admitted. they are good, useful and well-structured, they just need some clarification, as has already been said. we must adopt them as a matter of urgency. it is neither meaningful nor dignifying for us to be divided, for example, on the composition of the board of directors, on the appointment of the high authority or on the location of the high authority. the report by mr whitehead is balanced and sensible and deserves my support.
in writing. - (pt) we cannot see the creation of a european food authority (efa) as the panacea for food safety problems. the current eu policies on food safety, notably at the level of the common agricultural policy and the common fisheries policy, need to be changed. productivism and the pursuit of profit have had a negative impact on the overall safety of food, which has cost human and animal health dearly, as we have seen with the bse crisis, dioxin and, more recently, foot-and-mouth disease. the eea cannot be a smokescreen for public opinion. furthermore, an authority which ensures risk assessment for food safety, based on sound scientific advice, cannot relieve the commission of its responsibility or replace it at the level of risk management of community food policy and the competent authorities in each member state. the whole food chain must be involved, with emphasis on the role of the agri-food industry in food safety. we agree that the fundamental principle of all food law is the protection of human health and that it is therefore essential to introduce the precautionary principle in risk management where scientific data are insufficient or inconclusive. the traceability of food, with an integrated 'farm to consumer' approach, is a good principle, although we have our doubts about its immediate application in portugal, without transitional periods, given the characteristics of our agricultural production and the sectors involved. however, the question arises as to whether such a structure, which is burdensome from the administrative and budgetary point of view, is necessary. the same applies to the appointment of representatives to the administrative board, which should be subject to greater participation by the respective authorities of each member state. the amendments tabled by parliament concerning the need for imported products to comply with community legislation and the extension of the legislation to animal feed are, however, fundamental to us.
in writing. - (pt) as the report acknowledges, important documents already protect children at international level, including the un convention on the rights of the child, the protocol on the sale of children and child prostitution and child pornography, and the council of europe convention on cybercrime. it is up to the european union to complete and deepen this work, taking into account the seriousness of the situation which persists in this area. but it is not enough to provide legal frameworks. we must also adopt substantive political measures to deal with the causes. the european commission's proposal is rather poor in its analysis of the causes and consequences of the sexual exploitation of children, and is very timid about the measures proposed and the definition of child pornography itself. during the debate on the proposal in the committee on women's rights, we tabled several amendments, thus contributing to the improvement of the initial positions. the report makes a significant improvement on the commission's proposal in many respects, although it falls short of what is desirable in this area, particularly as regards the concept of child pornography and the penalties applicable.
in writing. - (pt) this report is based on the commission's proposal which links trafficking in human beings for sexual exploitation (prostitution) and trafficking in human beings for labour (migrant workers), which is not the best way of analysing and dealing with these two serious problems. the un treats them very differently and thus makes a clear distinction between the two realities and the approaches they require. however, the report greatly improves the commission's proposal by introducing some 50 amendments, including the view that trafficking in human beings should not only be punished but should be expressly classified as a criminal offence. it also proposes tougher prison sentences and considers that incitement, complicity and aiding and abetting in such trafficking should be punished. the proposal to create a compensation fund for victims of trafficking in human beings is also important. this fund will be financed from the confiscated assets of criminals. other positive points include the emphasis on the need for adequate protection of victims and witnesses, the provision of temporary residence permits to victims of trafficking in human beings for the duration of the investigation or judicial proceedings, including the provision of adequate housing and other assistance, including for reintegration, and the creation of a database of missing persons. that is why we voted for the report, regretting that commissioner antónio vitorino was not prepared to accept all these proposals from the european parliament.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the subject we are discussing today is part of a much broader problem which involves not only the external representation of the community but, in the final analysis, the cfsp itself. the situation of the union's external representation has been subject to a number of criticisms in recent years and it still needs to undergo a major joint effort to transform it. the interpenetration of the different vectors - trade, development aid, the economic, political and diplomatic - is becoming increasingly evident in the world today and must be clearly assumed, not so much as an inevitability, but above all as an opportunity. an opportunity for the european union to strengthen its role in the international system by defending the principles of democracy and human rights that legitimise this same objective. we recognise that this awareness underlies the commission's concern to improve the external service, which has resulted in the adoption of several communications since 1996. but the whole thing lacks coherence and there is still a lot of work to be done. i must confess that we are looking forward with great anticipation to the commission communication on the external service which commissioner patten promised to send to parliament last september and which i believe should constitute a strategic framework for further important developments. it should not be forgotten that today's subject is really very limited, namely the strengthening and adaptation of the network of delegations, but its implications are extremely concrete and, as i have already stressed, also reach the political and diplomatic sphere. we must therefore support the general decentralization and rationalisation of external aid, with the resulting need for resources and technical means. i would like to stress, however, that i disagree with the almost hyper-realistic view that still exists in certain conceptions of the union's international role in development aid. i would therefore like to stress three aspects: firstly, the importance of regional cooperation, but it should be increased in the light of political balances, the processes of transition to democracy and its consolidation, and the relationship between the different states. another related aspect is the need for consistency in the basic guidelines, not being bound and dependent, sometimes almost exclusively, on constraints of means whose urgency must also be verified. another is the importance of taking on some political criteria, in particular by recognising the role of the different states in the processes of democratisation and the defence of human rights in their respective regional areas. we call for the project of closing the delegations in these countries to be suspended, also being aware that the cost of maintaining them is irrelevant as a whole. this and other aspects have been considered by the rapporteur, mr galeote quecedo, who has done an excellent job and we therefore support his motions for resolutions and hope that they will be considered by the commission and the council.

- as the time allotted for question time in the council has run out, questions 22 to 31 will be answered in writing. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. before we interrupt our work until 9 p.m., i should like to say on behalf of the presidency how much we have appreciated the work done here by the president-in-office of the council, mr lars danielsson, under the swedish presidency, for the sympathy, the simplicity, the activity and the objectivity with which he has interpreted the vicissitudes of question time, in particular, and the dialogue with the european parliament. we wish him well personally and we wish him well for the gothenburg summit. (the sitting was suspended at 7.35 p.m. and resumed at 9.00 p.m.)
the commission has also taken the initiative of introducing a new system of vat, which is to be introduced in the member states. the difficulty with this proposal is to find an optimum balance which, by making it easier for businesses, especially smes, since this directive will apply to all companies in the european union, will not lead to a loss of revenue or to control difficulties for tax administrations. it is worth remembering that vat revenue is not only a very important source of revenue for national budgets but also one of the own resources for financing the budget of the european union. i therefore propose that the commission proposal be accepted in its entirety, since it meets three important political objectives. firstly, the proposed harmonisation of the mandatory particulars on invoices will reduce the administrative burden for operators operating throughout the european union. the european union currently has 15 different regulations and a total of 25 different compulsory mentions. the proposal to replace this amalgam of rules with a single list of mandatory particulars is therefore welcome, which in practice means that a european operator will only have to comply with a single regulation for all invoices issued to customers in the european union. secondly, the introduction of the common framework for electronic invoicing will alleviate the administrative burden on businesses, while making vat audits more effective. in addition, electronic invoicing will save businesses money, since the cost of sending and processing an electronic invoice is much lower than that of a paper invoice. thirdly, the introduction of a common regulation for electronic invoicing will also stimulate the development of electronic commerce in europe, especially as regards the services provided by this means. i therefore agree that the amendments which i and my colleagues have tabled in committee have improved the draft directive. however, there are two aspects of the vote in committee with which i do not agree. one concerns an amendment tabled by my colleague josé manuel garcía-margallo y marfil on the numbering of invoices. the commission proposes a single number, and i propose that this single number be sequential, with one or more series. in other words, we can adopt different series, by country or by product type, but always on a sequential basis. i think that this must have been the intention of my colleague josé manuel garcía-margallo y marfil, but the version that has been adopted says exactly the opposite. the other aspect concerns the electronic signature. i agree with the commission's proposal that the authenticity of the origin and the integrity of the content of the invoice should be guaranteed by using an advanced electronic signature within the meaning of the directive of the european parliament and of the council on a community legal framework for electronic signatures. the committee on economic and monetary affairs voted against this proposal, or rather, introduced an amendment to delete this proposal. i think we should reinstate it, that is, we should keep the commission's text. why would you do that? the reason is that if electronic signatures involve costs, these are relatively insignificant compared to the potential savings. the level of security chosen by the commission can be purchased on the belgian market, for example, for eur 15 per year. this cost is expected to decrease in the near future, particularly with the forthcoming entry into force of the directive. parliament and the council have adopted a directive to make this simplified electronic signature available. we have here the opportunity to use it and give assurances to the tax authorities. i think that this principle proposed by the commission should be maintained. i would like to say that this is a good proposal, that the amendments which parliament is proposing - with the exception of these two cases - are all in the right direction: the project will make life easier for businesses and tax administrations.
in writing. - (pt) as the european council in gothenburg is being discussed, a few days after ireland voted no in the referendum on the treaty of nice, it is essential that the political leaders of the european union, including the governments of the individual member states, draw all the political consequences of this important decision. democracy must respect the results of an act like this. the amendments to the treaty approved in nice should therefore be reviewed and not insist, as was the case recently with the vote on the report co-signed by the socialist member of parliament, antónio josé seguro, on speeding up the process of federalist integration of the european union, which calls into question the fundamental aspects of national sovereignty and the interests of small countries. it is regrettable that the gothenburg summit was not used to reflect on the need to review the current process of neoliberal, federalist and militarist integration, including the undemocratic methodologies that have been used. it is important to insist on a new direction, based on respect for cooperation between sovereign and equal states, on the promotion of peace and on responding to the real needs of the peoples. it is essential to make progress in gothenburg in support of peace in the middle east, respect for the rights of the palestinian people, protection of the environment and sustainable development. it is also important to have a clear position against george bush's most aggressive positions, particularly on the us missile defence project and the death penalty, and to demand respect for the kyoto commitments.
in writing. - (pt) sustainable development is at the top of the international agenda. this week the european union is honouring it with a council meeting - the one in gothenburg. next year the international community will meet in johannesburg at the rio+10 summit, precisely to update its strategy for sustainable development. but we won't deceive. the international frenzy around this fundamental concept - sustainability (which is to say solidarity between future generations and solidarity between peoples) - is more about adhering to a long-planned timetable than about implementing a strategy. ten years after the earth summit in rio de janeiro, which marked the world's commitment to development that combines not only the economic and social but also the environmental dimensions, signs of unsustainability persist. from worsening global warming to the depletion of the ozone layer. from food insecurity to growing resistance to antibiotics. from the proliferation of chemicals, especially those that are organically persistent, to the depletion of biodiversity. from the accumulation of industrial waste to the congestion of cities. and of course, there is this horrible signal that proves that our growth model is not sustainable: poverty and social exclusion. in this context, the gothenburg council is of particular importance. it will simultaneously approve new sustainable development policies for the european union and lay the foundations for a global strategy to be agreed in johannesburg. before i make my political wishes for gothenburg, let me briefly touch on the institutional question. the swedish government's decision to conclude its presidency of the european union with a council devoted to sustainable development is to be congratulated on - and the political agenda has been a very refreshing one. the document prepared by the european commission to serve as a basis for the decisions of the council ("a sustainable europe for a better world") is also to be welcomed. but it is unacceptable that the very late presentation of this document by the european commission has prevented parliament from making an appropriate contribution to this reflection. i would like to return to the political question and make three wishes for gothenburg. 1) the the gothenburg council must give birth to a comprehensive strategy for sustainable development the european union must lead the global debate on a new growth model based on new production and consumption methods. a model where economic growth and social well-being do not depend on the intensive use of resources. gothenburg is the appropriate stage to open this global debate. it would not only be absolutely ridiculous, but also pointless, if, a few months before a world summit was to be held with the intention of outlining this model of global development, the 15 heads of government were to start looking at the navel of the european union in gothenburg. in the era of globalisation, we need european sustainable development policies and goals that aim to contribute to and strengthen global sustainable development policies and goals. we hope that the eyes of gothenburg will be turned to johannesburg...2) the gothenburg council must give rise to a political commitment - with specific objectives and actions - to correct the unsustainability in the european union. gothenburg must be action oriented. with goals and specific timelines. in this connection, i hope that the council will approve the following objectives: - reduction of european greenhouse gas emissions by 30% by 2020 compared to 1990 levels; - elimination of subsidies to the production and consumption of fossil fuels by 2010; - alternative fuels must account for more than 7% of all fuels used in road transport by 2010 and more than 20% by 2020; - implement the new community chemicals policy by 2004 and ensure the total eradication of all chemicals harmful to the environment and human health by 2020; - adopt a plan (with implications for agricultural and health policies) to reduce human resistance to antibiotics as early as 2001; - completely halt the depletion of biodiversity by 2010; - adoption of environmental liability legislation by 2003; - promote public road, rail and maritime transport so that by 2010 road transport use is below 1998 levels; - encourage the use of renewable energy by aiming for 12% by 2010 and 50% by 2040. 3) the gothenburg council must lay the foundations for a 'greening' of the european union's common policies. there is little point in setting environmental objectives if the policies, especially the common policies, which are the vehicle for achieving these objectives, prove to be inadequate. it is therefore essential that the council indicate when and how the common policies of the european union which are not of a sustainable nature will be reformed. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
- mr president, the central issue in this legislative proposal is the inclusion of the selfemployed. the rapporteur proposes a three-year transitional period for the automatic inclusion of selfemployed persons in the scope of this directive, which is sensible, balanced and worthy of applause. the common position adopted by the council, which excludes selfemployed drivers and does not even guarantee that they will be covered by the proposed working time arrangements, would have the following consequences: it would jeopardize the objectives of the common transport policy, it would not promote road safety and a growing equality of conditions of competition, it would deny adequate protection of the safety and health of persons carrying out mobile road transport activities as a profession. this proposal thus represents an acceptable balance between economic aspects and road safety and workers' health. in fact, if adopted, as we hope, it will not only help to reduce the number of road accidents associated with overworking, but also to establish a healthier competition between selfemployed and salaried drivers, making it possible to combat the phenomenon of fake selfemployed workers which distorts the rules of fair competition in this sector. as regards the maximum night working time, particularly in view of the situation in peripheral member states such as portugal, the solution adopted by the council, namely 10 hours for every 24 hours, is more appropriate. road workers in these member states are obliged to travel longer distances to reach the major centres of consumption and production. the only way to reduce the travel time is to take advantage of the night when traffic is less intense.
- mr president, i would like to congratulate the commission on this second legislative package which, rather than starting from scratch - the member states already have technical capacities, their shipyards, their shipowners, their scientific community, their operators - gives the european citizen today the start of the legislative guarantee and management of the security of his exclusive economic zone. we think it is worth noting the effort and persistence that the commissioner has put into this dossier. i would also like to stress the contributions of our three rapporteurs. the cope fund, as has already been said, is complementary. it is intended to meet the needs and cover the risk of more serious situations, such as those already mentioned here. there are still many risks to be avoided. of course, maritime transport, subject to conditions of competition, must also be subject to a strengthening of its rules within the imo framework in order to allow its economic exploitation to continue to be profitable without sacrificing safety, not only in europe and the united states, but in all waters and on all oceans. the opportunity and objectives of the agency have also been widely discussed here. i think the rapporteur rightly points out some shortcomings, but i also think that it is not the european parliament's job to run the agency, but to monitor the administrative rules and good practice of this and other similar agencies. the agency must ensure absolute fairness and equal cooperation between member states, and also ensure the participation of citizens, professionals and other economic and social interests. the independence of the agency must therefore be a paradigm, an essential factor for it to be respected, to meet the expectations of public opinion and to develop and promote capacities for action and cooperation at european level. that's probably what's missing. and as many speakers here have said, the seat of the agency can be in many places, there are many candidates and all equally competent and prepared. i think lisbon is definitely the most appropriate.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the specific difficulties of the outermost regions are well known: distance, dispersion and small markets, which increase costs, particularly for transport, and hamper their development. however, on the basis of article 299 (2) of the treaty of amsterdam, which recognises the specific nature of these regions and the need to adopt appropriate community measures, it is possible to take measures to promote sustainable development and to strengthen the productive sectors which generate employment, and it is therefore important to use this legal basis, irrespective of the reference to the articles of the cap, the structural funds or other community policies. it is in this context that it is important to analyse the commission's proposals, which, although positive, fall far short of the needs of these outermost regions, as i have been able to see directly from the entities in madeira and the azores. that is why the work of the rapporteur, mr martínez martínez, is so important, and i congratulate him on his report. in general, the report addresses the issues which the commission has not dealt with properly and which are important for those regions. as regards the first package of proposals, which aims at revising the rates of assistance from the structural funds to increase, it is important that greater efforts be made by slightly increasing the rates of community cofinancing, especially as regards agri-environmental measures and public aid to the productive sector, given that these regions are among the poorest in the european union and their production, processing and marketing structures need to be strengthened. with regard to the second package of proposals, which aims to review the mechanisms for aid to agricultural production in these regions so that agricultural support has a positive impact on farmers' incomes and on lower consumer prices, the report contains some one hundred important proposals, which deserve special attention because of their importance for the portuguese outermost regions of the azores and madeira, the need to strengthen specific support for the restructuring of the vineyards and their cultivation, the production and marketing of potatoes, the livestock sector, not forgetting the question of milk quotas and measures to ensure the viability of sugar refining in the azores, or the strengthening of support for tea, as well as specific changes for madeira wines and support for vineyards. i therefore hope that the commission and the council will accept the proposals contained in the reports under discussion, including those on fisheries.
- mr president, i congratulate the rapporteurs, mr miguel angel martínez martínez and mr bernard poignant, on their excellent work. the adoption of this package of regulations adapting the community's contribution rates to the structural funds and the revision of the posei programmes marks the end of a cycle in the field of agricultural and structural legislation for the outermost regions. from the azores' point of view, this body of rules seems to me to be positive and will allow the region to continue to move towards european development standards. i also congratulate the rapporteur, mrs carmen fraga estévez, on her report. it is necessary to extend and develop support for fishermen and the fishing industry affected by the non-renewal of the agreement with morocco. in this context, i would like to raise again the most dramatic problem facing the azores economy today, which also affects the economy of madeira: the drastic reduction in the quantities of tuna migrating from the equatorial waters of the atlantic to these archipelagos of macaronesia. this is a direct consequence of the increase in fishing effort, notably through the dozens of patrol vessels that the european commission encourages in west african waters, where tuna begins its migration to macaronesian waters. the european commission must immediately assess the effects of its policy, both in the waters of west africa where it is applied directly and in the fishing communities of the azores and madeira and in the processing of azorean tuna canning where its consequences are felt, by reversing its policy of supporting overfishing and industrial and predatory fishing methods in order to defend sustainable fisheries and fishing communities.
in writing. - (pt) the european commission's proposal to exclude selfemployed drivers is unacceptable because it undermines the important objectives of the common transport policy. the proposed scheme creates conditions for distortion of competition and is detrimental to the objective of road safety without contributing to the promotion of quality of service. the position adopted in the hughes report, which we voted for, is therefore of the utmost importance, even though the proposals we put forward in the debate were not fully incorporated. as we have argued, it is essential that selfemployed drivers be included in the immediate application of the directive that will be adopted, or at least that this should be done as soon as possible. only in this way will it be possible to promote road safety and avoid distortions of competition.
in writing. - (de) my group welcomes the fact that the european commission has taken into account the demands expressed by parliament in 1997 during the debate on the harmonization of safety standards and reporting systems and that it has presented a proposal for an autonomous directive on the reporting of occurrences in civil aviation. incidents, faults and malfunctions, which are essential safety information, will now be compulsorily reported in all eu member states. at the same time, all such information should be treated confidentially. the constant improvement of aviation safety is a requirement to which all the parties concerned are attached: passengers, airlines and technical services, as well as responsible political forces such as us, members of the european parliament. this is accentuated by the fact that air traffic will experience enormous growth in the coming years. it is well established that most plane accidents are due to human error. therefore, in order to ensure a systematic assessment of the most diverse incidents and the drawing of conclusions and preventive measures, it would be useful to set up in all member states of the community a single system for the collection, recording, exchange and dissemination of information on dangerous or potentially dangerous events. it will be particularly important, when training staff, to systematically assess inadequate human decisions and behaviour. reproducing situations and simulating situations where decisions have to be made will help staff to be better prepared and to react calmly in unusual or distress situations. in addition, safety risks can be reduced through the systematic collection and storage of all technical problems, incidents or failures in a database accessible to the safety authorities and through the assessment and dissemination of the findings. that is why we welcome the draft directive, but also why we will vote for mr collins' report and the few amendments which have been tabled.
in writing. - (pt) although it is important to ensure good conditions for animals, the reality of each member state must be taken into account. in portugal, where small pig farms predominate, it would not be appropriate to introduce immediately a whole series of far-reaching changes in the farms, which would render them economically unviable. the maximalist measures adopted by the european parliament do not take account of the reality of a small country like portugal. in the short term, their application would lead to concentration of production and the destruction of most small and mediumsized farms and would worsen the situation of family farming. these are certainly important measures for the countries of the north, but they are not adapted to the realities of the south. so while continuing to advocate the improvement of animal welfare and treatment, we voted against this report.
in writing. - (pt) the european commission's proposals for the revision of poseima are far from meeting expectations. in view of the good results achieved since its launch, the profound qualitative change brought about by the introduction of the provisions of article 299 (2) of the treaty on european union and the subsequent declarations of intent by various european councils, much more was expected. the council has not yet adopted the common position on the proposal for a directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the protection of employees in the event of the insolvency of their employer. i would like to emphasize the proposals put forward by the rapporteur, mr martínez martínez, on his own initiative or through the contribution of various members, including myself, with the presentation of 20 amendments to the commission's text, which i am pleased to see included in parliament's position, adopted by such a large majority. nevertheless, in voting for parliament's position, it is important to preserve the following: - i do not accept that poseima is limited to the agricultural sector and, incidentally, to the fisheries sector; - i also do not accept the 'principle of budgetary neutrality', according to which the financial envelope proposed for the future is less than that of the launch of the programme in the early 1990s (still without an article in the treaty on european union); - i do not accept the nature of the proposed measure to protect the main production of the azores: milk. i consider the additional 70 000 tonnes allocated to azorean farmers to be insufficient and i do not agree with the transitional nature of the measure, which is only in force until 2003, which is in clear contradiction with the provisions of article 299 (2) of the treaty on european union. i hope that the possibility of reducing certain measures has been ruled out, as the commission's text proposed. in this context, i would like to stress the opinion adopted by the committee on budgets, annexed to the martínez martínez report, which took account of all these reservations.
in writing. - (pt) the treaty of amsterdam has established a legal basis (article 299 (2)), recognising the need for a different treatment from the eu for the outermost regions in view of their unique and specific reality. at that time, it was a question of recognition of a 'legal and constitutional' nature and not, as in the case of amsterdam, of de facto recognition through declarations annexed to the treaties or instruments such as poseima, which is symptomatically called the programme of options specific to the remoteness and insularity of madeira and the azores. i therefore fully support the ep's favourable opinion on a set of measures which we have just discussed and which have been put forward by the european commission to give substance to the principle of ultraperiphery. finally, i would like to make a special comment on the proposal for the revision of poseima, a programme which has proved to be of great significance in that it has ensured that important aspects of the common agricultural policy are applied in madeira and the azores in a way which is adapted to the specificities of agriculture in these regions. poseima has made a significant contribution to the maintenance of our agriculture. on the other hand, poseima was crucial, because it included specific measures to reduce the extra costs of supply, particularly in terms of transport, food for consumption or industrial processing. the proposal for a revision of poseima, with the amendments recommended by the ep, is very positive. new instruments have been introduced and existing instruments have been modified in the light of experience. i hope the commission will accept the amendments tabled by the ep.
in writing. - (pt) since the expiry of the fisheries agreement with morocco on 30 november 1999, the community fleet has been inactive. the commission proposes to amend the regulation on structural measures with two objectives: to extend the period for granting compensation for cessation of activity until 30 june 2001 and to allow the 4% ceiling for fifg aid for this type of aid to be exceeded, given the number of fishermen and shipowners affected by the closure in portugal and spain. in this report, mrs fraga estévez proposes an extension of the aid until the end of 2001, which seems appropriate in view of the uncertainty surrounding the still remote possibility of renewing the fisheries agreement and the delays in making available the necessary means to cope with the high costs of restructuring the community fleet in the event of non-renewal, which appears increasingly difficult given the blockage of negotiations and the conclusions of the last council of fisheries ministers. the situation in portugal is very difficult, with 30 vessels and some 600 fishermen living in this constant blur. the extension of the aid, even if delayed, could mitigate the consequences of the paralysis or even of a more profound restructuring. that is why we voted for the report.
- mr president, i would first of all like to welcome this proposal from the european commission, which aims to simplify the procedures for small farmers applying for direct aid from the cap, for its timeliness and its meaning. in fact, the experience we have today of the application of the cap is that it in practice marginalizes small farmers, defined in this proposal as those who have received less than eur 1 000 per year on average over the last three years. firstly, because the aid per hectare or per head of cattle is only marginally reflected in the income of these farmers, who often have no more than five or ten hectares of arable land or no more than half a dozen head of cattle. secondly, because small farmers are generally required to do the same bureaucracy as is required for aid amounts 10, 50 or even 100 times higher, sometimes having to fill in a dozen different forms each year to receive an aid amount that is sometimes ridiculous. in other words, the small amount of aid to these farmers, together with the bureaucratic requirements, means that the vast majority of small farmers do not feel at all motivated to use the aid to which they would be entitled. in addition to this positive assessment in principle of the proposal now under discussion, i should like to make three comments: the first is that i think it is reasonable that the amendment adopted by the committee on agriculture should raise the ceiling from eur 1 000 to eur 1 500, which would potentially cover one third of farmers in the european union and in some countries, such as my own, more than two thirds. it is a prudent but realistic start which seems to me reasonable and important. in addition, the amendment adopted here to increase the level of aid by 20% over the last three years seems to me to be important, not least because this level has not yet been stabilised following the reform of agenda 2000, and on the other hand it is also another incentive for small farmers to use aid. but there are still some aid items which are not included, such as olive oil, bananas, tobacco, potato starch, etc., which i hope will be included in the future. secondly, the principle of disconnecting aid from farmers' production choices has some potential, but it cannot lead to disregarding the requirement for a farmer to have any actual production. otherwise, we could encourage absenteeism. thirdly, in future it will be necessary to consider a different scale of aid for small farmers, who will receive a relatively higher unit amount of aid than large or mediumsized farms, otherwise the aid will be ineffective. this is critical. aid for small farms cannot be calculated on the same unit basis as other farms. it will also be necessary to provide for new bases for the allocation of aid, since the average amount received over the last three years excludes those small farmers who grow crops not eligible for aid, but who, in the light of multifunctionality, will also be entitled to receive aid, just like the others.
mr president, the cap policy, in addition to its costs, makes it difficult to grant aid to small farmers in countries like portugal, where 75% of farmers are small farmers. simplification of the direct support system is essential and it is therefore important to make progress in this area, even if only on an experimental basis. the report by parliament improves the commission proposal by extending the scope of the simplified scheme to farmers who receive up to eur 1500 a year and also increasing the aid by 20%. however, given the characteristics of the agricultural sector in portugal, we would have preferred the threshold for the scheme to have been set at eur 2 500, as proposed by mr friedrich-wilhelm graefe zu baringdorf, which would have ensured that many more portuguese farmers could join the scheme. i therefore regret the proposal by mr arlindo cunha, which aims to reduce this threshold to eur 1 500. however, care must be taken that this simplified system is not used by the commission to achieve other objectives, in particular in the context of a reform of the cap, and clear conditions for access to the simplified system must be defined in collaboration with national governments with a view to ensuring the maintenance of agricultural activity. in any case, we support the report that parliament is presenting.
- mr president, the complex situation in angola deserves constant and careful attention, and we therefore feel justified in putting the matter back on the agenda of our parliament, while it is certain that the search for peace and the encouragement of all those who are really seeking it must be a central objective of our institution. but even for the sake of the coherence which is required of it, it cannot ignore its previous resolutions, much less the more or less recent positions of the other community institutions and international bodies, in particular the council, which has particular powers in this field. how can it not and should it not ignore or ignore relevant aspects that have recently occurred and that mark the evolution of the situation in the country and clarify, if necessary, the real intentions of some forces acting in the territory, in particular mr jonas savimbi's unita. that said, i would also like to stress, and i consider it a good thing, that the compromise resolution as it is presented to us has essentially put an end to the attempts to whitewash the angolan situation and to whitewash unita which were the basis of the initial proposal for the un. however, it is also true that, even after the positive amendments to this commitment which we have proposed, in particular the statement of agreement with the position taken by the swedish presidency on behalf of the union, it still suffers from unacceptable positions and serious shortcomings. this is what happens with the obvious attempt to put the responsibilities of the legitimate government of angola and those of unita on the same level, in clear contradiction to successive united nations resolutions which are symptomatically and for that very reason now forgotten; the same applies to the unacceptable omission of the recent and reprehensible unita attack on cachito, which resulted in the death of a significant number of defenseless and innocent people and the unspeakable abduction of dozens of children and adolescents. i therefore believe that it is of the utmost importance and timeliness for the commission to reaffirm the positions taken by this institution and the council on behalf of the european union. we believe that there is no justification for the existence of these positions and omissions in a resolution of this parliament, which is also the main reason why we do not support the compromise. we are also surprised by the stark gap between what was recently adopted and reiterated by the council and what is now being proposed, especially when some of the main supporters of the current compromise come from the same political force that gives shape to a government that has only recently defined and taken up the position adopted by that government. that is why we have tabled a number of amendments to allow parliament to align itself with the positions of the council and the international community and to ensure continuity and consistency with what we have always approved here. the vote will of course determine our final vote.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, we have just heard the talk of war, the talk that has led to war, the talk that has led to the successive failures of international mediation. and that was not our intention. we want to contribute to peace, because there are at last clear signs in angola that something can change in that country for good. that's the big news of the last few months. amidst the news of the terrible war that continues, it is these signs of peace that stand out strongly from angolan civil society that make our vote today urgent. and it's these signs that we turn to to point them out, to embrace them and encourage them. we didn't just want to mourn more dead or mourn more destruction. we want to salute those who with courage, generosity and independence, from the churches and from the many sectors of angolan society, are opening a new road that can lead to national reconciliation and lasting peace. let's hear it for the angolans. they know more and better than we do. the amc, a broad citizens' movement and civic platform recently launched in luanda, speaks of inclusive national reconciliation. inclusive in the sense of not excluding anyone, because it is the poisonous spirit of exclusion that sows war, misery and ruin throughout angola. inclusive because the spirit of inclusion is a condition for reconciliation and because without genuine reconciliation that restores trust there will be no peace. the angolans want peace. let us listen to the angolans, let us trust that internal mediation and the energy of civil movements can achieve where international mediation has failed. let us listen to the voice of angolans, good people, not only those of the amc, but of so many movements that are boiling in a hopeful network of peace: the inter-church committee for peace in angola, the angolan reflection group for peace, the women for peace and development, who are today holding an international conference in luanda, the martin lutter king association, the justice, peace and democracy movements, the sanzala of peace, the messengers of peace, mosaico, and others, such as the forum on the multiple consequences of the war, which has been calling for an immediate ceasefire for months. those are the voices we need to hear. we have to meet these faces, learn these names, spread these faces, shake these hands. because the recent tragic history of angola teaches us one thing: we must only trust those who talk to us about peace without weapons in hand, with gestures and instruments of peace. we must end the monopoly of the bipolarisation of war. i therefore ask you to join me in extending an invitation that i have already asked the president to make so that we can hear here the vision of angola and the future of peace that is aspired to by men like d. zacarias kamuenho, vieira lopes, rafael marques, justino pinto de andrade, william tonnet, chivukuvuku, marcolino moco, cesinanda xavier, and others who join this civil wave for peace. these are the ones who can teach us a lot. angola continues to suffer, but it doesn't have to be this way. this peaceful angola expects us to be able to send out new signals different from the new signals with which it challenges us.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in angola there is still war, destruction and the needless and shocking waste of human life. angola has all the conditions to be a great, rich, developed country and a factor of stability and progress in south-west africa, but it has been wasting its resources and its energies in a conflict which seems to be going on forever. we condemn the use of war. we condemn in particular the inhuman attacks on defenceless civilians, women and children, attacks which can never be justified. over the years, this parliament has spoken out on angola and criticised attitudes and behaviour. we remember those deliberations, which we subscribe to and support, and which the compromise resolution subscribes to, remembers and supports. we are aware of the deliberations of the united nations on the conflict and we are aware that there are different responsibilities on the part of the various parties. but in our view, at this very moment when a small window of hope is opening up with statements from key officials and initiatives in civil society in favour of peace, our role is to encourage this movement. we must tell the people of angola and the political leaders that the international community is ready to cooperate in the search for peace. there is no military solution to this conflict. the general affairs council said so at its meeting in luxembourg on 11 june. today it is the turn of the european parliament to say so on behalf of the peoples of europe. we welcome the compromise text that has been drawn up and we will vote against proposals that might distort it, not because we disagree with the content of several of them, but because what is expected of us today are words of encouragement and not of reproach.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commission representatives, i too support the motion for a resolution on angola, which was the result of a compromise and which is, in my view, a moderate and fairly balanced proposal. it is, in essence, a call for peace which, as has already been stressed by mr josé ribeiro e castro and now by mr carlos coelho, must be supported by the international community and, in particular, by our parliament. the situation in angola is a tragic one. angola is a devastated country, which has been living in uninterrupted civil war for 26 long years. that is, since independence on 11 november 1975, there has been a protracted civil war in angola. this civil war, as has already been pointed out, has no military solution. and the theory that you can come to a solution by crushing the enemy, even if the assassination or savimbi's death happens, is not a solution because the war would continue. the war can only end through negotiation between those who make it and through pressure from angolan civil society, which is finally emerging. and there are independent voices from civil society calling for peace, supported above all by the angolan catholic church, which has played an extremely important role in this, and by all the other churches, which are putting great pressure for peace, that is, for a negotiation for peace. it is this pressure that we must support, both the european union and its body, the european parliament. there was talk of a proposal by some of our friends on the left - the greens and the communist party - who wanted a special condemnation of the cachito massacre. we disagree and have proposed an oral amendment which could be a compromise. this oral amendment condemned all massacres, wherever they came from, because there is not only the cachito massacre, there are also the massacres that have recently occurred, for example in cabinda. so if we only talk about a kind of massacre, we are looking blindly at a reality which, as has been said, is a complex reality and this, therefore, being an apparently humanitarian act, is essentially a political act with which we cannot agree. that is why we shall vote for this motion for a resolution as it stands and not for the amendments which have been suggested. in doing so we believe we can contribute to peace in angola, and i want to support a proposal by my colleague josé ribeiro e castro that the european parliament should be able to hear independent voices from angola who are today fighting very courageously for peace, such as the journalist rafael marques, for example, the archbishop zacarias kamuenho, for example, the former prime minister of angola marcolino moco, and many other angolans who are able and have the courage to come to this parliament and say in truth that the warlords must be put to an end. because if it is true that there are warlords on both sides and that they are both reprehensible, either on the side of the mpla or on the side of unita (and we must condemn both), the truth is that there are interests that win from war, and those who win from war are the interests of oil and diamonds, and what is needed is to bring civil society and the churches to the fore so that this people so martyred can finally achieve peace.
- mr president, this is not really an oral amendment, it is more of a factual clarification which, if there were no objection, i would ask to be corrected in the joint resolution. the sixth paragraph mentions the existence of three million people displaced as a result of the war in angola. the latest statistics and information gathered from international organizations and ngos put the figure at four million. so i would ask that if there is no opposition, the number be corrected: four million people instead of three million.
- mr president, we would very much like to see a perfect and complete commission working document on the settlement of consumer disputes, particularly those involving crossborder solutions. however, as mrs wallis, the rapporteur, very well points out, optimism that comes late is often the enemy of the good that comes along, and we must welcome the commission's limited initiative to chart a possible path for the rapid and economic settlement of consumer disputes of generally low value. as we know, the aim is to set up a european extrajudicial network capable of resolving these conflicts on the basis of the credibility, honesty, transparency and technical knowledge of organizations which have emerged from the free association of consumers, and which are able to form a network of controls protecting the rights of consumers and the average quality of life of european citizens. we are thus moving towards the regulation of the protection of the economic rights of individuals, by de-bureaucratising their protection from the heavy traditional judicial machinery. in contrast to the invisible hand, the internal market for consumer goods is self-regulating, coordinated and supported by the european institutions, thus advancing the credibility of the union itself and its proximity to its citizens. mr president, the way is open, mrs wallis has told us how to proceed, and that is why my group will vote in favour of the report now under discussion.
mr president, commissioners, first of all i would like to welcome the commissioners present and remind the commission represented here that what has happened to the fruit and vegetables sector is truly unacceptable. we have two types of agriculture in europe today: the first, highly subsidized, depends on the support of the cap, and the other depends on the market and the risks that farmers have to take on that market. unfortunately, the fruit and vegetable sector, and particularly the dried fruit sector, is included in this second group of products, a kind of stepchild of the cap who has to live off the market, has to take risks on the market, with all the uncertainties that agriculture has. the common agricultural policy has not provided adequate answers to this problem. and dried fruit, as the commissioner knows, is a product of the southern parts of europe, of very arid and semi-arid areas where there are sometimes few alternatives, often in dry farming. and you cannot even use the argument that it is intensive agriculture and an enemy of the environment, quite the contrary. what we have found is that this sector has been completely neglected for all these years, and we are now in danger of entering a legislative vacuum because the commission - without us knowing why - has decided not to submit proposals. it is truly regrettable that this has happened, and this motion for a resolution which we are tabled here today in parliament is intended to make the commission aware of this gap, this void, and to bring forward appropriate proposals for the fruit and vegetables sector. in this context, there are three situations which i think are fundamental to consider: the first is the need to provide answers, to legislate on operational funds for producer organisations. and of course, as my colleague ayuso gonzález and other colleagues have said here, it is essential that the cofinancing should be increased to 70%. it is essential that the operational funds' operational support be effective. secondly, we must be aware that it is not enough to set up operational funds as the main instrument of agricultural policy for the sector, because there are many regions, such as spain and portugal, where there are almost no producer organizations, where not even 10% of production is included in them. we must think of measures to respond to the producers in these areas, and as long as there are no producer organizations there it would be unfair and unacceptable to marginalise them. thirdly, commissioner, it is time we put in place permanent support measures for dried fruit producers. why not consider direct aid? if almost all sectors have them, why should this sector, so poor and marginal in europe, not be supported? i hope that the commission will quickly put forward the proposals we are now asking for.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the economic, social and strategic importance of the fruit and vegetables sector is well known, especially for the southern countries, including portugal, and it is therefore essential that the commission prepare a reform of the common organization of the market in this area, since the latest date is 1996. it is essential that the commission correct the most negative aspects, including that concerning dried fruit, its poor relative, and increase the limits of aid to operational funds for producer organisations. it is not fair that a sector such as fruit and vegetables, which accounts for some 16% of final agricultural production, should continue to receive only 4% of community aid when it is one of the sectors which creates the most jobs in the european union. the commission must therefore take account of the proposals put forward last year in the jové peres report and approved by this parliament, in particular in terms of raising production thresholds and support for them, increased co-financing of the operational fund of producer organizations and the maintenance of the minimum price in order to ensure the maintenance of this important agricultural sector, which is also suffering from the pressure of imports resulting from bilateral agreements in which, on several occasions, it has been used as a medium of exchange without taking account of the interests of the countries of the south and the importance it has for the employment and development of the respective countries.
mr president, the proposal for a directive to which this report refers is, as we know, aimed at harmonizing the conditions for the provision of services by institutions managing occupational retirement provision and their investment rules, but, more broadly, it aims at creating a single market for supplementary pensions by framing it in the perspective of financial services actions for greater liberalization of capital movements. we therefore disagree not only with the proposal for a directive but also with the report itself, which seeks to reduce prudential rules to a minimum by liberalizing procedures and the investments of funds in more volatile financial instruments, thus placing a premium on risk capital, which in the current phase of financial speculation is a serious risk to workers' rights. for portugal, this situation is particularly serious as it may call into question portuguese prudential rules, in particular the obligation for 50% of occupational pension funds to be invested in public debt securities, and may constitute an unacceptable pressure on the capitalisation funds of the public social security scheme.
