- the next item is the statements by the council and the commission on the situation in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia.
in writing. - (pt) the controversy surrounding the conciliation process of this proposal for a directive, which is clearly visible in the result of the vote in plenary (the proposal was rejected because of an equality of votes), shows that two conceptions were confronted with some practical differences in the process of harmonizing legislation on public procurement tenders. despite the small progress made in the conciliation process in favour of minority shareholders and the adoption of certain rules on the obligation to inform employees, the situation still does not justify all aspects of the possibility of intervention by employees and small shareholders. finally, although the position taken in the conciliation may be of some interest to portugal, since portuguese companies may be potential and easy prey for cross-border bids, there are other, more appropriate ways of defending national interests. that's why we abstained.
in writing. - (pt) the aim of the directive of the european parliament and of the council on company law, relating to takeover bids, is to harmonise the laws of the member states when companies governed by the law of a member state are the subject of a takeover bid and their securities (securities, shares) are admitted to trading on a regulated market. its main objective is to protect the interests of minority shareholders and, by virtue of harmonisation, to protect those interests where the takeover bid has a cross-border nature. thus, by defining a principle of equal treatment of all shareholders, the aim is to create the minimum conditions for all shareholders to be able to decide and assess the terms of the offer presented, while allowing the company to operate during the offer period, thanks to rules concerning the announcement of the offer and the deadlines of the period of its acceptance. that is why i fully support the agreement and therefore the joint text adopted by the conciliation committee, for a directive of the european parliament and of the council on takeover bids, which aims to establish a set of harmonised rules governing the process and ensuring equal protection for all shareholders.
in writing. - (pt) i could not vote for the joint resolution on the gothenburg summit, although it contains various positive conclusions, particularly as regards enlargement, the circumstances surrounding the summit following the lisbon european council, external relations and the middle east in particular, among others. nevertheless, the fact that the majority of meps continue to insist on a post-nice debate model which goes beyond, both in terms of method and content, not only the conclusions of the heads of state and government in nice, but also the treaties in force, especially with regard to the severe criticism of the intergovernmental method and the ambition to establish a broad, even unrealistic, agenda for this debate, has meant that i have had to abstain. i do not think that conclusion 5 is positive either, because it is not the irish government's responsibility to ratify the treaty but the irish people's, in accordance with the constitutional terms applicable in that country.
in writing. - (pt) the fundamental objective of the european central bank (ecb) is to guarantee price stability, which means, in practice, maintaining wage moderation in the euro area. it is a blind objective based on controlling inflation, which ignores the contribution of monetary policy to economic growth and employment. this over-concern can lead to deflation and unemployment. it is on this basis that, despite the economic downturn, the ecb does not lower interest rates to stimulate investment and reduce the costs of excessive debt for households and other economic actors, forgetting that the euro area is not a homogeneous area and that there are specific structural difficulties, especially for cohesion countries such as portugal. it is with this policy of monetary stability imposed by the stability pact, which the rapporteur welcomes, that we do not agree, although it attempts to inscribe the ecb's policies in the context of greater economic coordination and a development of the quarterly dialogue with the european parliament. but the ecb requires effective political control and not just economic coordination increasingly linked to the objectives of the stability pact and the neoliberal orientations of the eu.
in writing. - (pt) regardless of the negative position we adopted at the time of the creation of the single currency, not only because of the sovereignty issues but also because of the economic consequences of a state losing control of monetary and exchange rate policy, with the respective consequences at the level of employment, including the philosophy underlying the stability pact and wage moderation, we consider it essential to pay maximum attention to this preparatory phase of the introduction of the euro. it is essential to create macroeconomic mechanisms that minimise costs and, in the transition period, workers and small businesses will not have to pay the costs of the changeover to the euro, whether through the artificial increase in prices, the costs of the whole process and the attempts at abuse and fraud that may take place during this period. it is particularly important to support effectively the fundamental role of traditional trade in enlightening the population, because it is closer to the people. the traders are a central element in the whole process of replacing national currencies with the single currency, which requires an extra effort, beyond all the enormous changes, in particular in terms of accounting, internal and external documentation, computer technology, etc.
- mr president, it is right to start by congratulating mr thierry cornillet on the excellent report he has produced. i am a witness in the committee on civil liberties to the commitment, the work and the quality that you have given to this report, both in its structure and in its content. respect for human rights is a requirement of our civilization which we must cherish and pass on to future generations, without any distinction based on race, colour, sex, language, religion, public opinion or other convictions, national or social origin. the adoption of the charter of fundamental rights by the european parliament and its proclamation in nice was groundbreaking in that it became a reference text in the field of human rights. it is now up to the european parliament, in cooperation with the national parliaments, to ensure that fundamental rights are respected by the member states and even by the european institutions. the cornillet report follows the structure of the charter, dividing fundamental rights into six separate chapters, as has already been pointed out by commissioner vitorino. firstly, respect for human dignity in the affirmation of the right to life, in particular by stepping up the fight against terrorism, the prohibition of torture and inhuman treatment, the prohibition of slavery and forced labour. secondly, the freedoms, particularly the protection of privacy and personal data - i hope that the community and the member states will take the necessary measures to protect citizens from the intrusion of communications interception systems outside the legal framework, such as the echelon system -; respect for freedom of thought, conscience and religion; respect for freedom among citizens; solidarity with people in poverty; strengthening european citizenship and justice, which involves speeding up judicial proceedings. the slowness of justice is itself a factor of injustice: by not solving the problems it faces in time, the feeling of impunity is widespread. my country, portugal, is no exception here, with its unbearable levels of judicial backwardness. our europe must be a model, an example in this respect.
- mr president, commissioner, madam secretary of state, ladies and gentlemen, my first word is precisely on this question of whether or not the european parliament delegation will participate in the european union's coordination meetings. and i want to say that i was not at all satisfied with the way in which the secretary of state responded to the legitimate questions put by mrs caroline jackson. the delegation of the european parliament does not claim privileges, it claims its legitimate right to participate actively in the bonn conference and not to play the role of an extra, as we had to do during the hague conference. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, climate change is more than an environmental issue, it has become a truly symbolic issue on the international political agenda. if the kyoto protocol fails, the readings will be clear: states and politicians are quick and well when it comes to maximizing the benefits of globalization, but they are unable to agree when it comes to minimizing the drawbacks of our model of society. and the current context could not be worse. on the one hand, action has never been so urgent as it is today: the recent forecasts by the intergovernmental panel on climate change leave no room for doubt. on the other hand, it has never been so difficult to act as it is today: the united states' decision was a major blow to international work that has been going on for ten years. these factors - urgency, public pressure and the abandonment of the united states - make the bonn conference decisive. it will be the moment of truth for the kyoto protocol. our position is very clear: the kyoto protocol is the most appropriate political instrument to achieve this. we are therefore going to bonn with the ambition of reaching agreement with as many countries as possible on the kyoto leftovers so that the protocol can be ratified and put into practice as early as 2002. it is obvious that if we are to succeed in the negotiations, particularly with japan and russia, we will have to give some signs of openness, but signs of openness which will have to respect the integrity and the spirit of the protocol. a final word on economic issues. it is true that in the short term the implementation of the protocol will have economic costs for european companies. however, as the commission report has just shown, these costs will be much lower than initially assumed. i am convinced that in the medium term the kyoto protocol will make the european economy more competitive, given the technological transformation we will be undertaking sooner than others.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use is a very important one. such an understanding presupposes respect for the regulatory framework, existing agreements and between the institutions concerned, taking into account the representativeness of each and the priorities they have legitimately defined. it is therefore necessary to take account of the essential points of view of each of the interveners, otherwise no agreement will be possible. i would stress that the report under discussion is a basis for negotiation with the other institutions, based on the guidelines we adopted in april. the commission has not yet adopted the final position of the european parliament on the 2002 budget. as a basis for negotiation, it should not deal with areas where parliament has the final say. it should anticipate the discussion of matters where positions are more distant from the council's final word. this is the case with agricultural expenditure, security policy and international fisheries agreements. it is only logical that we should also deal with some other key points, such as the level of payments for next year or the content and pace of the reform of the commission. we are therefore in a negotiating phase, i repeat. i would like to stress the main points of the european parliament's position, which you all know. in december 2000, the council and the commission agreed to submit a progress report by 30 june 2000 on a number of issues aimed at improving the level and quality of budget implementation, improving the link between the legislative and budgetary processes and ensuring the success of the commission's reform. this means that it is a priority for parliament to deal with qualitative budgetary issues, to take account of their impact, to take note of them, to take account of budgetary implementation. the commission has already submitted the progress report, which is a matter of satisfaction, and we are sure that it will be treated as the essential instrument that it is and which we have already begun to examine. in april this year, the european parliament also adopted guidelines indicating as political priorities the issues in the progress report i have just mentioned, as well as food security and consumer confidence, linked to sustainable agriculture, the setting of priorities and better implementation of funds in external policy and, in general, better budgetary implementation, very concrete aspects such as e-learning, an essential element of the priority always given to employment and social inclusion issues, the development of asylum and immigration policy. in this context, the concern about the speed of meeting commitments through the prompt completion of payments and, at the same time, the effective reduction of late payments, the reduction of the ral (residual to be settled) is logical. it is therefore clear that the payment possibilities provided for in the financial perspective must be used to the full, and we want to respect the financial perspective for 2002 of 1.14% of the combined gnp of the member states. i also distinguish the following: the need for a precise, rigorous and up-to-date assessment of the implementation of the reform of the common agricultural policy, taking account in particular of the consequences of the bse and foot-and-mouth disease crises, and also a calendar perspective for the future; the respect of the commitments of the european union made at successive european councils, notably lisbon on employment and competitiveness, tempere on asylum and immigration and nice on the consequences of not concluding the agreement with morocco on fisheries; the clarification of expenditure on the common foreign and security policy, in particular as regards the sharing of responsibilities between the council and the commission; the effective respect of the texts of the financial perspective also in category 4, where the european parliament's well-known classical priorities will be taken into account; the precise identification of expenditure arising from the enlargement process with a special focus on administrative expenditure, with a view to the effective formulation of projects and proposals for action, in particular, category 5; the preparation of proposals for action which parliament has already considered in advance. i hope that on 20 july - the date set for the conciliation meeting - the positive and constructive spirit which animates us will be extended to all those present.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european union is also committed to the development of a common foreign and security policy. in the text of my report you will find some data which are already impressive enough. however, the latest estimates show a worsening of the situation. in the last two years, an estimated more than 5 million people have been forced to leave their homes. it is estimated that more than 30 million people are now displaced, some as refugees seeking shelter in foreign countries, others forced to seek refuge in the safer areas of their own countries. africa was the hardest hit continent, but the situation in asia also worsened. look at afghanistan and the refugee camps in pakistan. for example, in timor there are still problems, even though east timor is making important steps towards independence and democracy. as regards latin america, the situation in colombia has become virtually unsustainable. we could mention other dramatic cases, but the basic one is all too clear. in the face of this reality, what have we done? since 1984 the european parliament has been promoting the creation of a budget line for refugees in asia and latin america. but it was not until 1997 that such aid was given a legal basis. and what is the substance, the rationale for such aid? we all know that there are budget lines for emergency aid, such as the echo programme and other, say, horizontal aid. however, they are limited to the first six months. they're trying to ensure a phase that we can classify as survival. the aid line referred to in this report seeks precisely to fill the gap between emergency aid and development aid. in many cases, after a first phase of survival, the population still lacks the organizational capacity to absorb development aid - and sometimes even because of the lack of political structures at the level of the state itself. there is a broad consensus in parliament on the importance of this aid line, as evidenced by the adoption of the howitt report in 1996. however, the 1997 regulation expired in 1999. and what has parliament gotten us, more than a year later? a proposal for a regulation similar to the previous one. at the same time, and in relation to the past, there was a budgetary reduction. it should also be noted that, owing to the commission's lack of planning, the time-limit for parliament's work was exceptionally short. and we have only come to an end today thanks to the cooperation of many ngos, but also of the people who have followed the process on behalf of the commission and the council. parliament has not only worked to bring about greater flexibility, clearer coordination and greater rigour in the evaluation and implementation of aid, but has also sought to introduce in this regulation greater coherence in cooperation and development policies, taking account of the political commitments made by the european union. it is precisely for the sake of consistency that the increase in the funds made available will be important: the commission's proposal is to increase the budgetary resources available to the member states to cover the expenditure incurred by the member states in the implementation of the common agricultural policy. we do not intend to consider that sufficient. but it is important to stress that this is a positive and significant political signal at international level and that it also serves as an incentive for the work of ngos and the united nations high commissioner for refugees. let me also draw your attention to the urgency of the european union's approach to the refugee question in an integrated way. our strategic objectives must be to prevent this from happening again. we must invest in conflict prevention and in creating living conditions that spare local populations the drama of large-scale displacement. we must value the policy of cooperation and development as part of the european union's external policy as a whole, and even as an important source of legitimacy for the common foreign and security policy. the belgian presidency, in making the question of africa one of its priorities, is moving in this direction, and we welcome this intention. we hope to have the support of the commission and the council. it is also because such actions give the common european project greater meaning.
- mr president, the timeliness and importance of the subject now under discussion, which concerns the uprooted populations in the developing countries of latin america and asia, is obvious, and i would like to express my full support for the rapporteur's concerns in general and in particular for a rapid compromise with the council so that concrete support measures can be followed up objectively. i would like to stress, however, my disappointment at the council's insistence on introducing a time limit for the application of this regulation, which is still limited. the problem of refugees and displaced people is a structural and global problem which cannot be solved by specific, short-term measures. the council's intransigence on this issue thus prevents a strategic perspective which was, moreover, present in the commission's initial proposal. we cannot but punish the council for this attitude.
in writing. - (de) my group shares the view of many other colleagues and experts that the financial resources available to the european union in the financial perspective for 2000-06 are insufficient, particularly in view of the enlargement of the eu, the tasks imposed by the stabilisation of the situation in the balkans, the resolution of problems in the field of employment and the stabilisation of social systems, to name just a few. it is therefore legitimate to put the question of additional resources for the european union on the agenda. in her report, mrs haug proposes to abolish the present system of member states' contributions and to change the system of own resources in order to achieve the financial autonomy of the european union. it calls for the creation of a european tax as a direct revenue. this point cannot be approved by my group. the current system of paying a percentage of a part of the gross national product is based on a principle essential for the economic and social cohesion of the union, which is the principle of solidarity. to abandon this principle would be to remove the foundation of the european union. we do not accept that. we consider the idea of supplementing the current system of eu financing with other sources of revenue in the form of european taxes which would not have the effect of reducing the tax revenues of the member states of the european union to be constructive. we are thinking, for example, of the introduction of a tobin tax to reduce capital gains from financial transactions, of a tax on co2 emissions by companies at european level. the latter solution would also have positive effects in order to impose a future-oriented environmental policy: it would increase the pressure for more environmentally friendly energy production processes and would promote the achievement of the objectives of the kyoto protocol. we also think that it would be desirable to change the current financing system so that the financial resources not claimed by the member states are not returned to them but remain in the european budget. at the same time, it is necessary to impose stricter budgetary discipline, for example by making commit ment authorisations which have not yet been paid up, because the grant recipient has not made use of them, expire after three years. that means we have to change the rules on aid. for all these reasons my group rejects the report.
in writing. - (pt) the present preliminary draft budget of the commission, which does not exceed 1.06 per cent of community gnp, represents a real historical minimum. it is therefore unacceptable, if only because it provides for a reduction in expenditure, especially in the field of cooperation with less developed third countries, east timor not even escaping this downward trend. we are therefore surprised at the conciliatory tone of the report and at the lack of strategy and priorities, especially in the face of the constantly announced enlargement, not to mention the social issues which have been completely forgotten. the ep should be bolder and more uncompromising, once again. the presentation of the fourth amending budget for the current financial year shows the chaos in which the community finds itself and also indicates the reasons which led us to table a proposal to reject the budget last year. in the meantime, if the haug report is to be believed, everything could be settled by the creation of a community tax! we think that this proposal is a false question. beyond the questions of principle raised by this proposal, it should be stressed that the reduction in the community's budgetary burden is essentially the result of the restrictive guidelines of the stability pact. it is in this field that the appropriate answers can be found. it is necessary to ensure that the participation of the member states is in accordance with their relative wealth - and therefore on the basis of gnp - and that the effort of the citizens' contribution is carried out in an equitable manner. solidarity is a basic issue which also arises from the point of view of the community's budgetary revenue.
in writing. - (pt) the report critically analyses the proposal for a recommendation on the implementation of integrated coastal zone management in europe and proposes various amendments, most of which deserve our support, whether on the importance of coastal zones or the causes of the degradation and destruction of different areas, as well as the new threats arising from global warming and climate change, which are perhaps the greatest threat to european coastal zones. but it should also be noted that the decline in fishing activity and related jobs, which is a fundamental element of the socioeconomic cohesion of many of these fisheries-dependent regions, makes them highly vulnerable. so, as the rapporteur says, although the recommendation is a vital first step in protecting europe's coastal areas, it also requires commitment from the member states and some coordination between the different instruments to be used. however, we have some reservations about the measures outlined by the rapporteur, particularly as regards a binding common strategy, because of the uncertainty surrounding the role and capacity of the member states to intervene in the whole process.
commission. - the decision on the country to host the 2008 olympic games will be taken by the international olympic committee at its meeting in moscow on 13 july. i have some knowledge of how the international olympic committee works. i stood before that committee in 1990 to support manchester's magnificent bid to host the olympic games. it was one of the first examples i had in my life of what it's like to fight windmills, but i know a little bit about the way the ioc works. the european union as a whole and the commission do not take a position on whether the games should be held in china or elsewhere. the decision is up to the international olympic committee alone, although i understand why members are so determined in their opinions on this. i do not have to explain to parliament that i share the concerns expressed about the human rights situation in china - and i shall return to this subject shortly. just as i have always had doubts about mixing commercial objectives with the achievement of human rights objectives, i also have doubts about mixing sport with human rights. my concern in both cases is that too often we do not achieve our objective, we just mix up the objectives. when i was governor of hong kong, i was very much involved in the debate and defence of human rights and democratisation, and i continued to go to washington every year to plead for china to be granted most favoured nation status, and i certainly did not criticise china's previous bid to host the olympic games. i admit that, on the side, it may sometimes be useful to take action on sport. for example, we cannot argue that taking those measures in respect of south africa during the apartheid years was not helpful, largely because of the importance that the white community in south africa attaches to sport. if we look at the question of the olympic games, although i can accept that there are horrendous examples of the violation of the olympic spirit - and one of the speakers referred to the 1936 olympics, in which my late father-in-law competed - we must also take into account the seoul games. it will not be easy to find democrats in seoul who would argue that the holding of the olympic games has delayed the establishment of democracy and human rights in the country, but rather the opposite, so the argument is perhaps a bit more complex than some of the honourable members have suggested. although these questions are somewhat subjective, in defending this position i am not doing so out of - to use a phrase used by the honourable member - passive complicity. i do so with a moderate but not absolute conviction. if beijing is selected to host the games, it will require some steps, both welcome and necessary, from the chinese authorities if they are to have any chance of matching the extraordinary performance of sydney as host last year. for example, it would be inconceivable for an olympic host country to prevent or be able to prevent its citizens and visitors from accessing the internet news about the games themselves. on a visit to china a few weeks ago, a very interesting visit, it was curious to see that the websites of both the bbc and cnn were inaccessible. i don't think that's possible at the time of the olympics. i have spoken to this parliament on numerous occasions about the eu-china human rights dialogue. i agree with what the honourable member has said: that we must exercise great caution so that the dialogue can actually produce some progress and not become, as i said, an act of complicity in the violation of human rights. our dialogue with china is undoubtedly the most complex and multifaceted human rights dialogue we have with any country, as evidenced by discussions with a country where so many violations unfortunately continue to occur. the european union is constantly seeking to work with china to be more effective and to have a more tangible impact on the human rights situation on the ground. we encourage and support china in its rapid ratification and implementation of un conventions and urge china to cooperate with the un high commissioner for human rights. we welcome the memorandum of understanding which china signed with high commissioner mary robinson and we now expect it to be implemented. in this dialogue, we will continue to advocate action in the areas of greatest concern to us, such as: the repression of political dissent; the suppression of religious freedom; the tremendous and excessive use of the death penalty in the context of the harsh campaign of persecution; the treatment of ethnic minorities, including those in tibet; and the arbitrary conditions of detention. i also raised, during my last visit to china, the issue of the reports of torture and ill-treatment inflicted on followers of the falun gong movement, and there are other issues that we will have to address. there is growing concern about the abuse of psychiatry in the treatment of dissidents and members of religious groups in china. i recommend to parliament an article recently written in the asian wall street journal, summarizing an academic study by robin monroe, a highly respected observer of the situation in china, author of what is probably the best book on tiananmen, entitled 'black hands of beijing', which i also recommend to the house. i share parliament's concern about the environment in beijing and china more generally, and about the effects of the preparations for the games on the city. during my visit in may, i met a number of genuine chinese non-governmental organisations working in the field of the environment. i have the strong impression that china will not be able to achieve the environmental protection objectives it has set itself without the help of civil society, which has mobilised itself voluntarily. it is essential to relax the rules governing the establishment and operation of genuine grassroots ngos in order to bring together the energy and commitment of a population of over a billion people. the same is true of any country or city that hosts the olympic games. the success of sydney was due to the dedicated commitment of its citizens to the organisation of the games and the desire to make them a success in the eyes of the world. any country that hosts the olympic games will have a problem on its hands if it cannot get the support of its citizens. i don't think i'm being naive here. i know there are enormous problems, but it seems to me at least inconceivable that any country like china - although there is no other country in the world like china - any country with the kind of authoritarian and repressive government that china has would consider it impossible to organise the olympic games without having a considerable impact on the development of civil society in their own country. in writing. - (pt) the rapporteur focuses a great deal on the need to reduce the capacity of the member states' fleets and on the creation of sanction mechanisms obliging member states to comply with the provisions for reducing their fishing fleets in accordance with the multiannual guidance programmes (mpps). the rapporteur also points out that some member states have not fulfilled the criteria laid down in the pop. it also calls for more precise criteria for measuring the capacity of fleets. unfortunately, the rapporteur does not mention the member states which have not respected the objectives of the pop. portugal is one of the countries which have met the criteria of the pop; it has not taken the opportunity, as other member states have, to modernise or even increase its fleet. the green paper on the future of the common fisheries policy states that member states which have already made substantial reductions should not have to make any further reductions in the future. the balance between the consumption of fish in a member state and the capacity of its fleet must also be taken into account. on the other hand, reducing fishing effort does not necessarily mean tearing up boats. this reduction can be achieved by improving fishing techniques or by means of fishing ban periods, with compensation for the subjects concerned.

- mr president, we all know how difficult it is to reform an administration: the commission's proposal is to replace the balance based on inertia and the routine of years and practices by another, virtually excellent in principle but unknown and always experimental in its implementation. it is, of course, a brave challenge, and we welcome this challenge. that is why we support the commission's reform initiative in principle - without a blank cheque. but it is worth recalling that this reform is not merely administrative but also political, and that in addition to the traditional balances, the traditional european institutional balance must be safeguarded, and that this is a question of power which cannot be treated as if it were a neutral reform. what is it that we're concerned about, essentially? the question is whether we are moving towards a strengthening of the independence and autonomy of the european civil service (which is a good advice for those who have to decide), or whether discretion in career progression is privatising the hierarchical relationship, making a european civil servant very similar to a multinational civil servant. furthermore, on what objective anchors are the skills to be rewarded based? how can we prevent merit from being diluted in kindness, in obedience to the bosses, by creating a structure of senior officials, some quickly promoted, others slowly promoted? and how can we prevent this polarity from being shaped by considerations of national policy, of defending the national interest, as opposed to the functional essence of the commission, which is responsible for defending european interests? these are the political risks of this reform, our fears, our apprehensions. it is up to you, commissioner kinnock, and to your probity and intellectual honesty, to prove that these risks, these fears, these fears have no reason to exist. but the burden of proof is on the commission and in particular on commissioner kinnock.

in writing. - (pt) this proposal for a directive has been under discussion since 1970 and despite the minimalist agreement reached at the nice european council, with the creation of a regulation on the status and a proposal on the involvement of workers, it is a fact that the first step has been taken to create an effective legal form of a european company. the main objective of the european company is to remove obstacles to the mobility and operations of companies operating at european level by increasing their concentration, facilitating the expansion of multinational companies' activities to different countries of the european union and enhancing the benefits of the euro and the internal market. on the other hand, it aims to create a supranational corporate status, giving european capital an identity and a face. the current regulation thus mainly deals with constitutional issues, requiring that community and national law fill in legal gaps. it is no coincidence that the rapporteur's main focus is on the question of the harmonisation of tax provisions and the creation of a uniform framework in other areas, such as competition, worker participation and insolvency. it is also true that the rapporteur is concerned about the link between the regulation and the directive on worker involvement and is concerned about the situation of smes, but it is also true that the regulation is aimed at serving the interests of large multinationals and unice, which makes the worker involvement proposal inadequate. the amendments tabled by the eldr group are even more unacceptable, which is why we voted against.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. firstly, the nice summit, where the european heads of state or government reformed the community institutions to accommodate an enlarged europe with 12 more countries. failure to do so would mean that the enlargement project would be compromised for many years to come. secondly, the gothenburg summit, which was the culmination of the swedish presidency. in their conclusions, european leaders emphasised that the enlargement process was irreversible, setting for the first time a time horizon of the end of 2002 for the conclusion of negotiations with the candidate countries that were eligible, with the stated objective of allowing these countries to participate as members in the 2004 elections to the european parliament. this political commitment must be complemented by the emphasis on the principle of differentiation, according to which the candidate countries will continue to be assessed solely on their own merits. it is in this context that the report on hungary's accession to the european union and the development of the application process for accession in the last year should be analysed. the european union is a truly national project for hungary. the agreement reached by all the hungarian political parties with seats in parliament in favour of accession and the consensus reached on holding a referendum on this decisive step for their collective future beforehand is proof of this. by channelling all its energies towards this national objective, hungary has already been able to conclude negotiations with the commission on a substantial number of chapters (22 out of a total of 30), which gives us hope that it will be possible to conclude the negotiations for hungary's accession to the european union within the 2002 time horizon set in gothenburg. this, together with the existence of a well-defined negotiating road map, is of the greatest political importance in that it has not only enabled hungarian officials, and indeed the community negotiators, to plan and carry out their work in accordance with the previously established phasing-in, but has also helped to avoid the emergence of fear and disappointment in hungarian society resulting from the inability to establish commitments regarding the deadlines for the conclusion of accession negotiations. mr president, as regards the development of the application procedure, the main points i wish to stress are obviously continuity, although some new situations can also be pointed out. as regards the former, it is appropriate to refer at the outset to the economic situation in hungary, which is continuing to make good progress despite the signs of a slowdown everywhere. gross domestic product growth is around 6%, exports continue to perform well, as do housing investment. consumption, employment and external investment also showed very positive rates. it can be concluded that hungary today has a rapidly developing market economy and if it continues to make progress on structural reforms and consolidation of public accounts it will face without major upheavals the competitive pressure and market forces within the european union. however, high inflation remains at around 10%, and the government must be vigilant by taking appropriate measures to reduce upward pressure on prices. the situation of the romanichel population, which remains difficult despite the steps taken under the medium-term programme, is also highlighted. that is why we point out that this programme must be supplemented by concrete and immediate social support measures in the fields of education, employment, housing and health, among others, coordinated with the representatives of that community and monitored jointly with them, some of which are already under way. the need to step up the fight against corruption, which is fully recognised by hungarian politicians, is also mentioned, with the adoption and implementation of a comprehensive and immediate anti-corruption legislative package. we know this difficult situation inherited by countries with a recent history and location similar to hungary, and we know that this fight must be fought throughout the european union and beyond. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, hungary has recently passed a law on the protection of hungarian minorities living in neighbouring countries, which has caused a number of reactions and controversies, particularly in romania and slovakia, where the largest numbers of these minorities live and work. the hungarian authorities have insisted that this new law has no extraterritorial effect and will have no consequences that are not in full harmony with the legal systems in force in the neighbouring countries where these minorities are integrated. in this respect, the report's message should be reaffirmed, namely that the commission should present an assessment of such laws as to their compatibility with the acquis communautaire and appeal to the spirit of good neighbourliness and cooperation of hungary with neighbouring countries and, in general, with all the member states. the fact that this law contains a provision - article 27 - which establishes the primacy of community legislation over its internal rules and the fact that the hungarian government has expressed its openness to discuss bilaterally with neighbouring countries questions concerning its interpretation and application gives us reason to be optimistic about the resolution of any problems it may raise. mr president, in the short time allowed me as rapporteur, it is not possible to go any further in presenting the main conclusions of this report. we are, however, convinced that our colleagues will read, analyse and support us. in this way, the european parliament will continue to make its indispensable contribution to this process of bringing european peoples and states together again, by making the citizens of the europe of the fifteen and the candidate countries truly aware of the advantages and also of the obligations of european reunification.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr commissioner, enlargement is the greatest challenge ever to the european political project. but enlargement is not an end in itself. it is a means of achieving political, economic, cultural and civilizational ends. enlargement must mean more and better europe, not less europe, even if it is increased in space and territory. enlargement must be supported and well received in national public opinion. many public opinions are in favour of enlargement for reasons other than mere economic calculation and national selfishness. they hope that enlargement will strengthen europe and be fair. otherwise, you will feel let down. for example, and taking into account opinion polls, the portuguese are clearly more in favour of enlargement than - oh, irony! - the belgians, the french and the germans. why would you do that? because the portuguese have benefited from the support of the european union at a dramatic moment in their recent history and they feel that other europeans have that right too. but the same portuguese citizens would not have the same attitude if they feared that enlargement would weaken the european project and compromise europe's internal cohesion. we welcome the orderly, methodical and fine-tuned way in which the negotiations are proceeding, but the hard core of the most difficult dossiers has not yet been opened. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european parliament. that sounds reckless. we must not delay the accession process with expedients, but we must not put our foot on the gas pedal either. the pace of each candidate country must be respected. the famous french writer paul valéry, in the 1930s, famously coined the phrase, and i quote, "civilizations now know that they are mortal". he stressed that europe's main weakness was its inability to constitute a political body with both strong and legitimate powers. that's the problem! we hope that enlargement will help to create this legitimate and strong body and not the other way around.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the bonn conference has sent three political messages of the greatest importance. one is environmental, one is economic, and one is governance. on the environmental front, this conference will go down in history as the one that brought to an end the ten-year-long talks and negotiations on climate change and allowed us to finally take action. it is true that the agreement is less ambitious than we would have liked and that it has been achieved mainly at the expense of the european union's negotiating concessions. but the alternative to this baby step was incomparably worse: without a political agreement in bonn, the kyoto protocol would be dead and we would have to waste another ten years in drawing up a global policy to mitigate climate change. i would therefore like to congratulate commissioner margot wallström on her excellent political leadership of this dossier since her election, and i would also like to congratulate her on her announcement today that she will be presenting to this house the process of ratifying the kyoto protocol and the concrete measures at the level of the emissions trading system and the european programme on climate change. i would also like to highlight the good cooperation that the european parliament delegation, the commissioner for the environment and the council, through the belgian presidency, established throughout the conference. the second message from bonn concerns the change in the economic paradigm: from the combustion-based economy of fire to the carbon economy. in this economy, the ton of carbon has a price, and carbon dioxide emissions represent a cost to be included in the overall composition of all products and in the financial statements of all companies. we will therefore see a shift towards cleaner technologies in all economic sectors. obviously, the competitive advantage will be on the side of those who convert more quickly. and this is precisely where president bush's miscalculation is at, and where, as we saw in bonn, american businessmen are already beginning to worry: kyoto will cost less in the short term than previously thought and will give rise to competitive advantages in the medium term. the third message from bona is about globalisation. it is that, more than an environmental dossier, climate change has become a symbolic issue of the environmental regulation of globalisation. the bonn conference thus demonstrated for the first time that those who have been quick and successful in maximising the benefits of globalisation are also capable of acting to minimise the negative effects of globalisation. this is the great hope born in bonn: it is possible to find common solutions to global problems.
- mr president, commissioner, i shall refer specifically to the collins report. i would like to congratulate our colleague on the work he has done and also the commission on presenting us with this report on the protection of the rights of air passengers. we are dealing with a subject which is of interest to more and more people, more and more europeans, because there are more and more air passengers. but there is also growing dissatisfaction with the transport services we are offered and the conditions under which they are provided. it is the responsibility of the european parliament and the commission to defend the interests of europeans travelling by air. what is certain is that the problems facing passengers do not depend solely on the airlines, they also depend on the airports, and we must deal with both of these situations. today, airports are like subway stations where the density of people is such that there is no comfort and there is no feeling of security. in transport, passengers demand safety, but they also demand to know in advance the conditions of transport. and i am grateful to mr collins for accepting many of the amendments we have made to his report, because i think it was really useful to have them introduced. the passengers must be able to know in advance and compare the conditions provided for them in transport. not only prices, but also safety conditions, comfort - distances between seats, for example - and also, for example, air quality (one of the aspects that has been mentioned is that flights have become non-smoking flights, it is true, but the safety conditions against pollution have been reduced to reduce the costs of air transport). i think we must tackle this problem very forcefully, and i would also like to ask commissioner loyola de palacio whether she still intends to bring forward legislation on this matter by the end of the year and what it will be.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, mr ruffolo's report calls for better and deeper cooperation between the member states in their cultural policies. but it has another merit that deserves to be highlighted: the commission's cultural policy, particularly in implementing the culture 2000 framework programme, should avoid a certain margin of fluctuation in the selection criteria for applications. the commission is bombarded every year with proposals of the most varied nature, the resources at its disposal are ridiculously limited and there are numerous agents and operators whose valid proposals are eventually set aside because of the known limitations. the commission is currently trying to find methodologies which it believes will best address these problems. the follow-up to culture 2000 will be examined in due course and we will have the opportunity to express our views. the functioning of the instruments envisaged in mr ruffolo's report will eventually enable the commission to obtain more precise data on the cultural policies of the member states and on the cultural needs felt by them. a cultural policy at european level should take these elements into account when defining its objectives and narrowing its criteria for selecting applications. it is important to remember that we are talking about a european cultural policy and not national policies. these are also covered to a large extent by the structural funds, so it makes sense to take account of the dual nature of the resources involved, but to separate methodologically the fields of focus of a european culture policy on the one hand and the set of national policies on the other. the european institutions should therefore be concerned primarily with the implications of the cultural heritage which is common to all of europe and should be able to avoid confusion with the national policies which are developed by the member states, without prejudice, of course, to the encouragement of multilateral dialogue between their contemporary creations. a european cultural policy must contribute to making europe's vast and diverse cultural heritage dynamically available to its citizens.

services, whether their own or those of third countries, respects the european convention on human rights.
- madam president, for reasons that are sometimes difficult to admit, europe is unarmed in the face of terrorism. most states do not have anti-terrorist legislation, prison sentences vary from one state to another and the prosecution of criminals outside the territory where the crime was committed is possible - when it is - only through the mechanism of extradition, always subject to the limitations of reciprocity and constitutionality of each member state. the net is wide, in this way, for terrorism in europe. fortunately, the treaty of amsterdam, the tampere council and our commissioner, mr vitorino, have led to progress in judicial cooperation in europe. in this connection, the european parliament recommends to the council, in this excellent report by mr watson, minimum rules for the definition of the constituent elements of crime and its sanctions, the replacement of extradition by the principle of mutual recognition of judicial decisions, the establishment of a european search and seizure mandate which will prevent criminals from making a mockery of the courts and the police, and rules of approximation in the matter of compensation. madam president, apart from these criminal and legal aspects, we must also ensure that intellectual prejudices and outdated historical judgments do not continue to frame terrorism in the heroic and romantic vision of the struggle for freedom. freedom is guaranteed in the states governed by the rule of law, which today are, fortunately, all the states of the european community. françois mitterrand taught us that nationalism is war. and today we are witnessing, very close to us, right before our eyes, in the name of cultures, languages, regionalism and religions, the murder, death and violence that europe cannot tolerate. europe either trusts its values or melts into its antitheses. terrorism is the decisive weapon of madness and of ethnic, religious or cultural fanaticism. it's the opposite of freedom and life. we put him, by force and violence of law, out of europe. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i too would like to begin by congratulating mr watson on his excellent report. he is right to stress the need for effective, clear and rapid measures to prevent and combat any acts of terrorism, whatever their form or manifestation. we reject and condemn all acts of terrorism, which are intolerable because of the blind violence that characterizes them and which mostly affect innocent citizens. we are aware that they constitute one of the most formidable challenges facing our societies. and the truth is that over the past few years, the european union has seen an increase in acts of violence and terrorism. this increase shows that the traditional means of judicial and police cooperation are not sufficient, especially if we take into account that new forms of terrorism are constantly emerging, as in the case of cyber or environmental terrorism. we also know that these acts are increasingly the result of the activity of networks organized at international level, which end up taking advantage of the various legal constraints and the diversity of national legislation. it is regrettable that most member states do not have specific legislation in this area: only six member states, including portugal, have specific legislation; and of those six, only four, which again includes portugal, have adopted a definition of terrorism in their legislation. the amsterdam treaty, by adopting the principle of solidarity in the fight against terrorism, opened up new possibilities for coordinated action by the european union. and with the commitments made in tampere, terrorism is now integrated into the overall approach to the fight against crime. the report under discussion deserves our support, particularly in so far as it calls for the approximation and harmonization of the legislation of the fifteen member states.

in writing. - (pt) although i voted for the report, i cannot but disagree with the conclusion 13 of the report, which is the result of the adoption of an amendment tabled in committee, calling on the hungarian government to abolish the provisions of the penal code, in particular article 199, concerning homosexuals and lesbians. if this were merely a matter of fact, the conclusion in question would not merit any comment. however, after consulting the hungarian authorities, i was informed that the criminal provision in question only relates to homosexual relations involving partners under the age of 18. it is not therefore a standard of sex discrimination, but a standard of protection for minors, which completely changes the meaning of my position. i therefore voted against the paragraph in question, having requested a separate vote.
in writing. - (pt) the report of the temporary committee of the european parliament on the echelon case states that there is no doubt about the existence of a global communications interception system, with the participation of the united states, canada, australia and new zealand, but it seeks to minimize its scope and capacity, it does not draw all the serious consequences that arise from the existence of an undifferentiated communications interception system, which is why we voted against. it is unacceptable to maintain a network that collects and processes, in secret and without any control, information of all kinds, including political, economic and military information, in flagrant violation of the fundamental right to respect for private life, enshrined in article 8 of the european convention on human rights and article 6 of the treaty on european union, which is why this global telecommunications interception network must be completely dismantled. although the report indicates some measures to develop systems and adopt practices to protect, in particular, privacy, against this system (and other identical systems, etc.) and calls for cooperation measures between member states (necessarily at the judicial level and not the collection of information, on the basis of a case-by-case analysis under the control of the courts), or measures to control information systems, in particular at the level of national parliaments, the fact remains that the system will be maintained and will allow the creation of an eu-level information system integrated into the cfsp and the csdp, which is also unacceptable. it is incomprehensible that, in the context of its conclusions, the report does not even call for the united kingdom to dissociate itself from the echelon system and for germany to close the interception base on its territory. it is regrettable that the eu is more concerned about industrial espionage than private eavesdropping and that it can be reassured that in some member states there is a system of spying on other member states, with the participation and possible control of third countries, notably the usa.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the positions of the european institutions are at present very different in important respects. the european parliament has to fulfil certain preconditions: first, to ensure that the budget and its implementation are rigorous and transparent; secondly, to take a position only if and to the extent that we have the necessary information. in this context it is important to make perfectly clear once again the objections we still have. in the field of agriculture what is preventing the new food authority from being able to function quickly; and on the prospects for the development of these issues in the short and medium term. as regards the structural funds, be paid in the same year. in the field of internal policies - category 3 - we find that new expenditure on new actions and new entities is emerging and the overall amount is being maintained. of course, the margin is getting smaller. as regards the external action of the european union - category 4 - the new needs are obvious and cannot be met at the cost of reducing previously undertaken commitments, either by cutting the funds committed or by delaying payment, as is happening. in the meantime, funds are being budgeted for the fisheries agreement with morocco, which we all know will not be concluded, and the aim is to freeze eur 125 million in a category that is clearly in difficulty. as this is non-compulsory expenditure, i am sure that parliament will also take its responsibilities here. in category 5, administrative expenditure, the content and pace of the reform of the commission are limited, security and defence expenditure is doubled and the aim is to cope with enlargement without spending additional money, contrary to the provisions of the interinstitutional agreement. as for pre-accession expenditure, payments are overdue and this proposal does not seem to be able to change this situation. the implementation of previous budgets is not such as to give us peace of mind about the future. in view of the difficulties announced in meeting some old and new commitments, how can we accept that in 2001 almost 10 billion euros were returned to the member states that were not used? on the one hand, there is the debate on more euro, less euro; the commission has also decided to extend the period of validity of the budget to the end of the year. we shall only take decisions on agricultural matters if we know the present situation and have a view to its future development. we cannot accept the level of payments proposed to us, especially if we want to make up for past delays without jeopardizing the present. we want a clear and objective already under way as a result of enlargement. we expect the commission to be reformed and to be made more effective. in short, we want to assume fully the responsibilities we have as a budgetary authority, which derive from our very reason for being: the representation of those who elect us. we will do so by reaffirming our openness to collaborate in the search for the best answers to the outstanding questions.

in writing. - (pt) this report contains controversial aspects, as does the commission's proposal. as the rapporteur for the legal affairs committee says, it would be preferable if the two directives were combined, namely the current proposal on the processing of personal data and the protection of privacy in the electronic telecommunications sector and the directive on the universal service. on the other hand, the commission's proposal to give employers the right to monitor their employees is a complicated and very sensitive issue. similarly, unsolicited bulk e-mail (known as spamming) is another source of concern that needs to be addressed satisfactorily. the position of deco, which supports the commission's proposal, therefore deserves full attention. the commission is seeking to make prior opt-in consent mandatory for unsolicited e-mail, as it appears to offer the most guarantees to european consumers to be able to choose whether or not to receive spam, to avoid being bombarded with unsolicited e-mails of an advertising nature, unless they have given their consent to do so.

engraved on the faces of many angolans, which were destroyed by compromises not made. now, when it is clear that there is no military solution to the conflict (there never was one!), it is said, more than ever, that the key to opening the way to peace lies in weapons. and while this is being said and done, young people are not studying, adults are not working, children are not going to school, hospitals have no medicines and, because of this path of arms, angola has no future. the missionary continues: "but i cannot accept that this should be the end of a country full of material and above all human wealth". that priest, father tony neves, a deep knowledge of angola, ends with a dream of peace, but warns us: "angola, 25 years after independence, has only two alternatives: peace or total destruction". and it is in the face of this that we must be aware of our responsibilities. we can't do service to the warlords, be it diamond or oil. and we can only trust those who fight for peace without weapons in their hands, those who, driven by the churches, by archbishop kamuenho, by the inter-ecclesial committee for peace, rise up in civil society; those who are the moderate sectors of unita and the mpla, like the former prime ministers marcolino môco and lôpo de nascimento, as certain journalists that we sometimes hear here about the persecutions that they are subjected to, like certain moderate deputies of unita: chivuku jaka jamba; many moderate people of the mpla and, above all, other small parties that exist in the angolan parliament and that nobody ever listens to. why do we not listen to these people? like philomeno vieira lopes, like mfulopinda nando vítor, those who have never held a weapon. these are the ones we have to listen to, because angola is not only about the savimbi and the santos. angola, it is the angolans, and we have to listen to the spokesmen of those who suffer and not always the spokesmen who kill. the candidacy of mr zacarias kamuenho is part of that effort. and i appeal to all colleagues that we, as an international community, understand the tragedy of the angolans. the cold war is long gone, it is time for the international community to stop "pouring gasoline on the fire" in angola and to contribute responsibly to peace, to dialogue, to a bilateral, simultaneous and immediate ceasefire.
- mr president, the heinous terrorist attacks that unita of savimbi has been carrying out in angola, first in caxito, then against a train, then against buses, against schools, against hospitals, deserve our strongest condemnation, especially because they are primarily aimed at the civilian population. we are facing some of the most violent and reprehensible acts of terrorism in recorded history. we therefore believe that the european parliament must also express its strongest condemnation and join the international community, and in particular the other community institutions, in unequivocally condemning such barbarism. however, if there were any doubt, these acts have proved in a brutal way the correctness of the positions successively taken by the international community, which, in unison, has called for and decided to isolate and sanction unita. this organization has already given sufficient evidence of being at the centre of the responsibility for the tragic situation in which that country and its people find themselves, in particular by rejecting the election results, preventing the disarmament of its forces, waging war and, more generally, by not respecting and preventing the implementation of the agreements it has itself signed. but now, with such acts of vandalism, it has laid bare its true face even more, and there is no longer any doubt, however small, as to the nature and aims of this organization. and that is why there is no room for equivocation, particularly on the part of the european parliament. any position or initiative, particularly by friends of unita such as mr ribeiro and mr castro, which tends to whitewash, to exonerate or to divert attention from the enormous responsibilities of unita in the terrible situation angola is experiencing, and also and especially those which tend to put the legitimate government of angola on an equal footing with such a terrorist organization, is unacceptable. moreover, it is now more evident than ever that there is no path to peace in angola that does not necessarily pass through the unequivocal accountability of unita, its political and diplomatic isolation, the end of the sale and purchase of the diamonds that sustain it and its definitive disarmament. that said, a few words on the motion for a resolution which we shall be voting on shortly. we believe that its adoption, despite some provisions which we do not welcome, but which are not essential, is a positive step towards allowing the european parliament to return to its traditional position of alignment with the international community and the other community institutions in this matter. but i would particularly like to stress the right characterization of the resolution in calling these acts by unita as terrorist acts. it is the right word and all the consequences should be drawn from it.
mr president, i would like to respond to a personal reference which i consider unacceptable and, in this context, insulting. i totally reject with the greatest vehemence the clumsy insinuation, and the absolutely shameful and indecent claim, coming from the communist party's leadership which has the greatest responsibility in the war in angola, that i am a "friend of unita". i want to make it quite clear that i condemn in the strongest possible terms the attacks on civilian victims, but i am aware of the situation in angola. i do not serve the diamond lords, the oil lords or the corrupt power in angola which is largely responsible for what is happening and the suffering of the angolans. the honourable member should be ashamed of what he says and does, especially as he is the chairman of the committee on development, and he should make a more responsible contribution to promoting the ceasefire and dialogue that the angolans are asking us to promote.
