mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the united states of america and the free world have been taken by surprise by a wave of absolutely barbaric attacks which have claimed the lives of several thousand men, women and children from over 60 countries, murdered in appalling circumstances of horror and brutality. once again, our first word must be, like so many others, one of solidarity and sorrow, our hearts breaking for so many lives lost and the suffering of their families. such attacks have gone far beyond the bounds of the imaginary and confirm fears that the fanaticism of terrorist groups does not yield to any consideration of compassion, raising fears of the worst in the future if not enough is done to combat or eradicate it. far be it from us to want a new schism between the west and the east. we must distinguish clearly between those who freely and peacefully profess their islamic faith and those who, in an absolutely intolerable way, seek in the appropriation of a medieval concept of holy war the inspiration to attack internationally citizens, peoples and states who wish to live in peace. it is therefore necessary to respond firmly to those responsible for these heinous acts, true acts of war against the free world, whether those who directly prepared them, or all those who harbour, protect, finance or inspire them. in this context, the european union has enormous responsibilities as the community that is the custodian of the universal values of life, liberty, peace and security. while promoting relations of trust, cooperation and cultural dialogue with all peoples and citizens of the islamic religion who share these universal values, and with a view to involving them in an active commitment to combating terrorism and the threat it represents, the european union and its member states should establish, among themselves and at international level, new and more effective forms of cooperation in the field of the collection and processing of information in the police field, the reconciliation of judicial and legal systems and, finally, in the field of common security and defence, which help to prevent and allow the severe punishment of all those who inspire this new form of global threat or are its agents. we wish to express our expectation and indeed our hope that today's debate and the conclusions of the european council next friday will constitute a decisive step in a long and difficult fight against international terrorism, a fight which cannot but be considered, at present and as long as the threat exists, as our first priority in the field of european, atlantic and world security.
- thank you, mr. commissioner antonio vitorino. - i'm not sure i can do that.
- the agenda calls for statements by the council and the commission on the future of cohesion policy.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr commissioner, cohesion is a fundamental pillar of the european union. in fact, there will be no political cohesion in the european union without economic and social cohesion, much as some federalists think. but on the other hand, there can be no economic and social cohesion without political cohesion, however much this may displease some sovereignty-lovers. cohesion policy, which has played an important role in recent years, will play an even more important role in the future as a result of enlargement. cohesion policy is indeed a prerequisite for successful enlargement. it's obvious! but enlargement has its paradoxes, mr president: the european union is becoming poorer in terms of average per capita income, and it is making the countries that are currently the poorest richer as if by magic. this is a problem. what my country, portugal, and other cohesion countries cannot accept is that, as a result of enlargement, they are left on the margins of cohesion policy, victims of a statistical mechanism which automatically places them above the fatal 75% threshold. in view of what i have just said, i believe that the future cohesion policy cannot be separated from the following principles: - first, the increase in budgetary resources for cohesion; we cannot avoid this question, despite what mr walter said. - secondly, to ensure that the interests of the regions and countries which are currently the most disadvantaged are not affected and that a fair formula is found to ensure that they continue to benefit from the level of support they are receiving today; - thirdly, a thorough reform of the common agricultural policy and greater protection for the countryside; - fourthly, the definition and implementation of a european territorial development strategy which will reduce congestion in the present dense and concentrated urban centres, to the benefit of the whole of europe.
the commission's report on the activities of the cohesion fund in 1999 shows that, as in previous years, it continues to pursue the immediate objectives for which it was set up, namely to provide the so-called cohesion countries, portugal, spain, greece and ireland, with economic infrastructure in the fields of the environment and transport. in these two areas, for example, the cohesion fund has been crucial in the construction of infrastructure which is indispensable to the development of my region. only in 1999 did it finance the extension of madeira airport and a major solid waste treatment plant. but we have even more reason to be pleased with the cohesion fund's activity in 1999. in fact, the physical and financial implementation of the projects supported, the allocation of appropriations between the environment and transport, compliance with community legislation on the environment and the distribution of funds among the beneficiary countries continued to comply with the fund's operating rules. in addition, no cases of fraud or duplication of funding between the cohesion fund and any other community fund were detected in 1999. and because everything went smoothly in 1999, because this is the last year of the 1993 to 1999 programming period, and because the commission's report gives a general overview of developments during this period, it seemed more important to focus my speech on the contribution of the cohesion fund to the realisation of the principle of economic and social cohesion, of which it is a fundamental instrument. it is true that the principle of solidarity, which is the real pillar of the european construction project, has seen better days. the commission has also been active in the field of structural policy since the beginning of the 1990s, particularly in the context of the doubling of financial resources for structural policies in 1986 and 1992, which was intended to enable the cohesion countries to integrate more fully into the dynamics of the single market and economic and monetary union. but in june 1999, when the heads of state and government of the european union had to decide at the berlin summit on the structural funds to be allocated for the period 2000-2006 under the so-called agenda 2000, there was no longer any duplication. there was even a clear reduction. it was the summit where national selfishness supplanted the european interest in a more cohesive and balanced europe, which had in jacques delors and helmut kohl tireless champions. but today, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the approach of enlargement and the need to combat the enormous regional imbalances which it entails make it imperative to revitalize the policy of economic and social cohesion. we are confident that the commission and commissioner barnier in particular will rise to the challenge. and let us bear in mind that the revitalization of cohesion policy is necessary not only to meet the needs of the countries which are expected to join the european union in the near future, but also to continue the fight against the cohesion deficit of the present european union of 15 countries, which still existed at that time, even after enlargement. the regional asymmetries within the current europe of the fifteen may even increase as a result of the dynamics induced by the enlargement process, particularly in the periphery-centre relationship. in other words, countries such as portugal can become more peripheral and the current centre (london, paris and beyond) even more strengthened in its centrality because it will become the centre of an economically integrated europe from the west to almost the eastern border. it is important that we are aware that excessive regional imbalances constitute an economic and political threat to the european union: an economic threat because if we are not able to make full and more efficient use of the resources of the most deprived regions, it is the whole of the european union that will lose; a political threat because the european union is not viable if it is based on blatant territorial and social inequalities. europe will be united or it will not be. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval. we therefore believe that the creation of the cohesion fund by the maastricht treaty was fully justified. this is also shown by the balance sheet of the first period of operation of the cohesion fund between 1993 and 1999. the cohesion fund, we can say, has been an important factor in real convergence, as well as a strong incentive for the beneficiary countries to meet the nominal convergence criteria laid down in maastricht. we must therefore oppose any attempt to abolish the cohesion fund. it is also necessary to draw lessons from this first period of operation in order to further improve the effectiveness of the cohesion fund.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the european union has played a decisive role in denouncing and trying to solve the problem of the wage gap between men and women in our 15 countries. both the treaties and now national laws impose the principle of equal pay for men and women. but no country follows the laws it has made in this matter. the differences vary from country to country, but the results are common. the feminization of sectors of activity is reflected in the devaluation of wages. it is the predominantly female occupations that are accepted by society as the lowest paid. even in the scandinavian countries, where women have achieved equality, even in political life there is a difficulty in their penetration into decision-making positions in private companies, where salaries are higher. we are therefore faced with a situation of mismatch in which the laws require equality, but society continues to internalize the model that devalues the roles that women play. for true democracy to be established, for a new model of society to be created that internalizes equality, it is necessary to add rights to men in the sphere of their private life. the laws that have been adopted or proposed in this area are quite revealing. for example, in portugal the government recently presented to parliament a bill to make parental leave compulsory for a period of at least five days. it is a law which does not exist in any european country where this leave is optional, and what is intended is to help to revolutionize mentalities. the fact that society continues to accept men's dismissal from the exercise of family life duties is a real unfair competition to which women are subjected in their working life. if we want to change the disadvantaged position that women still have in the world of work, we need to change what is in people's minds and in the practice of the social partners. and here the european union must continue to play a decisive role. at the lisbon summit, europe recognised that it would only be possible to achieve the economic and social development it wanted within ten years if the employment rate of women was increased. europe now has to recognise that it needs not only more women in work but also professions that women can occupy. in this respect, the commission has proposed and the council has accepted equal pay as a priority for 2001 in its programme for equality. the european trade union confederation has also chosen this theme as its priority for this year. the european parliament, in drawing up its own-initiative report ... (the president interrupted the speaker)... i would just like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs smet, and say that i agree with her proposals.
the rapporteur. - mr president, this report is aimed at the recognition by a state of judicial decisions of a court of another member state which decide to freeze property or evidence on its territory in order to ensure the integrity of the judicial process even before the trial stage. this framework decision, which is an initiative of the governments of france, sweden and belgium, is to enter into force by 31 december 2002 and will apply in all the member states without the need for ratification - a very important point! - as was, moreover, the general rule of the mechanisms on the recognition of criminal judgments which have existed up to now. in short, it is a matter of obtaining, by automatic effect of a judicial decision of a member state, the guarantee that property, values or evidence essential to the prosecution and punishment of a crime, which is located anywhere in the european union, is not lost, disappeared or denied to criminal investigation. this mutual recognition mechanism is addressed in the original proposal for a decision on crimes of a cross-border nature, such as drug trafficking, tax fraud against the union's budgets, laundering of the proceeds of crime, counterfeiting of the euro and corruption and trafficking in human beings. the committee on civil liberties, however, considered, and rightly so, that this classification of offences as being limited to types of crime which are particularly relevant to the functioning of the internal market leaves out other criminal offences, particularly, as is appropriate, the one which is of the greatest concern to us now, which is the phenomenon of terrorism. the new wording of article 2, which was also obtained with the valuable help of our colleague ana palacio and which was approved by the committee on civil liberties, is, in my view, much more appropriate to the effectiveness of an area of freedom, justice and security. it is a question of giving substance to the principle of mutual recognition which will underpin the prosecution of criminals and their organisations in a european framework. i think we are all very well aware of its instrumental importance today, and that is why i am calling for amendment no 5 on article 2 of the original proposal for a decision to be adopted by this house and to be taken into account by the commission and the council, in keeping the present proposal for a decision in line with others which will follow it, starting with the fight against terrorism, as announced here yesterday by commissioner antónio vitorino. mr president, this decision is of the right size, it is a small contribution to the construction of a common judicial area, but it is with small things, with small stones that the foundations of a european judicial building capable of protecting citizens are laid. this is how the legal vacuum in europe is tackled and how the inertia and the so-called bureaucracy of the institutions are combated. our job is to prove to the citizens that europe also serves to protect them, that a european civil and criminal legal order is possible. to foster citizenship, of course, but also to counter the faceless and homeless crime that is present everywhere. even the most pessimistic of us will not deny that these are the things that make the right path. isolationist sovereignty facilitates crime and gives life to criminals. let us therefore, starting tomorrow, tomorrow exactly, at the extraordinary european council, speed up the building of the europe of freedoms, that is, the greatest area of democracy, freedom and justice in the world.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, schengen cooperation began in 1985 between five member states. the aim was to abolish internal borders by allowing the free movement of persons and by adopting compensatory measures, inter alia in the field of police cooperation, the fight against drugs, extradition and the creation of the schengen information system. at present, all member states are part of schengen, although the participation of the united kingdom and ireland is only partial. two non-member states, iceland and norway, which are part of the nordic passport union, also participate. with amsterdam, the schengen acquis was integrated into the treaties and certain third pillar matters were brought into the community pillar, such as visas, asylum, immigration and judicial cooperation in civil matters. in drawing up this report i have come across a number of issues which i would like to see resolved as quickly as possible. first, enlargement to include the other member states. while welcoming the enlargement of the schengen area, the european parliament regrets that it has not been sufficiently informed or even formally consulted on developments in this area. as for the partial participation of the united kingdom and ireland, which we regard as a positive step, we have understood it as a provisional situation, a step towards full participation. secondly, the lack of transparency. the integration of the schengen acquis would be expected to lead to a considerable increase in transparency. the results, however, are disappointing. in other words, there has been a late publication of the acquis, there has been an incomplete publication of the acquis, and paradoxically the european parliament today receives less information on schengen than it did when it had a strongly intergovernmental character. thirdly, the incorporation of the acquis in the treaties. it could have made considerable progress, but it has had a chaotic and incoherent result, perhaps in part due to the power of initiative conferred on the member states. on the other hand, because of the council's restrictive interpretation of the treaty provisions, parliament has not been able to participate more fully in the legislative process under the third pillar. fourthly, what i would call the perversion of schengen. article 2 (2) allows any state to unilaterally reintroduce internal border controls on grounds of public policy or national security. they have already been implemented by france, belgium, the netherlands, spain and, more recently, italy. we are not opposed to the existence of what we might call "safety valves". we are all aware, especially in the times we live in, that more freedom must be matched by more security. but we must stress that the absence of a legal framework which explicitly states under what conditions and on what terms this article may be invoked is a genuine blank standard. so in the light of the scoreboard, i would urge the commission to come forward with a formal proposal which includes rules on prior agreement by the council, approval for a limited period, the proportionality requirement and the conditions for renewal of the period. fifthly, schengen cooperation and police cooperation. schengen cooperation is separate from police cooperation, with a separate development of europol. however, these two sets of rules, when considered in isolation, together with the issue of the confidentiality of documents, have made the field of criminal and security matters even less transparent and more confusing for citizens. parliamentary and judicial controls on enhanced cooperation between member states, which form part of the third pillar, remain inadequate and need to be strengthened. sixthly, enlargement to include the applicant countries. this enlargement will significantly change the shape of europe, and the new member states will become responsible for controlling thousands of kilometres of the european union's new external borders. the verification process of schengen compliance has required considerable time periods in respect of the current member states. i therefore believe that we should have two distinct moments: the acceptance of the acquis with accession, and the subsequent full application of the acquis, involving the removal of frontiers as soon as conditions allow. seventh: the sis. it is the largest database in europe, the schengen information system, the first experience in terms of large-scale exchange, of exchange of data of a sensitive nature at international level. in practice, it is expected that in the long term it will become a european information system. sis cannot continue to be operated in secret on a purely intergovernmental basis: the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment and the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. mr president, i would like to conclude by thanking all the members for their help in drafting this report, and in particular mrs van lanker, who has contributed to several compromise proposals.
in writing. - (pt) congratulations to mr sérgio marques on the excellent report he has presented to us. this is a considerable body of work which highlights some key points, of which i would like to highlight the following: 1.cohesion is an objective of the treaties that must be realised as a matter of urgency economic and social cohesion, which is enshrined in article 2 of the ec treaty as one of the essential objectives of the union, requires that existing disparities between the various regions be reduced and possible imbalances prevented, while taking into account the promotion of the harmonious development of the community. the reality is that there is still a long way to go to achieve this goal. 2. the the cohesion fund fills a key gap cohesion is also fundamental to the progress of economic and monetary union, which implies a convergence of the economies of the member states. but as the rapporteur rightly points out, the maastricht treaty does not provide for any support and cyclical adjustment mechanism in the context of emu, which reinforces the importance of the structural funds and especially the cohesion fund. 3. the the objective of cohesion does not end with enlargement the rapporteur draws our attention to the fact that disparities between the current member states could increase as a result of the impact and dynamics of enlargement. this must be prevented and prevented from happening; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to give its opinion on them. not only does the objective of cohesion not end with enlargement, but it could and should be further emphasised afterwards. enlargement will thus represent a major challenge for solidarity in the union, which requires careful and effective preparation, taking into account the union as a whole and its specificities.
in writing. - (pt) the 11th annual report on the structural funds (1999) brings to an end the 1994-1999 programming period and, hence, the delors ii package, which made a decisive contribution to regional development, both in terms of infrastructure and production and in terms of social integration. after the end of the 1994-1999 period, the 11th report shows that the situation of cohesion remains unsatisfactory. despite the budgetary effort made under the edinburgh package and the progress achieved, the european union still has large regional asymmetries in economic and social development, as there are still asymmetries within the european union between the peripheral regions (including the outermost regions) and the centre. the application of the structural funds is not sufficient to correct the income disparities between the richest and the least developed territories. in this context, i would like to support the text of mr nogueira román, and in conclusion i would like to stress that the remoteness and insularity of the outermost regions can be offset by the development of activities financed by the structural funds to increase the competitiveness of the production chain, to facilitate access to information and to integrate these regions into the context of an enlarged european union. this will require the strengthening of the current cohesion policy, which is even more necessary to meet the challenges of enlargement.
in writing. - (pt) as we know, although the principle of equal treatment for men and women for equal work was enshrined in ilo convention 100 in 1951 and in the treaty of rome in 1957, and then developed and improved in other community legislation, discrimination persists, as the smet report tells us. these disparities are on average 28% in the european union. even after taking into account structural differences between men and women in the labour market, such as age, education, occupation and career pattern, women's wages remain on average 15% lower than men's. the only explanation lies in the mechanisms of value discrimination, which is unacceptable. the problem of wage discrimination against women, which persists in practice, and its mechanisms are analysed in a relatively thorough manner, although there are still many shortcomings, particularly in the statistical information of the european union and the member states. but, as the rapporteur says, one thing is clear: the wage gap can only be reduced by a policy based on two pillars: on the one hand, by seeking to improve the position of women on the labour market and, on the other, by eliminating discrimination in the process of wage setting. we therefore consider it important to give full priority to this issue, including the commission's proposal to launch a european-wide campaign on equal pay in 2002. but it is also important to put forward a proposal for the revision and updating of the 1975 directive on equal pay.
in writing. - (pt) we voted in favour of this report because we believe that it correctly addresses the serious problem of moral harassment, including sexual harassment, in the workplace. the report mentions a survey carried out by the dublin foundation among 21 500 workers in the european union, in which 8% of those interviewed said they had been the victim of moral harassment in the last 12 months. if we extrapolate this figure to the total number of workers, it means that probably more than 12 million workers are affected in all 15 member states. the report says that although the true extent of this phenomenon is not known, it is a serious problem in working life. it is also clear that the increase in fixed-term contracts and the precariousness of employment, especially for women, exacerbates the conditions conducive to the practice of various forms of harassment. we therefore support the measures proposed in the report to combat this phenomenon, including those concerning prevention, the development of knowledge, the exchange of positive experiences and the presentation of a green paper by march 2002 at the latest, followed by an action programme containing community measures against moral harassment in the workplace.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, more and more european citizens are affected by environmental noise, but despite this the european union has been slow to implement a common noise policy. i therefore agree with the ambitious options formulated by the rapporteur, mr alexander de roo, and i congratulate him on his work. i supported your report in the committee on the environment and i support it again. let me highlight a few aspects. firstly, i agree with the transformation of this directive into a binding framework directive which will later give rise to specific directives on noise caused by cars, motorcycles, trains and aircraft. secondly, i also agree with the imposition of common noise limits in the vicinity of all civil airports in the european union. not only because the noise caused by night air traffic is the most disturbing factor for the sleep of citizens, but also because, in the absence of common standards in the european union, we have seen situations of a clear distortion of competition between airports and a lack of fairness towards the citizens of the different member states. thirdly, i welcome the amendments to the common position on noise indicators. the amendments allow member states to shift up to two hours from the evening to the daytime or nighttime period, which allows noise legislation to be applied without affecting the habits and customs of each country, and in particular those in southern europe. this new legislation must be implemented as soon as possible, but to comply with it requires that member states invest very quickly in the technical and human resources needed to monitor and control it. otherwise, we'll have a good law once again, but one that nobody applies.
- mr president, despite 11 september, the commission's communication on asylum is, in my view, still, in essence, not only appropriate but more necessary than ever. the report by mr evans has not lost its relevance or its substance. i don't think this is the time to back down; it is therefore urgent to seek a common solution in all the member states as regards the criteria for evaluating applications and cooperation at the level of national administrative systems. i recognise that we will have to face some challenges to achieve standards with useful effect. this effort should not only be made on the european level, but also through cooperation with third countries, mainly the countries of origin of asylum seekers. in order to avoid the kafkaesque situations that we often find described in the european press, we urgently need - and we are not asking for much - minimum rules for faster and simpler asylum procedures to prevent the delays and uncertainty to which asylum seekers are subjected. the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice inevitably requires the mutual recognition of judicial decisions. but to reach that level we need first to harmonize the asylum appeal procedures. mr president, this communication, which is the responsibility of commissioner antónio vitorino, is very timely on the agenda. on the basis of the framework defined by tampere, we are embarking on the creation of a common policy on asylum which i am sure will have the commitment of the council and parliament. i believe that in a joint effort with the community institutions and civil society we will reach a level of understanding so that by 1 may 2004 the objective set by the european council will have been achieved. mr president, history calls for a decision. there's no time for hesitation. i would like to thank the commissioner for his report.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are well aware that this debate is about one of the most important and 'hot' issues at european level: european asylum and immigration policy. this is due to two distinct factors: firstly, because the treaty of amsterdam has given the community new powers, opening up new possibilities for action at european level; secondly, because there has been a considerable increase in migratory pressures from third countries, aggravated by some dramatic events of recent times, such as the massive influx of refugees caused by the kosovo crisis or the dover tragedy. it is therefore necessary, for both humanitarian and economic reasons, to define a comprehensive policy to manage these migratory movements effectively, which is a gap which the european union cannot fail to address at the present time. it is important to bear in mind that there are three different types of people in this problem: asylum seekers, temporarily displaced persons and economic migrants. if the symptoms are similar, it is important to separate the different causes and find different responses. confusing these statutes does not help solve the problems, but rather aggravates them. we also know that this external pressure is accompanied by internal pressure from states facing population decline caused by either slowing population growth or a decline in the working age population, an increasing population over 65 years of age and growing concern about the sustainability of social security systems. the response cannot consist in vain attempts to prevent and stop immigration at european borders; it is, on the contrary, to manage and regulate it. the need for a common, orderly, clear and coherent european policy to manage migration flows is therefore clear. to avoid new problems, it will be necessary to take account of the real needs of the labour force in each member state and the resources available for the reception and integration of these immigrants. naturally, we are closer to the approach taken by mr pirker. i take this opportunity to congratulate commissioner antónio vitorino on his announcement today that he will present within a month two initiatives on combating illegal immigration and repatriation. finally, i would like to stress that we need a prudent approach to a delicate problem that requires intelligent answers. it makes more sense, for example, to give priority to the legalisation of immigrants who can be integrated into the labour market and who are already in the community territory than to encourage a new wave of immigration.
- the next item is the report (a5-0309/2001) by mr titley, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy, on the second annual report drawn up pursuant to point 8 of the operative part of the european union code of conduct on arms exports (13177/1/2000 - c5-0111/2001 - 2001/2050 (cos)).
- mr president, until a few weeks ago the distinction between conventional weapons, including light and heavy weapons, and weapons of mass destruction, i.e. nuclear, chemical and biological weapons, seemed relatively clear. now it is the very concepts of armament that are at stake, when a civilian aircraft can become a missile provided it is guided by an absolutely destructive purpose. we can be tempted to adopt an attitude that is both relativistic in terms of means and ultra-securitarian in terms of procedures. i believe that, on the contrary, the need to avoid politically unmoored derivatives is more important than ever. the need to speed up the construction of a common foreign and security policy with the appropriate means and backed up by effective joint commitment is, i believe, becoming clear at the moment we are going through. this policy must be based on cohesion, which also means trust between the member states, and on consistency between stated aims and concrete action. we are faced today with a subject, the export of arms, which is more appropriate than ever to apply these principles. it would, of course, be better not to have to talk about guns, but unfortunately, that is not the world we live in. the european union code of conduct on arms exports adopted in 1999 is a basic instrument for rationalization and control in this area. the second annual report from the council on the application of this code of conduct, which the rapporteur himself welcomes, calls for greater transparency and convergence in the arms export policies of the member states and for third countries to adopt the common principles, but also stresses the need to go further by combating illegal trade by exercising greater control over the brokering and the destination of arms. i would like to support the content of this report and stress once again the need for consistency and cohesion in this matter.
- thank you very much, commissioner christopher patten. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
- mr president, the durban conference was an important moment in the denunciation of the phenomenon of racism and xenophobia, and it resulted in a clear and strong message whose need and urgency remain unquestionably timely. the 20th century has witnessed the most horrendous crimes perpetrated in the name of racist doctrines, which, it should be borne in mind, took place in europe, whose civilization was the cradle of the great declarations of rights. but the durban conference was much more than an exercise in the duty of memory. durban was a reminder that the modern world has proved incapable of eradicating racism and xenophobia from its societies, which, even in europe, continue to cause victims and to be the source of violent conflicts. unfortunately, some have succumbed to a manichean and historically lax view which holds europe disproportionately and simplistically responsible for all the evils and oppressions associated with racism, such as the use of slavery. europe does not throw away its history, whether in its moments of light or in its moments of shadow. but you must not consent to gross manipulation. europe must not accept that history is being put to the test, that the past is being put on trial, so that this innocent judgment of the present time hides the present responsibility for the persistence of the abject and anachronistic phenomenon of slavery and, more generally, the systematic violation of fundamental rights. judging history through the eyes of the present is a pernicious exercise in demagogy that simplifies the complex and thereby offends the truth. manichaeism tends to segregate fanaticism and mobilize all kinds of crusaders who read history as a long struggle between good and evil. the fanatic, by definition, knows no interlocutors, only enemies. history is not a summation of criminal offences and civil damages provided for in existing legislation. we refuse this useless exercise. the european union's political responsibility in upholding the universal values inherent in human dignity is both internal and international because it is inherent in the very essence of our common project. our collective european responsibility is expressed in the unreserved confirmation of our place at the forefront of the fight for the universal affirmation of human rights and against racism and xenophobia and in the determination to persist and deepen a constructive role on the international scene for the liberation of humanity from all discrimination based on hatred, prejudice and ignorance.
- madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the development of a european satellite radio navigation programme raises a number of questions which deserve our consideration. it is a world-wide project using state-of-the-art technology and involving vast amounts of public and private financial resources. the strategic importance of the political decisions to be taken in the context of the project explains to a large extent the hesitations which the council has shown, despite the commitment and enthusiasm which both the commission and the european parliament have shown in their statements and documents. in fact, the problem is not minor. i am sure that is because a project of this magnitude requires close international cooperation. this cooperation is essential not only because of the future installation in various regions of the world of control centres and communications equipment to support the approximately thirty satellites which will also have to be placed in space orbit, but also because the success of a project of this nature obviously depends on its interoperability with the other existing systems: the us gps and, to a more limited extent, the russian glonass. it is therefore pointless for us to base the argument and the need for a system like galileo on any feeling of mistrust in europe and among europeans towards the united states because of the quasi-monopoly position that this country currently enjoys. and that could lead them to unilaterally and without plausible reason, that is irresponsibly, interrupt the transmission of satellite data, or to introduce unreasonable degrees of gradation in the accuracy of this data for civilian users. the truth is that gps and the satellite radio navigation it enables have been used successfully all over the world and particularly in europe. in addition, the reception of gps signals has been completely free of charge for users, and any citizen can access the necessary sectors at affordable prices. the reasons why the european union should opt for the definition 'development, installation and operation' of such a system are therefore of a different nature. they relate mainly to the boost that galileo would give to the european high-technology industry and the providers of the related services by introducing a factor of economic competition in this sector of activity which is decisive for its development. this system would also be of great help in developing europe's technological potential and in strengthening the academic and applied research associated with it. it is also worth emphasizing the valuable contribution that a project of this size would make to the creation of new jobs (over 100 000), and we are talking about highly skilled jobs. and finally, the installation and operation of the system would allow the development of a public or private partnership of enormous strategic value to support such important sectors as transport (be it sea, air or land), air traffic management, search and rescue, meteorology, mobile telephony and many others. it is for this set of reasons, briefly summarized, that we support the galileo project, hoping to contribute to the construction of the real europe of the future.
- mr president, europe was asleep. his only dream seemed to be comfort and peace. suddenly, the shock of the brutal terrorist attacks in new york city shook her confidence and her hedonism to its core. europe suddenly felt vulnerable and unprepared. after all, their hard-won fundamental freedoms were threatened by mysterious terrorist networks, in which, however, islamist religious proselytism seems to predominate. this violent, anti-modern proselytism is by definition anti-western. however, this does not justify the cultural cretinism and historical ignorance which leads to an abusive conflation between islam and the aberrant, irrational and sectarian expressions of the perpetrators of the attacks. it was not just the united states, the target of the sinister attacks, that was put to the test. europe has also been tested and the european union must make a deep examination of conscience. the european union was at first, still in shock, up to speed with the events at the brussels summit. you were quick, clear and accurate in your emergency response. but what now? europe cannot henceforth take a superficial, opportunistic and immediate approach. europe cannot be a toy of fate, a fate drawn by others. what is required in ghent and laeken is that europe should rethink its project and its role in the face of the new world context and adopt a clear guideline for the troubled times ahead. without becoming pessimistic about the human condition, europe must be less naive about the dangers that lie ahead. europe must be more united economically, politically, militarily and in its internal security. it's obvious. but how can we do that? what are your priorities beyond the unbridled fight against terrorism? what are the new aims of the european project? will enlargement continue on the same terms? will it not be necessary to speed up the application of turkey to the european union, for example? is it not the most basic common sense to give absolute priority to the mediterranean strategy and to give a new impetus to partnerships with muslim countries?
- mr president, given the gravity of the terrorist attack of 11 september, there are several important issues to be discussed and clarified, related to the will to combat terrorism in practice. the first is the need to find effective means of combating terrorism, which do not necessarily require legislation in a security sense. it is important to remember that it is not, and never has been, effective in a democracy to reduce the scope of freedom in order to combat the enemies of democracy itself. we think, in fact, that the problem is something else. it is not just the lack of legislation at national or european level, or the lack of coordination of policing at international level. the problem has much to do with the hesitations and weaknesses in the fight against terrorism, which have been manifested in the laxity towards terrorist activities, in the hope that terrorist acts would always be manifested in the next country, and in the slow deterioration of the means of defence and security, of police, of intelligence services and of armed forces. european governments which thought that wars were over for good and which often cut off the financial resources available for defence and security cannot speak out against terrorism. the second question has to do with the political consciousness of the moment we are living in. if we denounce what happened in new york and washington as just a criminal act, we will be limiting the scope, the significance and the effectiveness of the fight against terrorism from the very beginning. what has happened is indeed an act of war, certainly a war unlike any other, but unfortunately all wars are unlike any other. they are usually more deadly, hitting more civilians, not hesitating to use any means of slaughter, they are increasingly total wars. to classify what happened as an act of war is not a mere semantic question. this has important consequences, starting with the right of the nation or nations affected to respond with military action, if necessary, against the country or countries found to have responsibilities in supporting or carrying out terrorist acts. at the moment, this is an essential condition for effectively combating terrorism, which cannot reach the dimensions of the september 11 attack without the support of one or more states. unfortunately, there has been little discussion on this subject, but it is necessary to know what the position of the council, parliament and the commission is on the need for military intervention against states which commit or support terrorist acts.
in writing. - (pt) the report comments on a commission communication adopted in november 2000 on immigration, which considers immigration from an essentially economic perspective, linked to employment prospects, which may create conditions for the establishment of immigration quotas in the european union. it seems that the european union only wants to use the labour force of third countries to satisfy the needs and shortcomings of european capitalism, while remaining indifferent to the obstacles it can create for the development of these countries, by taking away essential human resources. it is essential to adopt a new approach to cooperation and development policy, particularly with third countries, in order to contribute more effectively to their development and to the establishment of decent living conditions for their populations, which is unfortunately not emphasized in the report. as far as immigrants are concerned, all their rights must be guaranteed and respected. hence our proposals to defend the legalisation of all immigrants, our insistence on the need to recognise their civil and political rights, including the right to vote in local and european elections, and to defend equal treatment in economic, cultural and social rights.
in writing. - (pt) our vote expresses the conviction that it is worth having a european project that works well and is independent of military and defence uses, including from third countries, which is not the case today, given europe's dependence on the united states of america. in the meantime, it is essential that the implementation of the project respects the rights, freedoms and guarantees of citizens, including their privacy. its creation may be an advantage for mobile telecommunications services, which can use it in location applications requested by users, if the financial cost does not make this use prohibitive. it is also necessary to take account of the costs of financing it, in particular the part which would be required of countries not directly involved in its construction, so as not to hinder its implementation in the various member states.
- mr president, we are living in a time when it is becoming clearer that the stability pact is acting as an obstacle to development and economic and social cohesion: the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a step in the right direction. in several countries and from various sources, warnings are being issued about the need to amend the stability pact and the priority being given to the monetary policies of neoliberalism. this is felt both in the more developed countries and in those where situations of deeper inequalities persist and/or where threats of social tensions arise. in a country like portugal, with the lowest wages, pensions and pensions in the european union and the highest rates of early school leaving, accidents at work and poverty, it is not acceptable to apply rigid timetables and deadlines and to impose severe restrictions on public spending and investment, otherwise we will impede their development and boycott the path of economic and social cohesion. the need for the immediate suspension of the stability pact is therefore on the agenda. in a european union which says it is concerned about social problems, committed to creating jobs and combating social exclusion and poverty, it cannot continue to give priority to maintaining monetary stability at all costs, when it knows that the consequences will be more unemployment, more wage restraints and more poverty. it is not just a question of defending some relaxation of the criteria of the stability pact and the implementation of the stability and growth programmes, which each country had to submit. it is essential to undertake a thorough review of the objectives, criteria and foundations of the stability pact, on the basis that the budgets of the member states should reflect the needs of each people and each country and not the interests of the financial groups which the european central bank seeks to express.
- mr president, commissioner, europe is not technically in recession, but it is clearly experiencing a slowdown in growth, and the full economic, political and social consequences of this are not yet foreseeable. it is in this context that many people are asking themselves whether the restrictive and fundamentalist interpretation of the stability and growth pact is valid and what consequences this interpretation may have for the process of development and cohesion in europe itself. the stability pact is a global commitment that combines the realism of a concrete macroeconomic scenario with a healthy concern for consolidating public finances. the covenant must be interpreted as a whole and, of course, if one of the terms of the equation changes, the other must be adjusted and made more flexible. i do not want to get into a debate with the guardians of the school of single thinking who have unfortunately become protagonists on the scene of european economic policy. but i cannot help saying that the real defence of the stability and growth pact comes first of all from understanding it. understanding this means that it is right to accept, in an unfavourable international environment, the inevitable loss of revenue and the consequent increase in the public deficit, provided that it does not exceed 3%, as the honourable member has rightly said. this cannot and should not be confused with any permissiveness or inefficiency on the part of the tax administrations, let alone with the abandonment in the medium term of a policy of adjustment based on budgetary procedures and on a serious and continuous effort to control public expenditure and to improve the efficiency of administration at all levels. the control and rationalisation of public expenditure is a right objective. the revision or annulment of the stability pact is therefore not in question. consolidating spending is not an irrational demand for a linear reduction in deficits. it is to consider that in the medium term budgetary balances should lead to an addition of zero profit.
in writing. - (pt) we reaffirm our categorical and unequivocal condemnation of the terrorist acts that have been perpetrated in the united states of america, and the need to develop a sustained response by combining efforts and international cooperation within the framework of the united nations and in full respect of its charter. but we strongly disagree with the method the commission has tried to impose by presenting the 'proposal for a regulation concerning restrictive measures directed against certain persons and entities in connection with the fight against international terrorism'. in the fight against terrorism, we consider it necessary to adopt measures to combat organised crime, including the fight against money laundering and various forms of trafficking, and the abolition of tax havens. but we consider it unacceptable to ask the european parliament to approve a list of organisations and persons - possibly provided by the government and intelligence services of the united states of america, a country which was hardly opposed to the control of tax havens - without presenting any substantial evidence. secondly, we believe that the necessary fight against terrorism cannot be used as a pretext to touch upon essential aspects of the sovereignty of the member states of the european union.
in writing. - (pt) while reaffirming our categorical condemnation of the terrorist acts perpetrated in the united states of america and reiterating our respect for the pain and grief of the families of the victims and the american people, we call for the fight against terrorism not to contribute to more innocent victims. the fight against international terrorism will only be fully effective if it is placed under the aegis of the united nations, which is why we reject in particular the use of article 5 of the nato treaty, which risks dragging the european union and the international community into a spiral of violence with incalculable consequences. we also believe that the necessary fight against terrorism cannot be used as a pretext to call into question the rights, fundamental freedoms and judicial guarantees of individuals, nor to touch upon the essential aspects of the sovereignty of the member states of the european union, including that of militarisation. we do, however, consider it important, alongside the fight against international terrorism, to develop the fight against money laundering, including the abolition of tax havens. so our vote reflects the deep disagreement on some essential aspects of the joint resolution voted by a majority in the plenary, although we supported the whole of that resolution.
in writing. - (pt) japan is already experiencing economic recession. the united states is just a stone's throw away from it. europe is growing much less strongly, as the possibility of recession is not excluded. in order to avoid a global recession, in a coordinated action with japan and the united states, economic activity should be stimulated through the growth of public expenditure up to a ceiling of 3% of the public deficit in each eu member state, provided that: - the increase in expenditure is aimed at strengthening public investment and stimulating private investment; - a simultaneous redoubled effort is made to achieve structural reforms that are favourable to employment, investment and competitiveness. the stability and growth programme must be relaxed beyond the 'automatic stabilisation' that follows from it, which is why i abstained when voting on the resolution tabled by the epp.
in writing. - (pt) i voted for the excellent report by mr peijs for the following reasons: as we all know, over the last 30 years there has been an excessive increase in public spending in europe, which has forced member states to raise taxes to unsustainable levels. it is therefore essential and urgent to achieve tax relief by putting into practice the necessary reforms to this end, in order to improve incentives for employment and entrepreneurship. the attempt to define what a sustainable tax relief should be is therefore to be welcomed. i hope that the commission will continue its efforts to guide national governments towards the design of these reductions, in particular through the creation of a 'code of conduct'. as regards ensuring that a greater proportion of the working population has access to the labour market, it is essential to remove the social, fiscal and administrative barriers which prevent women and older people from returning to work and/or remaining in employment. this is mainly because demographic change is characterised (as the 2000 un report clearly shows) by a slowdown in the growth of the active population and an increase in the population aged over 65, which poses the serious problem of maintaining the social security system or even the possible economic damage resulting from a shortage of labour in our labour markets. i also think it is important to give priority to young people's access to the labour market, as well as to lifelong learning, which requires increased public and private spending on education, research and development, accompanied of course by the respective structural reforms, in order to make an effective transition to a growth-based society.
in writing. - (pt) despite the difficult socioeconomic situation in the various member states, the report stresses the need to continue the effort to consolidate the budget and to respect the stability pact. it is a blind position, which we propose to eliminate. the stability pact must be suspended immediately and its objectives, criteria and foundations thoroughly reviewed. we also disagree with the idea that public investment is not a way out of the crisis or that it is generally poorly managed and targeted, which is why we table an amendment underlining the importance of public investment for economic growth and employment. a certain commitment to solving social problems, creating jobs and combating social exclusion and poverty means that we should not continue to prioritise the maintenance of monetary stability at any cost, knowing that the consequences will be more unemployment, wage restraints and poverty. unfortunately, the various amendments we tabled concerning a major revision of the report were not accepted.
in writing. - (pt) what emerges from this report is a whole set of pressures for an acceleration of the liberalisation process in different sectors. it even criticises the stockholm european council for not having set a timetable for the final liberalisation of gas and electricity markets, which is unacceptable. we also reject its call for the member states to liberalize the energy, transport and postal markets rapidly and uniformly. it is not enough for the commission to attack public monopolies, particularly in the electricity sector, to call for the liberalisation of local calls or to consider that privatisation and liberalisation of public services lead to better prices and quality.
in writing. - (pt) the service sector is of fundamental importance for the future development of the european union's economy. it already accounts for the largest share of gdp and more than two thirds of employment. it should therefore be given the utmost attention in order to improve its quality and maintain employment. however, the commission's main concerns, as set out in its communication, are to create an internal market for services, analyse the obstacles to the free movement of services across national borders, propose their elimination and act to accelerate the liberalisation of postal services, distance selling of financial services, tendering and the value added tax on digital products. the rapporteur is not content to agree with the commission's proposal, but stresses the need to accelerate and complete the process of liberalisation in sectors such as postal services, electricity and energy, which we reject because it is generally a question of confining the state to a simple function of regulating public service activities, by creating regulatory bodies, allegedly independent, directly or indirectly controlled by economic and financial groups. in general, liberalisation leads to job cuts, higher prices for the provision of public services, a reduction in the quantity and quality of service provision and the denial of the right of universal access to public services.
in writing. - (pt) the khanbhai report provides an extraordinary analysis of one of the most serious challenges facing the world today, namely the fight against the main communicable diseases in the context of poverty reduction. every day, about 10,000 people die from infectious diseases in africa. thirty-five million people are infected with aids worldwide. hiv/aids, malaria and tuberculosis are thus posing a terrible threat to the socioeconomic development of the poorest populations. as our colleague mr khanbhai says, this requires a rapid, effective and broad response, in which the eu will have to take the lead. these diseases not only cause human suffering and many deaths, which is enough to justify this response, but also a severe reduction in life expectancy and the labour force, with all the social and economic consequences that follow (incapacity for work of a large part of the active population, delay in socioeconomic development, destabilisation of already fragile communities). i therefore agree with the rapporteur that there is an urgent need to set up a fund for infectious diseases, which is essential for treating people with infectious diseases, identifying carriers, preventing diseases, and researching and developing new medicines and vaccines.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, before i go into the actual wording of the resolution, i should like to draw the attention of the secretariat and the bureau to the fact that, at least in the portuguese version, paragraphs 2 and 11 of the resolution are virtually identical, certainly because of a lack of attention in the drafting of the text. perhaps after the vote we can merge their people in the world, who are liable to live on less than a dollar a day. and we do so in the wake of the one who proclaimed this day in 1987 and the united nations resolution in 1992 that enshrined it on a world level. we must, of course, turn to the programmes we ourselves have developed to tackle poverty and social exclusion. i would like to draw attention to the resolutions of the european - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, east timor is the first new state to emerge freely and democratically on the international scene at the beginning of the new millennium. the european parliament should be pleased with this result. for several years we have included the question of east timor in the international agenda. we have given voice here to those who have fought to restore the dignity of the timorese people and to recognise their right to decide their own destiny, and we have denounced the violation of human rights by indonesian troops and the militias they support. i cannot help but underline the hard work of so many members. i would like to make a special reference to my colleague carlos costa neves, who chaired the intergroup for east timor and was involved in all the missions that the european parliament carried out in the territory. but if we have helped these people to regain their freedom and democracy, we also have a responsibility to help them consolidate what they have won. east timor needs international political, technical and financial help. without this aid it will not be possible to rebuild what has been destroyed, to organise the new administration and to ensure the necessary development. and that's why we're appealing to the international community for more support and help. we therefore call in this resolution for the extension of the mandate of the united nations, for the return of refugees who wish to return to their homelands and for the fight against impunity for those who have committed crimes and violated fundamental rights. finally, i would like to express my satisfaction at seeing a considerable consensus and multiparty support for xanana gusmão's candidacy for president of the republic. i believe that this sakharov laureate can be a benchmark for unity and a key contribution to the consolidation of the new democratic institutions.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in this section of our work we usually deal with misfortunes, disasters, calamities, things that cause us deep sadness and pain. this has also been the case with east timor in the past, as my colleague carlos coelho has already mentioned. but today is a day of joy. what we have seen here today is the success of the timorese process. and at this moment i also want to testify to this house the recognition of the portuguese and, of course, the timorese, for the growing solidarity that their cause has had in this european parliament and has been spreading throughout the world. my colleague carlos coelho has already referred to the various resolutions and also to the high point which was the award of the sakharov prize to xanana gusmão, the historic leader of the timorese resistance and who will, by general national consensus, be the next president of the republic. this is an example that should stay with us of how with tenacity, with patience we can achieve good results and help excellent causes. three years ago, if anyone in this house said what's happening today was true, they would have called you crazy. three years ago it was impossible to imagine the referendum of august 1999 and the overwhelming support of the people. it was impossible to imagine that the extremely dramatic crisis which followed the referendum would be overcome and that on 30 august we would have the first elections of a new, free and independent state. we have to keep helping this country. we helped a lot to win the independence that they fought for, to defend their identity. we must keep this responsibility alive and alert to the threats that may arise in a geopolitical context which is always very uncertain.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, after the tragedy in east timor, one of the most beautiful pages of contemporary history has been written. the principles of an international political ethic have triumphed in east timor. east timor raises the hope that world history is not just a succession of barbarities and cruelties. in a tragic moment, fraught with apocalyptic fears and anxieties, east timor represents a ray of light and hope. the elections to the constituent assembly in east timor have crowned an impeccable democratic process which honours the united nations and above all the people of timor. but not all is roses in timor, as everyone knows. timor is a very poor country, it has an economy to rebuild, a state to organize, an education, health and social protection system to put in place. only with the help of the international community, and in particular with the help of the european union, can timor make its independence a shining example for humanity. portugal, my country, will not fail to give all its affection and support to timor, since timor has also become a portuguese cause and a universal cause.
