- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to congratulate mr christian von boetticher on his efforts in the two reports he has just presented to us. i should like to concentrate on the second, the one concerning the schengen system, which comes to this parliament not because of a clear political will but because unanimity could not be achieved in the council to maintain the intergovernmental financing system and a community source of financing is therefore necessary. these are the peculiarities of our work in the european institutions: many of the things happen by injunctive and appeal solutions. i would like to say that this proposal for the development of a new generation of the schengen information system, sis ii, can only be accepted as an initial preparation phase for the actual development of the system. community funding does not solve the problems which separate us when we look at the functioning of sis. it is essential, as i argued here last september when i presented my report on schengen, that in addition to community funding, the schengen information system cannot continue to be managed in secret on a purely intergovernmental basis, but rather that it be managed within the european union by a separate agency. at the same time, a single community information system should be set up, consisting of a computer network system containing data received under the three conventions, schengen, europol and customs, taking into account the need to keep the respective data separate. only in this way can we ensure an adequate level of protection of personal data, fair, equal for all citizens, respecting the level of protection required by the charter of fundamental rights. finally, i would like to mention the fact that we know that the sis reaches its capacity exhaustion with eighteen participating states, which means that it will not be able to survive beyond the next enlargement of the european union, so that also forces us to change the system quickly. i hope that the five years we have ahead of us will be sufficient to present a balanced proposal in line with what the european parliament has called for.
- mr president, the initiative of belgium and sweden on the development of the second generation of the sis system - which is based on two legal bases: the decision of the treaty on european union and the regulation of the ec treaty - relates to the objectives of this system, which serves as the third pillar for judicial and police cooperation in criminal matters, as well as the first pillar for visa, asylum and immigration and other matters relating to the free movement of persons. these initiatives are the real prerequisites for the preliminary work which will have to be carried out to give rise to the new sis ii system. they arise, as has already been pointed out here, from the refusal of intergovernmental funding by some member states and are based on the commission's willingness to take the risk and the political burden of developing this new information system in the coming years. the sis, which as we know has only been a community instrument since 1999, has quickly become obsolete. it has been designed for 12 member states, but is now operational in 13 plus iceland, norway and now with the uk and ireland knocking on the door. as we know, in the present historical circumstances it is a decisive means of european information on third country nationals and missing or stolen property, with a view to controlling the external borders or movements in any part of the community territory. in the face of the new enlargement and the challenges of adapting to new technologies and new needs that everyone can guess, it is imperative to develop the current system by financing its modernisation from the community. the need for its development towards the so-called second generation sis is now indispensable for detecting threats arising from the situation of third-country nationals, for allowing provisional detention of criminals liable to extradition, for helping to locate missing or abducted persons, for the surveillance of dangerous persons and for finding stolen property and valuables. i welcome the support of the rapporteur, mr christian von boetticher, who has done an excellent job. and i believe that the final vote on this report tomorrow will result in a text which is much more consensual for the opinion of parliament. mr president, only budgetary financing can give coherence to the new community nature of the schengen acquis, into which the present and future information system is integrated. on the other hand, the new needs for online and in-time judicial information and cooperation are capable of ensuring the security of european citizens. i think that we should not get bogged down in accounting or legal details. the important thing is that sis should go forward for the benefit of the citizens of europe.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, one of the complaints most frequently raised by citizens is that of the diminished role of the european parliament in representative democracy. citizens complain that they are granted the right to vote periodically, but not fully granted the right to participate in the day-to-day running of the institutions they elect: at european, national, regional and local level. it is precisely in the field of the environment that the dissatisfaction of the populations has been most keenly felt, who, disagreeing, often with reason, with a certain project, have no other option but to demonstrate their right to indignation in the streets or in the courts. this indignation is growing exponentially when many of these projects with unacceptable environmental impacts are financed under the common agricultural policy and the structural funds. it is therefore very important that, following the aarhus convention, the commission has been legislating on public access to environmental information, on public participation in certain plans, programmes and policies, and on public access to justice. greater public participation not only gives more legitimacy to the decisions taken by our institutions, but also increases the quality of those decisions. on this proposal for a directive on public participation in the drawing up of environmental plans and programmes, i congratulate the rapporteur on the work she has done, in particular for seeking, through several amendments, to extend the rights of citizens and to remove some ambiguities. there is, however, one point on which i cannot agree with mrs korhola: the public consultation will only be feasible if the definition of public is not too vague. i therefore prefer the commission's approach, which defines the public affected by a particular project as the target of consultation, rather than the rapporteur's choice of a broader and therefore more vague definition of the public.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, despite the support given to some of the european parliament's positions, it is regrettable that the council is persisting in maintaining a proposal for a directive which fails to meet the expectations of workers in the european union. when we know the behaviour of the employers of many companies, particularly multinational ones, who decide to restructure, relocate or even close down partly or totally, throwing hundreds or thousands of workers into unemployment just to reduce costs and/or increase profits, it is unacceptable that the council has not accepted the proposals we have approved here. examples of this are:- the reference to the planning phase in the definition of consultation, its extension to the economic and financial development of the company and the obligation to seek agreement on all matters for which workers are to be informed and consulted;- the extension of consultation in particularly serious cases; the extension of the notion of serious breach of information and consultation obligations; the application of the directive to public administration;- the abolition of the right of the employer to retain particularly sensitive information and the obligation for member states to promote social dialogue in smes. we therefore support the proposals of our colleague, mrs ghilardotti, who takes up some of the positions adopted at first reading, with particular emphasis on the amendments aimed at strengthening the sanctions and specific judicial procedures in cases of infringement of the directive, the suspension of decisions at the request of workers' representatives where their application results in serious consequences for workers, with a view to eliminating or minimising these negative consequences, the elimination of the transitional periods for the application of the directive in the member states where there is no system of consultation of workers, and the reduction of the period for its transposition to two years and its application in the public sector. these are positive proposals, which we support, as i said. but i cannot help but regret that the rapporteur has dropped her two proposals which aimed to eliminate the possibility that the council wants to give the employer in certain cases not to inform or consult the workers, although the rapporteur foresees in her proposals that there may be sanctions for offenders. but the council's position opens up loopholes which can be dangerous for workers, and it is important to eliminate this possibility. that is the fundamental justification for the two proposals we have tabled together with the green group. we hope that the majority of parliament will vote in favour of them and that the council will take account of the positions which have been taken in one of the most sensitive areas of the defence of workers' rights.
- mr president, i would first like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs weiler, on the merit of her report. this parliament strongly supported the quantitative targets set at the lisbon and stockholm european councils for the overall employment rate, the employment rate for women and the employment rate for older people. unemployment, as has already been said, is one of the most serious economic and social problems in the european union and currently one in twelve citizens is unemployed, with the groups most affected being women, young people, the elderly, the disabled and members of ethnic minorities. the guidelines for employment in 2002 must be accompanied, in all their aspects, by qualitative objectives. this is why it is important to adopt special measures to encourage and promote economic and social cohesion in the less developed regions with structural deficiencies. one of the pillars of the employment strategy, entrepreneurship, calls for encouraging the creation and growth of smes, which have a great potential for creating jobs. just look at the latest data provided by eurostat, which reveal that micro enterprises with one to nine employees have recorded the greatest increase in terms of job creation in the european union. reducing bureaucratic and tax burdens is well-signed as a means of stimulating the creation and growth of enterprises. however, we cannot fail to stress that there should be active support for the creation of support infrastructures for research and development, vocational training and lifelong learning in order to ensure quality and continuity in employment. we are all aware that the european union will only become a knowledge-based economy if all member states make investment in human resources a key priority.
in writing. - (pt) we supported this report from its first reading in february 2001. its importance requires our support again, which is why we voted in favour. developing, programming, monitoring and evaluating the european employment strategy involving the social partners and local and regional authorities are important and indispensable objectives for the full and effective realisation of the strategic ambitions defined at the lisbon european council. more and better jobs lead to more economic and social cohesion. to this end, it is essential to strengthen cooperation between member states in the exchange of experience, information and the identification of best practices, as well as the involvement of the social partners and local and regional authorities, without losing sight of the application of an active and transparent information policy for citizens, taking into account their requirements for transparency. in this report, particular attention should be paid to promoting equal opportunities for men and women in the labour market. the implementation of studies on the provision of childcare facilities and home care and support services is an essential instrument for assessing shortcomings at this level in an initial phase and implementing an action plan to facilitate the reconciliation of work and family life in a subsequent phase. we share and support the rapporteur's position on the budget and the annual appropriations.
in writing. - (pt) although it introduces positive corrections in community legislation, this proposal for a directive presents a reductive and inappropriate perspective of the application of the aarhus convention, which has already been ratified by all the member states of the european union. however, the contribution to the application of the aarhus convention in the member states is reduced, as is the application of the convention in the procedures and decisions of community competence. the proposals in the korhola report are generally in the direction of improving the document, but they are not sufficient to solve all the shortcomings of the proposal for a directive, which is why we also voted for other proposals which go further in the report's proposal, thus helping not to lose an opportunity in the difficult process of implementing the aarhus convention. this debate has clearly shown that the importance of the content of this convention and its ratification by all the member states justifies a deeper reflection, a broader and more participatory discussion, a more appropriate and committed response from the commission, not the minimalist vision of the proposal addressed to the european parliament by the commission.
in writing. - (pt) effective public participation in the development of environmental plans and programmes is of paramount importance, and therefore the dissemination of information to the public, particularly via the internet, is necessary. other areas are directly linked to the environment and require public participation - the structural funds, the cohesion fund and the common agricultural policy - which is why the commission must review its decision to treat the policy options as non-binding and ensure that its proposals are the result of the requirements of the aarhus convention for effective public participation. it is necessary to define the 'public' in as open a way as possible. access to resources by small groups of local volunteers, in the face of unequal access to appropriate means of evaluation compared to the resources of large enterprises and public authorities, is important and must be taken into account.
in writing. - (pt) the report recalls that from 1975 to 2000, the world experienced some 120 monetary crises, defined by the imf as the depreciation of a currency by more than 25% in one year. unfortunately, although it is known that each crisis entails very high costs for the countries involved, as was the case with the asian crisis of 1998, the report does not present any substantive measures capable of preventing new crises with any degree of effectiveness. data from the bank for international settlements show a breakneck acceleration in international financial activity, which almost doubled in the 1990s, with stock market speculation increasingly cut off from the real economy, which, combined with the acceleration in the speed of financial circulation, aggravates instability by contributing to further widening the gap between rich and developing countries. however, despite the report's considerations and criticisms, particularly with regard to the lack of democracy in the imf and its use by the us and the g7, it does not propose much in terms of alternatives. on the contrary, it considers it illusory to expect the creation in the near future of a united nations organization exercising full universal powers of regulation and control, when this should be precisely the fundamental path to be encouraged and followed in order to achieve fair, solidary and democratic international relations, taking into account development and the fight against poverty. although the rapporteur's recognition that the way to start from scratch in the direction of growth and the fight against poverty is to cancel the debts of the poorest countries should be welcomed, unfortunately he does not put forward any other concrete proposals, such as a tobin tax or the fight against tax havens, and the plenary rejected by a majority all proposals in this direction.
the rapporteur. - mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, under the guidance of the guidelines for the 2002 budgetary procedure adopted on 3 april, parliament has attached particular importance to improving the efficiency of the european institutions, stressing in this context the ongoing reform of the commission and the quality of budget implementation. in this context, we reaffirm our clear political support for the reform of the commission and establish mechanisms to monitor it effectively, while at the same time trying to avoid delays, such as those concerning the staff regulations in which we want to see the position adopted in a timely manner in a resolution of the european parliament respected - the mechanisms for early reform, the application of which to all the european institutions is essential, and also the strengthening of the capacity of the external delegations, the better coordination between the legislative and budgetary processes, among others. these are well-known objectives and we will continue to work towards them. at the same time, and with a view to improving the monitoring of budget implementation, the commission is expected to set clear targets for implementation and the corresponding timetable, which we will monitor throughout the year on the basis of information that is already available. it is not possible to evaluate the implementation of a budget if we do not have defined objectives and information to verify that these objectives are being met. and it is well known that the european parliament gives clear priority to establishing a more thorough work at the level of budget implementation. it is important for us to know how to forecast next year's budget. by definition, the budget is a forecast. but as important as setting the forecast for the following year is to see to what extent that forecast is put into practice, and, in cases where it is not, to know the reasons why it is not. it will also help to recover overdue payments already identified and to speed up the payment of new commitments. we want to eliminate as much as possible what remains to be liquidated, and we also want to help to prevent the appearance of new residues to be liquidated, new rals, to use the terminology customarily used in this field. the strengthening of operational expenditure will also be promoted by corresponding reductions in administrative expenditure. this is also linked, as we know, to a series of horizontal amendments, i.e. across the whole budget, proposed by the committee on budgets, which are actually intended to ensure a reduction in the ral, a greater readiness to make payments and also a strengthening of operational expenditure to the detriment of administrative expenditure. the commission is therefore expected to be able to provide the following information shortly: first, the study examining the impact of the various measures already taken in the field of agriculture in the context of the reforms envisaged in agenda 2000, and the budgetary consequences of these and the bse and foot-and-mouth disease crises, together with the prospects for the development of the agricultural budget. the european parliament cannot be the budgetary authority in name only. in order to decide on any budget line and, even more so, to decide on any category of the budget, we need to have complete and up-to-date information. and the fact is that at the moment, in relation to the latest reform of the common agricultural policy and in relation to the impact of the foot-and-mouth and bse crises, we need to have more elements. that is why we have been asking for these elements since at least march this year, so that we can then make a conscious decision, both on the 2002 budget and on the amending charter. in the same order of concern for information, we hope that new measures will be identified in response to the situation arising from the terrorist attacks of 11 september, relating in particular to europol and eurojust in the fields of internal affairs and justice, and also in the field of external action with the strengthening of humanitarian aid and the diversification of development cooperation, obviously without prejudice to the commitments previously undertaken. the situation following 11 september, we all accept, has changed a great deal in terms of world politics and therefore, the budget being a mirror of that same policy and translating that same policy, there cannot be any other than the budgetary consequences of this new reality. and this new reality has consequences both for 2001, for 2002 and for the future. and these consequences are already well known in terms of refugees and the dramatic problem they generate, but they are also well known in terms of concerted action in terms of the european union's foreign policy. and we don't want to be systematically confronted with a fait accompli. so it is certainly possible at this stage to predict how these situations will develop. and these new realities must be translated into the budget, they must be accommodated within the budget, and they cannot call into question other commitments already made by the european union. we are very much in favour of what was defined in 1999 in terms of the financial perspective, but we are also very much in favour of social and political dynamics, and in the light of political dynamics, we must draw the necessary consequences. we have repeatedly stated that we will not accept that new needs, new challenges, are met by calling into question old needs, which are as relevant today as they were yesterday. we also have high hopes for the report to be drawn up by the secretaries-general of the institutions on the multiannual analysis of administrative expenditure in the context of preparations for enlargement. we believe that this work, which has been carried out at technical level, can be continued at political level, with the cooperation of the two arms of the budgetary authority. so at this level, we can draw political consequences from the information we obtain technically, which is in progress. continuing on with matters of another nature, but also pending in the case of the commission proposal, it is expected that the proposal for the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument which will provide the necessary means for the conversion of the fishing fleets dependent on the old fisheries agreement with morocco will be formulated in the very near future. this flexibility instrument is a mechanism provided for in the institutional agreement, has been used every year and the corresponding proposal is expected for 2002. as sectoral objectives, i would like to highlight some of those to which we attach particular importance, such as the objective of strengthening the competitiveness of the european union, which is linked to the priority for more and better jobs. it is not enough to make declarations at european councils and european summits, we must then put them into practice, and more and better jobs also mean more and better support, more and better support mechanisms for small and mediumsized enterprises, as well as the development of the e-learning initiative, which we would very much like to see with the corresponding legal basis, and also the consolidation of common policies on asylum and immigration. special reference should also be made here to the intention to support the competitiveness of the border regions of member states in the context of the next enlargement, and also to the extension of the benefits of the leader programme to future member states. all these aspects are taken into account, both in terms of our guidelines, in terms of the proposals we make to amend the budget proposals and in terms of the comments we include in the budget proposals. of course, we will also continue to fight for an increase in the level of payments. i know that this is something that the member states often try to avoid, but the fact is that if we want more effective european institutions and if, on the other hand, we want more credibility for their action, if we want more effectiveness in their results, we must have the necessary means of payment. i would remind you that, in terms of the interinstitutional agreement, in terms of the financial perspective, the level of payments in relation to the gross national product of the member states should be 1.08% in 2002 and the council's proposals are well below that figure. i would like to conclude by expressing my full readiness, in the period between this first reading of the 2002 budget and its vote in december, to coordinate positions with the council and the commission in a systematic search for the most appropriate allocation of available resources, for the most appropriate responses to the challenges of the times. we hope that as from next thursday, and the vote that will then take place, we will have the european parliament's clear proposals on the table. and we would like all the conciliation work that will take place until december to take account of our priorities, to take account of what i have just said to you, namely that the position of the european parliament and the position of the council both deserve respect so that in the end we can take advantage of the best in both proposals. what we certainly don't accept, at present, as we didn't accept in the past, is that there is a kind of deaf dialogue where some say what they want and others accept what some said they wanted. in this particular case, and in order to have an effective negotiation, there must be respect for each other's positions, there must be consideration of each other's priorities and there must be a real look at each other's priorities. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have clear positions, we are prepared to negotiate, and we want the proposals from each side to be examined in depth so that the result obtained is a balanced one.
- mr deputy, your name is not on the official list of speakers registered for the presentation of opinions of committees, so i cannot give you the floor at the moment.
- mr deputy, the list of speakers is drawn up on the basis of official requests from the various committees on whose behalf it is possible to speak. since you are not on the official list and since there is no need to amend it, i shall give you the floor later, when the time comes for you to speak.
- mr president, the proposals for 2002 confirm the trend of recent years towards a decline in the relative weight of the community budget, as is clear from the lowest figure of the decade, of only 1.03% of gnp. the council, because of the restrictive guidelines of the stability pact and also because of the difficulties arising from a sharp economic slowdown, continues to suggest cuts, which creates objective difficulties for budget implementation. this year's procedure once again shows a real budgetary chaos, with new needs emerging and the number of priorities increasing and the appropriations getting shorter and shorter, leading to restraints and cuts, which can only be overcome by a revision of the financial perspectives, as we have always said. the european parliament, therefore, by following the council's logic, is merely trying to cover up the indisputable needs with a short blanket. this is also why there is no clear priority and no coherent strategy to ensure that community policies are fully financed in various areas, such as cooperation, agriculture and employment. this is a matter of concern which is further heightened by the creation of large reserves, which in the present context cannot but raise further doubts. at the level of category four, the only budget chapter where there is still an absolute reduction in amounts, the lack of funds is flagrant, particularly if we take into account the needs and commitments made in respect of the balkans, palestine, afghanistan, the global health fund or the conversion of the fleets operating in morocco. the rapporteur's idea, which is not new, is to cut expenditure, especially with the balkans, in order to be able to increase the remaining aid for cooperation and force the council to negotiate. let us hope that it will not lead to further cuts in cooperation as in the past year. we shall see in december what happens. these are some of the main reasons why we have moved away from this budget.
mr president, the closure, reduction of status and staffing of more than two dozen delegations in acp countries, recently decided by the commission, is a severe blow to the development commitments made in the cotonou agreements. if there is one lesson that i think has been clear about the monitoring of the external development action of the communities it is that the key to its success is the existence of a strong, responsible, autonomous and credible presence in the receiving countries. the arguments of budgetary neutrality used by the commission are fallacious, because opening a delegation in countries like saudi arabia is not comparable to the costs of the general european presence in these african, caribbean and pacific countries. moreover, it is not just a question of looking at the cost of a delegation, but also at the very serious losses in terms of the effectiveness of public expenditure that result from the absence of monitoring of public expenditure. the european union cannot do without an autonomous and consistent policy for the less developed world and replace it with regionalisation tailored to some of the former colonial powers. i hope that the commission will reconsider its approach and give the acp countries and the management of the edf the importance they deserve.
in writing. -(pt) analysis of the 2001 joint employment report, based on national reports, shows that the majority of member states pay little attention to the employment guidelines. only seven member states are concerned with the general objectives defined at the lisbon council and reaffirmed at the stockholm council, and only three have referred to the specific objectives for the female employment rate. as stated in the opinion of the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities, to which we contributed through proposals that were approved, there is a stark contrast between the employment rate targets that were set by the council and the low importance given by most member states to the incorporation of the gender perspective in their employment policies, either because of the lack of national targets to reduce gender disparities in the labour market, because of the inability to provide sufficient childcare services, or because of limited initiatives to reduce the gender pay gap. the weiler report is not sufficiently critical of the current employment situation in the european union, although it does acknowledge some negative aspects of the practices applied in these areas of employment, particularly when it stresses the need to set and achieve national targets. however, it does not critically analyse the guidelines proposed by the commission and it does not clearly present other options that should be pursued, in particular the need to give priority to employment policies and not to continue on the current path of subordinating these policies to monetary policies, which leads to the overthrow of the neoliberal perspective that prevails in the european union, since the report has rejected the proposals we have put forward in this regard.
- the next item is the report (a5-0308/2001) by mr poos, on behalf of the committee on constitutional affairs, on the reform of the council (2001/2020 (ini)).
- mr president, the poos report is simultaneously bold, cautious and realistic. it is bold because it proposes a set of amendments to improve the way the council operates. it is cautious because it recognizes the feasibility of amendments without the need to amend the treaties. it is realistic because it recognizes that it is also necessary to improve politically - and much better - the functioning of the commission and of this parliament itself. the proposals in the report also take account of the idea that improvements in the institutional functioning of the union require significant adjustments to the balance of power which, while not affecting the functioning of the institutions, ensure the full participation of all member states. this is the essential problem, in my view. the major objectives for the european union in the economic, social, security and external spheres will only be achieved in the short term if europe as a whole, through its public opinion, and the 15 member states, through their representatives in the various bodies, take up the priority and the imperative of strengthening political cohesion on a basis of participation which tends to be equal. passivity, indefiniteness, division and controversy between countries, leading to the inability to transform, help to maintain the current state of affairs, giving reason to those who see the european union as a mere group of merchants concerned with dividing, not always equitably and fairly, the limited financial resources at its disposal. strengthening the institutions, improving their functioning and effectiveness, and drawing up and developing community policies in the fields of security and external representation is absolutely essential, but it must be the achievement of all 15 member states, without exclusions and without lists. in this respect, this report deserves our support and our admiration.

in writing. -7 - despite some aspects which we consider positive, we can only express our disapproval and criticism of some very negative aspects of the resolution which was approved today by the european parliament, in particular its support for the us-led war strategy in afghanistan, which not only does not solve any of the problems but also aggravates the humanitarian crisis and the situations of racism and xenophobia. we also criticise the heavy political pressure to accelerate the militarisation of the eu and the communitarisation of certain aspects of 'justice and home affairs', with the 'creation of a genuine european judicial area'. we are particularly concerned about the presentation of proposals aimed at the creation of a 'european arrest warrant' or the controversial and dangerous common definition of the term 'terrorist act', as well as measures seriously affecting fundamental freedoms, guarantees and rights, which are linked to the coordination and effectiveness of law enforcement in a framework of cooperation with the united states. we also note the symptomatic absence in the resolution of any critical reference to the decisions of the ghent council on reaffirming the implementation of the stability pact, with the continuation of the policy of wage moderation, or on labour market reform, on the processes of liberalization of public services - the recent decision on postal services being an example of this - on the reform of public social services or on the council's reaffirmation of the launch of a new round of negotiations in the wto. these measures have a direct impact on the interests of workers and the population in the various eu countries. as for the creation of a "preparatory convention" for the new igc, we consider that its essential objective is to limit or condition the sovereign decisions of the national parliaments. we therefore vote against this report.
in writing. - the poos report on the reform of the council is controversial, contradictory and, although cautiously, it opens the door to european federalism. on the one hand, it has positive aspects in advocating greater transparency, independent of the concept of the evolution of the institutions in an enlarged european union, as is the case when it refers to the functioning of the council and to a greater participation of national and regional parliaments in eu policies. on the other hand, it has very controversial aspects, in particular the transfer to the commission of the functions of the high representative for the cfsp, which implies a reduction in the powers of the council and a greater communitization of this important policy, which can only be opposed by us. at the same time, it is drawing up plans for the functioning of the council which may have a negative impact on the role of national governments, encouraging greater federalism, and among which we can distinguish: a strengthening of the general affairs council, which should preferably meet every week with ministers delegated, the distinction between the executive council and the legislative council, the latter being to function publicly, which seems to be a step towards a kind of senate of the european union. so by voting against this report we confirm our opposition to the federalist project without calling into question the positive aspects of the report.
- mr president, the commission's proposal to create a single annual premium is a positive one because it simplifies and gives greater stability to farmers' incomes. however, it has several shortcomings and deficiencies. the first is the unsatisfactory level of the proposed premium of eur 21, which continues to reflect discrimination in the small ruminant sector against large ruminants. the proposal for the 30 euro that the committee on agriculture has approved will therefore correct this discrimination. however, if this proposal does not have the majority support of this house, then of course i will support any other level that contributes to this objective. the second shortcoming is that there is still discrimination between sheep for meat and dairy sheep and goats, with the latter receiving only 80% of the premium for sheep for meat. the proposals adopted by the committee on agriculture will also correct this inequality by making all premiums equal. the third shortcoming is that flexibility mechanisms are not included to allow member states to make some adjustments to national and regional realities. the amendments tabled by the committee on agriculture, which aim to create a supplementary premium under certain conditions, will greatly enrich the application of the common agricultural policy and introduce greater realism and adaptation to the environment. as the commission itself acknowledges in its explanatory statement, sheep and goat producers have the lowest incomes of all meat producers, with the added problem that 80% of the animals eligible for premium are located in areas of the european union which are both peripheral and disadvantaged. for this reason, and also because we are in a deficit sector which produces only 80% of domestic consumption, it is very important to introduce the above-mentioned corrections and to increase the supplementary premium for the objective 1 regions. i hope therefore that the house will accept the commission's proposal and that the agriculture ministers meeting in the council will do their job properly by taking up the positions of this house. and finally, i would like to pay tribute to the work of mr gordon adam, who was an excellent rapporteur, who worked a lot, and whom i thank for his ideal support.
mr president, the commission proposal insists on the strategy of separating production aids from prices, arguing that it simplifies the current market organization. however, the value of the fixed premium per head is manifestly low given that the basic price has not increased since 1993. and while it is true that the present system needs to be simplified, it is also essential to take into account - as the rapporteur has said - that sheep and goat farming is particularly important for the less-favoured areas of the community where they are concentrated, and therefore any reform of this common market must have as its main objective the improvement of the income of sheep and goat farmers. on the other hand, it must be borne in mind that in recent years the relative position of sheep and goat farmers has declined. the increase in per capita aid to eur 30 and the additional aid of eur 9 for the most disadvantaged regions are therefore positive and deserve my support, since this is a sector whose production is concentrated in disadvantaged areas where the viability of farms is most precarious, despite the fact that they play an important role in preserving the rural fabric of these regions and, in some cases, are even the only possible economic activity. it is also good that the distinction between dairy and beef sheep should be abolished and the same increase applied to goats. i also support the new aid of 5 euros, given the difficulties in this sector, although the danger of renationalisation through the creation of national envelopes must be taken into account. i hope therefore that there will be a positive response from the house - and above all from the commission - to this report and that sheep and goat farmers will see some of their main demands met.

- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, as we all know, harmonizing the different community policies is a necessary task, but it is not always an easy one. simply put, when the fight against world poverty is at stake, our work is not only indispensable but also urgent. fish is a scarce commodity for all of us. for the riverine populations of the poorer countries who live exclusively on fishing, it is a precious resource, their very survival as communities of men and women depends on it. we therefore welcome the commission's communication on fisheries and poverty reduction and support the report by our colleague paul lannoye, which intelligently sets out principles and points out ways of reconciling the supply of the community market with the fishing, processing and marketing activities of the countries with which we have fisheries agreements. the wise chinese proverb that teaches responsible conduct, 'don't give the fish, but teach the fish to fish' also applies here, albeit with some adaptations, because the fish we catch there is, first of all, a good for others. the promotion of scientific research on resources and the fair sharing of this knowledge with developing countries, aid to the organisation of local communities dependent on fisheries, the strengthening and promotion of regional cooperation mechanisms and support in the fight against flags of convenience and illegal fishing are just some of the measures which show us that the path now proposed is a good path to follow together.
- mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr commissioner, fishing is an activity which has accompanied man throughout the ages, but it has never brought about a great accumulation of wealth, just think that the neolithic revolution was made by agriculture and not by fishing. while it is true that fishermen in the european union today enjoy protection and guarantees that provide them with acceptable living conditions, although lower than those of many other workers, unfortunately the same is not true of fishermen and fishing communities in developing countries. for these reasons, we welcome, commend and support the commission's communication, which is clearly inspired by humanism and is further developed in mr lannoye's report, which recognizes that fisheries are a vital activity for many developing countries, threatened with exhaustion because resources are scarce and pressure on species is increasing. the fisheries agreements concluded by the european union with third countries are essential for the survival of activities and cultures that are an integral part of the history and identity of many european countries. however, these agreements cannot ignore and cannot be insensitive to the impacts they have on the vulnerable economies of poor countries and regions of the world. the legitimate interests of community fishermen and consumers must therefore be reconciled with the interests of the poor people of developing countries and with the preservation of fisheries resources, which are often devastated by irrational fishing, including fishing under flags of convenience. in short, the golden rule in this area will have to be to integrate the external implications of the common fisheries policy into the overall development support policy launched by the european union. finally, i would like to express my support for the excellent work of mr lannoye, which is inspired, as i have said, by the commission and contains entirely constructive and fair proposals which we should all support.
- mr president, commissioner, the decision to extend the validity of the mfa-iv until the end of next year, in order to take account of the results of the negotiations on the reform of the common fisheries policy, must not be used to call into question the interests of countries which have so far met and even exceeded the reduction targets set in the mfa, as happened with portugal, while other member states have not only failed to meet any targets but have increased their fishing capacity. it is also wrong, as the rapporteur and other members have said, to want to introduce amendments to the validity of pop-iv which would have a negative impact on maritime safety, the quality of life and safety of crews, the value and quality of catches and health and hygiene conditions. the commissioner should therefore review the position he expresses in these proposals sent to the european parliament and accept the general positions of parliament's committee on fisheries.
in writing - ((pt) the european union, through its development cooperation policy, should strengthen the objectives of fisheries cooperation with developing countries by means of programmes and additional technical and budgetary means in the various priority areas of intervention, which make it possible to contribute to the economic and social development of these countries, in particular rural development, transport, health, education and the fight against poverty. the european union will have to promote scientific research on resources and will be able to support the implementation of an effective monitoring and control programme in the eezs of developing countries.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it was in tampere in 1999 that, for the first time in the history of european integration, the heads of state or government of the 15 member states of the union put justice at the top of their political agenda and committed themselves to creating an area of freedom, security and justice within five years. one of the union's major objectives is to enable its citizens to live in this area of freedom, security and justice, but where this freedom is accompanied by a high level of security, and it is within this framework that the creation and operation of joint investigation teams is envisaged. the rapid establishment of joint investigation teams was already called for in tampere as a first step in the fight against trafficking in human beings and drugs, as well as against terrorism. following the terrible events of 11 september, this initiative comes within the framework of measures to be taken in the fight against terrorism and aims to adopt a framework decision providing the legal framework for the creation of joint investigation teams composed of police and magistrates specialised in the fight against terrorism and representatives of the pro-eurojust unit and europol, in order to coordinate ongoing investigations in this area. i welcome this initiative, which is intended to bring forward the entry into force of the provisions of the convention - which have not yet been ratified - which are considered more urgent by the member states, with the same enthusiasm as the rapporteur, mr kirkhope, and as a tribute to the work done. however, i cannot fail to call here for the need for rapid ratification of this convention in order to avoid the risk of a possible fragmentation of this legal instrument. the extension of europol's field of action to all the serious forms of international crime listed in the annex to the europol convention is also to be welcomed, as this will help to increase the effectiveness of cooperation under the europol convention. however, priority areas for action should be defined on the basis of the evolution of crime in the union. everyone will agree that, particularly at this time, special emphasis should be placed on terrorism.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in the 1957s, through the nordic passport union, denmark, sweden, finland, norway and iceland, five european states, opened their borders to people, while six others drew up a common market, favouring the movement of goods in a customs union. the two groups of states took different paths. the former, by giving shape to efta, the latter, by aiming for a united europe beyond the economic. fortunately, the project embodied by the six, today the fifteen, tomorrow almost twice as many, although more ambitious and complex, has triumphed over free trade or over simple border crossing facilities. it has attracted many of the founders of the nordic institutions and that is why we are now trying in this report to make sense of the contradictions and the setbacks of history by integrating norway and iceland into the schengen area and, very particularly, as in the case of the swedish initiative, into an area of freedom, security and justice in the european union. the swedish initiative thus extends the union's area of freedom of movement beyond the formal geography of europe, with corresponding consequences in the judicial world, making norway and iceland countries where the common rules on extradition to the schengen acquis apply. who would have thought that possible 45 years ago? this historical development, or rather this necessity - which makes new demands and commitments, and thus evolves, and thus imposes the progress of europe - is the basis for the certainty that the tampere programme, which implies the transition from the intergovernmental to the community method, will lead to the communityisation of judicial cooperation, because that is what the governments of the fifteen wanted, because the work of the commission and that of commissioner antonio vitorino cannot be betrayed by chronic reticence on a sovereign basis, and fundamentally because the times are changing and demand that europe should be equipped with a legal and criminal armor which will serve as the first instrument for combating international and cross-border crime. the real difference that we europeans can be proud of is not the arsenal of war, but the quality of the new european standards that we in this house have helped or will help to build, capable of reconciling deterrence and respect for freedoms without attacking the philosophical and cultural differences that influence the criminal standards of any member state. the european arrest warrant, based on the principle of mutual recognition of national judicial decisions, is, as proposed by commissioner antónio vitorino, an example to follow for the other aspects of tampere, particularly for the harmonization of criminal legislation. i therefore believe that this swedish initiative, which we welcome because it is timely and necessary, will soon become obsolete because it has been overtaken by the effectiveness of community rules, particularly on the catch mandate, and will thus make the two conventions of the union disappear, albeit with good intentions, but with little effect. the logic is that they will be replaced by mutual recognition of judicial decisions and the mere transfer of places of persons who have committed crimes, thus creating, commissioner, a single european area for extradition.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating mr marinho on the excellent work he has done on this report and to say that we are all aware of the sensitivity and the undeniable importance for the member states of issues such as asylum policy, measures to combat crime or the organisation of border controls and the fight against terrorism. i believe, however, that everyone now understands that common problems require common solutions, which, in order to be solved in an effective and coherent way, must have a necessary european dimension in addition to their national dimension. the main objectives of police and judicial cooperation in criminal matters within the european union are to combat transnational organised crime and to approximate legal and regulatory provisions. however, in the context of judicial cooperation, schengen cooperation has concentrated its efforts on the rapid processing of extradition requests via sis. the swedish initiative we are considering today does not contain - as our colleague mr marinho has already said - substantive provisions, particularly on extradition, and is aimed above all at clarifying the relationship between the different positions on extradition at the level of the european union and associating iceland and norway with the application of these provisions. i think it is essential to make the area of justice and security in europe a reality. the recent events of 11 september call for the need to fight terrorism not only by military means but also by judicial means. it should be recalled that the european union's strategy for the beginning of the new millennium - the prevention and control of organised crime - already envisaged the possibility of creating a single legal area for extradition at european level. september 11th only reinforced the urgency and the need for such a decision. that is why the justice council decided on 20 september, in its objective of combating terrorism, to replace the extradition procedure within the union by a simple procedure for the surrender of the perpetrators of these crimes, which will mean that all the rules currently in force on extradition will become obsolete, as has already been mentioned here, and will mean the disappearance and replacement of the two extradition conventions by the mutual recognition of judicial decisions and the mere transfer of the perpetrator of the crime, thus creating the single european area for extradition, the need for which we also stress.

in writing. - (pt) under the pretext of the fight against terrorism and the dangers inherent in greater security, a vast and complex mechanism of limiting freedoms is being developed and put in place, which can lead to the creation of a 'fortress europe', which includes this initiative and others such as the one concerning the processing of personal data. in the case of portugal, for example, the fact that europol considers the portuguese geographical area to be the main gateway to europe, combined with the extension of its mandate in the fight against terrorism and organised crime, would make our country a privileged area for the pursuit and monitoring of this structure, outside national policies, the public prosecutor's office and the effective democratic control of its action by the assembly of the republic. these projects aim to coordinate police, information services and files, without any democratic control, in the framework of a restrictive and xenophobic immigration policy, which limits individual freedoms. if there is no democratic control over the schengen information service today, what will happen to the transmission of information to third states and bodies, mainly under the supervision of europol and us agencies, under the pretext of combating terrorism? europol's mandate should not overlap with national policies and the control of the public prosecutor's office. its activities, in relation to each country, must be reflected in the internal security reports. europol shall be subordinate to the european court of justice for all the effects of its action. naturally, any extension of europol's powers must be preceded by a debate in the european parliament and the national parliaments of the member states.
- mr president, the countries of latin america and the european union share a common cultural background which greatly facilitates the mutual relationship between our peoples. however, despite some promising prospects opened up by the first eu-latin america-caribbean summit in rio de janeiro in 1999, which was aimed at a strategic partnership with an economic, social and ecological focus, the concrete results achieved so far are far from satisfactory. the reasons for this deficit are varied and relate to different responsibilities. the quota part of the report which concerns us seems to be a kind of european self-inhibition at times, perhaps even because of the geostrategic primacy, and not only, of the united states over the entire american subcontinent. as is well known, the countries of latin america are faced with joining the american free trade area, which could take place as from 2005 (so we are close!). at a time when the need for strong partnerships on the international scene is becoming more apparent than at any time since the end of the cold war, the european union must not shy away from any of its responsibilities or the challenges facing it. and one of the most important is precisely to be able to define coherent common strategies and give them effective political content. the proposals put forward by our colleague salafranca sánchez-neyra (whom we thank for the excellent work) in this own-initiative report deserve our full support. and not only because they substantiate and enumerate practically all the aspects which should be included in a common strategy for the further development of euro-american relations, but they also deserve our full support because they come at the right time, that is, before the preparations for the second summit of heads of state and government, which will take place next may.
in writing. - (pt) this report seeks to improve the commission's proposal which, despite its name, is actually aimed at privatising passenger transport. although important proposals, which we voted for, were adopted, it is nevertheless true that the introduction of competition could call into question the rights acquired by users (social security contributions) and workers. these rights are not sufficiently protected in this report and will certainly be even less so in the practice of the commission and the council. the mobility of users could be more difficult, despite some positive proposals adopted by parliament which go in a different direction. the coordination between the different modes of transport is also not guaranteed as it should be and there are no appropriate responses for users in less profitable areas, which private operators might not consider profitable and therefore not serve. finally, the vote against the legislative resolution is also justified by our fears about the application of the commission's position and the consequences for workers and users, who can call into question a quality service at reasonable prices. in addition, it is noted that, despite the commission's choice in favour of privatising transport services, part of them will continue to be financed from public money, which is unacceptable.
in writing. - (pt) our vote reflects our position on a highly controversial report, which is rejected by all sectors of the port services. as you know, the commission proposal is aimed at opening up competition to important sectors related to port security and does not guarantee workers' rights. the report itself, although containing some positive references, retains some negative aspects by covering up the commission's position in some way. for countries like portugal, this proposal can have very negative effects on a sector already affected by other measures which are equally negative, which is why we are voting against it.
in writing. - (pt) despite the efforts of the rapporteur and the majority of the members of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, there is no guarantee against non-discrimination towards countries and regions whose business structure does not have economic capacity, although there are scientific resources to participate in programmes aimed at the rapid transfer of knowledge at the level of technological innovation. the cohesion efforts enshrined in the treaty will thus be insufficient in the face of the loss of competitiveness this represents. the lisbon strategy's objectives were full employment and exclusion, not the other way around. without knowledge sharing and participation in programmes, the most disadvantaged regions will remain outside the economic and social convergence.
in writing. - (pt) therapeutic cloning, which opens up new possibilities for curing certain diseases, involves the destruction of embryos. although it is the most studied (and therefore the most accessible) solution, it is not the only one and certainly not the best. it would therefore be wrong for the legislator, in order to give the green light to this technique, to abdicate his obligation to legislate firmly for many years (which is even more necessary in a field that is evolving so rapidly), by signalling to scientists and industry that they must opt for the development of techniques, some of which are now poorly developed and very expensive (such as the manipulation of adult cells), the field of exploitation of which is still wide. we cannot stop fighting for the emergence of techniques which offer the advantages of manipulating human embryos without entailing its ethical drawbacks. furthermore, the creation of embryos through the transfer of the cell nucleus would facilitate cloning for reproductive purposes (which everyone considers to be something absurd). so, on the legal level, i support a ban at european level on the creation of human embryos for cloning (both for therapeutic and reproductive purposes). but this prohibition must allow for two exceptions: surplus embryos and the 64 available cell lines. as regards research funding, and particularly in the context of the sixth framework programme (the subject of this caudron report), i believe that priority should be given to community funding of research projects involving the use of adult germ cells for therapeutic purposes.
- mr president, this commission proposal arises from the problems arising from the liberalization agreements which it agreed to negotiate within the framework of the oecd, while at the same time limiting state aid to the shipping sector, promoting the concentration of shipyards and later eliminating operating aid. now that there are serious problems in the sector which is of strategic importance to the european union, in particular to ensure security of supply under appropriate conditions and to avoid excessive external dependence, it is necessary to support the european shipping industry to deal with the dumping from korea. but the commission's proposal, while important for the moment, is only temporary and partial, and is, after all, on the same plane as korean dumping. in any case, it is important that at least the proposals put forward by the rapporteur be taken into account, including the extension of these operating aids from 6% and in special cases up to a maximum of 14%, also covering other vessels, and we therefore support this report in order to avoid the economic and social problems which already exist in this area being aggravated in the short term.
mr president, i would like to thank the commission for proposing a temporary regulation for the shipbuilding sector, which is certainly segmented, but which, in some way, and once again, contributes to minimizing the effects of protectionism that exists in several trading blocs in the world and not only in korea. and in korea, it's not even about protectionism: this is a major violation of wto rules. and we want to believe that the complaint filed will bring some efficiency and discipline to this sector. the commission has, however, made it clear that it is aware that the mechanism proposed is insufficient, that it is only one step at a time, with all the negative effects of the immediate measures, once the deficit and the crises - some of which have already been mentioned here, and i will not repeat them - in the shipping industry have been overcome. but if it is true that the nordic countries are betting on investment and alternative technology and therefore are able to obtain more value and still remain active in the market without major social upheavals, this is not equally true for other regions of the european union, such as southern europe, regions where this sector is the largest employer, an employer that develops into a set of services and subsidiary industries. and it is often the only activity that generates development in these regions, so the disappearance of the activity would be a social tragedy for certain regions, particularly in my country, spain and greece. but if it is true that we expect the commission to apply this regulation at least until 2003, we expect much more in the future and a different view of the sector.
mr president, i speak on behalf of mrs torres marques, who for important reasons could not be present, and i share her views and the spirit of her speech. for years the european parliament, and in particular its committee on economic and monetary affairs, has been insisting on the need for banks to create payment systems in the euro area that allow citizens to use the euro as if it were a national currency. despite the existence of fixed exchange rates between the different euro area currencies, cross-border payments are processed at the same costs as domestic payments. and the citizens don't understand this: why are euro area payments processed as if they were international payments? is there a euro payment area or not? banks have perfected their national payment systems through credit, debit and e-wallet cards, have perfected their international operations and have forgotten the euro area. the euro zone is a single currency area for us, and it must have common monetary rules that are easily understood by the citizens. it is good that a recent study by the commission, showing that the costs of transfers in the euro area have not decreased but remained high, has made the draft regulation under consideration necessary, which aims to equalise, from january 2002, the costs of crossborder payments by credit and debit cards that each bank makes on its domestic market. from january 2003, the commission proposes to do the same for bank transfers and cheques. the european socialist party agrees with the rapporteur's proposals, and this draft regulation should therefore be approved by us, and we hope that an agreement with the council will be possible at first reading. its application should in no case increase domestic costs. crossborder payments account for only 1% of total payments made by banks, and these are electronic payments which effectively reduce the burden on the banking system. the european institutions must therefore firmly reject the use of this regulation to impose charges on domestic payments. the citizens have already condemned and rejected him. the main amendments adopted include the penalties to be applied in the event of non-compliance by a bank with the regulation, and the procedures to be followed in other situations to ensure that citizens who use the euro are protected. i believe that by adopting this regulation parliament is helping to give the new currency a good launch to the citizens, adding credibility and strength to the euro and facilitating the indispensable cultural and economic transition. we hope that, following the decision of the european council in ghent, this legislation will be adopted as soon as possible. this policy decision will be good for the euro area, but above all it will be good for the european union.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal for a council regulation on the promotion of the conversion of fishing vessels and fishermen, which was dependent on the fisheries agreement with morocco and on which mrs miguélez ramos' excellent report focuses, deserves our unreserved approval. the proposal shows that the european union is concerned about the fate of such vulnerable and fragile activities, the survival of which, as we see, depended on whether or not a fisheries agreement with morocco was renewed. european solidarity works and continues to work. this is for the skeptics. the commission proposal covers the problems of conversion in both its economic and social aspects. it also has the merit of mobilising new money, eur 197 million, which is compatible with the mobilisation of other resources already allocated under the structural funds. in portugal there are 570 fishermen who are receiving support under this programme, 400 of whom live in sesimbra, a small and beautiful fishing and tourist town very close to lisbon. of these 400 who live in sesimbra, 150 are already over 55 years old, which makes it possible to refer them to a pre-reform, since at this age it is difficult to convert them back to another art. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fishermen are not subject to the same treatment as the shipowners. the fishermen can get 10,000 euros within a year if they do not carry on any other activity. the possibility of a 20% increase, albeit modest, would allow them to raise that amount to eur 12 000, which would make it a little more attractive. the proposal is also rather rigid in its architecture and should be more flexible, allowing the member states, in the case of portugal and spain, to adapt better to very complex situations which sometimes do not fit within the rigid framework of the regulation. the commission has also proposed that the aid already granted by the end of 2001 should not be counted in the part of article 4 of the fifg devoted to socioeconomic measures and all of them should be allocated to the lisbon region and the tagus valley, and it would be preferable that they could be diluted in other programmes. finally, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i fully agree with the content and proposals of mrs miguélez ramos' report, which i consider to be clear and sensible, particularly those concerning the possibility of supporting processing activities which were dependent on fisheries. it is absurd that certain processing and marketing activities which are dependent on fisheries and which have a very negative impact on them should not also be financed. we therefore also express our gratitude to mrs miguélez ramos for her report.
- mr president, it is of the utmost importance that this report be adopted so that a decision can be taken at the next fisheries council on the programme for restructuring the fleet operating in morocco, which has been stalled since the end of 1999, affecting portuguese and spanish fishermen and shipowners and the populations of the areas in which they live. it's just that the stop-loss payments are due to expire at the end of this year. the central issue is therefore the financing of eur 197 million for the restructuring programme which is due to start in january 2002 and which must be improved in accordance with the terms proposed in mrs miguélez ramos' report. we, for our part, accept the commission's proposal to use a flexibility instrument in the budget and regret that the matter was not made clearer in the first reading of the budget by the european parliament, by putting the amounts in line and calling for the use of the flexibility instrument, as the committee on fisheries had proposed and as our president has just reminded us. it is therefore important that the next budget council break the deadlock and take these proposals into account, creating the appropriate solution for the fleet restructuring programme to come into force early next year.
- mr president, the commission's proposal is a positive one, but it needs to be improved. i would stress three points in this connection: the first is that the rigidity which the proposal for a regulation imposes in relation to the percentage of funds to be allocated to each type of action is meaningless. what would make sense is that, within the overall envelope with clear rules, each member state should be given the flexibility to allocate these funds according to its needs. for example, in the case of portugal, reduce the funds for the destruction of ships and increase the funds for the restructuring of some of these ships and for the social part. secondly, such an envelope should also include funds for the economic conversion of the areas most affected by fishing and include, as part of these compensations, ancillary activities such as the processing industry. this would imply from the outset that the financial envelope would be increased, which unfortunately has not happened in this commission proposal, which is still struggling with some budgetary procedural problems. the third point i would like to make is that, from the commission's point of view, this aid is essentially intended to close a chapter of the common fisheries policy, which was the agreement with morocco, without making the effort which, in my opinion, was necessary to extend the fishing possibilities of these countries in the waters of third countries, nor does it properly provide for measures, which are also necessary, to help these companies to internationalise more easily. the committee on fisheries has made some amendments, and i would like to pay tribute to the rapporteur, who has worked very hard on this. we ourselves have also tried to respond with amendments which will clearly improve this proposal in the gaps it has. as mrs fraga estévez said, i hope that, since there was no political capacity to negotiate at the time, there will now be dignity in the answers we give to the fishermen concerned. and i hope that the house will accept the proposal of the committee on fisheries and that the council will do the job it has to do.
the commission's report on social inclusion confirms that poverty remains at too high a level. the latest available income data in the member states, although they do not capture the full complexity and multiplicity of the dimensions of poverty and social exclusion, show that in 1997 some 18% of the population of the european union, i.e. more than 60 million people, belonged to households where income was below 60% of the national median income and that about half of these 60 million people had lived below the relative poverty line for three consecutive years. this situation shows that a comprehensive strategy to combat poverty and social exclusion, in defence of human rights in all their multidimensional aspects, is urgently needed and that this programme is an instrument that must be implemented urgently. a year ago we adopted the report on the first reading on this community programme to encourage cooperation between member states in the fight against social exclusion as one of the significant elements of the strategy to combat poverty which the council spoke so much about at the lisbon summit in march 2000. but at the same time, the member states have made new commitments, with the definition of the objectives of combating poverty in nice and in stockholm, where they committed themselves to promoting sustainable development and quality jobs, stating that they wanted to contribute to reducing the risk of poverty and social exclusion. national social inclusion plans have also been drawn up, identifying a range of factors that significantly increase the risk of poverty and social exclusion, such as unemployment, especially long-term unemployment, low income, low-quality employment, lack of housing, poor health, immigration, early school leaving, gender inequality, discrimination and racism, disability, old age, family breakdown, drug addiction and alcoholism, and living in a multi-disadvantaged area. in some cases these risk factors interact and accumulate over time, and it is necessary to break the recurrent cycle of poverty and prevent intergenerational poverty and new forms of poverty, including info-exclusion. it is in this context that the programme takes on special significance as yet another instrument to combat poverty and social exclusion, which as we see continues to progress. finally, after overcoming the council's intransigence on aspects which parliament has always considered essential, in particular in the area of the programme budget, participation and financial support for ngos, the role of the european parliament and the promotion of innovative approaches, a long process took place which included a second reading on 17 may this year, several working meetings with the belgian presidency and conciliation where a consensus was finally reached on 18 september. the agreement reached includes the budgetary aspects and the increase of eur 5 million, now eur 75 million for the five years of the programme, which is still too little, but given the council's intransigence, a no-agreement would mean that the programme would not enter into force at the beginning of next year. the promotion of innovative approaches and financial support for actions carried out by ngos was also confirmed, rising from 80 to 90% under exceptional conditions. it should also be noted that the participation of ngos, the european parliament, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions in the preparation and implementation of the annual round table on the development of the situation and the implementation of the programme was confirmed. it should also be noted that the role of the european parliament has been recognised, in particular in the timely assessment of the summary report for the spring council which the commission will draw up annually and in which it will assess the overall coherence of policies with social cohesion, including the progress achieved under this programme. i would like to thank all those who have taken part in and contributed to the implementation of this proposal and the consensus reached at the council, the commission and the european parliament.
