in writing. - (pt) some of the rapporteur's amendments indicate that the increase in nuclear power capacity installed in europe is essential to comply with the kyoto protocol. this argument lacks rationality and objectivity. all the evidence is to the contrary. between 1990 and 1999, the european union already reduced its greenhouse gas emissions by 4%, and will be able to achieve the remaining reduction share (4%) by 2012, thanks to low-cost measures. according to the european commission, it is possible to meet kyoto by applying measures at a cost of less than eur 20 per tonne of co2. the promotion of nuclear energy is not among the measures proposed by the european commission (european climate change programme and emissions trading framework directive).
, in writing. - (pt) despite the protest of the portuguese ngos and myself, the portuguese government insists on its maximalist approach to the alqueva project, preparing to fill up to 152 m. this communication and this report on sustainable water pricing show that filling up to 139 m, as i propose, would not only save more than 600 000 trees, but also very important habitats, and would be economically more sustainable. let us risk dropping to 500 feet and then, because of the price of water, we find that filling to 500 feet would be enough for agricultural needs.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this proposal because it is the lesser of two evils. however, unfair competition, now from korea, is permanently shifting activity to the east and leading to the disappearance of all shipbuilding, repair/transformation activity in the european union. without active, state-of-the-art shipyards, maritime security, employment and regional cohesion strategies are threatened and difficult to overcome by palliatives.
in writing. - (pt) i welcome not only the content but also the importance (as reflected in the strategy adopted by the european union for the rio+10 conference in 2002) which the european commission attaches to the new chemicals policy. i consider the following points to be particularly positive: 1) total prohibition, within one generation (2020), of the discharges and emissions of all hazardous substances into the environment. 2) creation of a system for the registration, evaluation and authorisation of chemical substances (reach), replacing the current system which treats new substances and existing substances separately. 3) enshrining the precautionary principle by reversing the burden of proof for substances of very high concern. it is up to the manufacturer to prove that a product is not dangerous. as regards the schörling report, i think it is very positive, but i prefer the position of the european commission on the tonnage threshold. the system will protect citizens if it proves to be applicable. the same assessment of substances produced in quantities of less than or more than one tonne per year will weigh on the system and thus make it inconsistent with the objectives set.
in writing. - (pt) the rio summit of 28/29 june 1999 marked the beginning of a new stage in bi-regional relations with a view to the creation of a strategic partnership between the european union, latin america and the caribbean. in line with the priorities agreed at rio, the main objectives of the new common strategy will be: at political level, the expansion of the bi-regional political agenda; the updating and revision of the ministerial political dialogue and the creation of a euro-latin american transatlantic assembly; the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's external action programmes, including the implementation of the eu's external action programmes. the commission has also proposed the creation of a bi-regional solidarity fund and a comprehensive plan to combat drugs, among other measures. i therefore fully support the rapporteur's position, which reiterates the need to realise the strategic partnership between the two regions adopted at the rio summit and in the recommendation to the european council for the adoption of the eu common strategy for latin america and the caribbean, on the basis of the objectives set out in the priorities of the rio summit. finally, it stressed the need to strengthen cooperation on the basis of a sustained and integrated partnership aimed at promoting human development and civil society and, consequently, the protection of all sections of the population.

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, this problem of the trade in and trafficking in small arms is one of the most terrible problems facing the world today. the first point i would like to make is that this expression 'small arms' misleads the public, because small arms also include: heavy machine guns, rocket-propelled guns, hand grenade launchers, mortars of a calibre of less than 100 millimetres. we are indeed talking about terrible weaponry. and to quote the secretary-general of the united nations, kofi annan, at the conference last july, we must be fully aware of the devastating effects of the current arms trade. it is estimated that there are 500 million of these guns in the world, and they kill an average of over a thousand people a day. this is therefore a matter which must be given serious attention by the states. i believe that this is one of the areas where the line between seriousness and hypocrisy is most clearly drawn. for many years i have argued that the question of arms industry and trade should be a matter for the state. the market economy applied to this area can only have terrible consequences and, when we complain about trafficking and brokering, we had to take stronger measures to ensure effective state control of the production and trade of these weapons. but unfortunately, governments are not always blameless either. in portugal, a campaign is currently under way, launched by catholic missionary organisations and supported by the portuguese section of amnesty international, to draw attention to this scourge. and why the missionaries? because missionaries, particularly in africa, are often the ones most in touch with the terrible consequences of this reality. unfortunately, my country - not just france, the united kingdom and the union countries often mentioned - is not exempt from shortcomings in this area. according to the data that have been made public, in the year in which the european union code of conduct was signed, in 1998, portugal sold some eur 9 million worth of arms to countries that should feel prevented from doing so, such as angola, algeria, colombia, israel, jordan, kuwait, sri lanka and turkey. and sold some 5 million euros worth of arms to 11 other countries that should at least be under strict observation. we support the resolution as it has been tabled. this is a matter where some steps have been taken, but where it is important that more energetic steps be taken. the european union must above all set an example in this area. we cannot spend thursday afternoons crying crocodile tears over conflicts that are continually fuelled by the legal or illegal trade in these weapons.

- the next item is the report (a5-0377/2001) by mr lynne, on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the proposal for a council decision on the european year of disabled people - 2003 (com (2001) 271 - c5-0306/2001 - 2001/0116 (cns)).
deputizing for the draftsman of the opinion of the committee on citizens' freedoms and rights, justice and home affairs. – mr president, i would just like to say that i am standing in for my colleague, mr andrews, and i am very honoured to do so because of the important issues in this excellent report by mrs lynne. the purpose of the year is to provide for a coordinated effort to change attitudes and to promote the removal of social and environmental barriers to equality and to assist in legal and policy developments. this means putting a value on diversity and celebrating diversity. it also includes making us aware of issues relating to multiple discrimination. i want to stress our full support for the designation of 2003 as european year of people with disabilities. i know that the committee on citizens' freedoms and rights, justice and home affairs also supports the objectives of the year, as members unanimously backed my colleague's opinion. there are approximately 38 million people in the european union who have a disability. they face a wide range of barriers, including different forms of discrimination. people with physical and sensory disabilities are just as much a part of our society as able-bodied people. very often, however, they are hindered from taking part in our society. what makes it worse, as mr andrews states in his opinion, is that they are not hindered by their disabilities, but by their environment and the attitudes towards them. they suffer multi-discrimination. this must change. some of the problems include public transport and access to buildings, stairways and doors which are not wide enough to allow wheelchair access. surveys show that the intellectual capacity of people with physical disabilities is very often under-estimated. they can be discriminated against when applying for jobs. the exclusion of people with disabilities from society not only denies their fundamental rights, but also deprives society of diversity and inclusivity. people with disabilities can make a positive contribution to society. their experiences can encourage more tolerance, understanding and awareness of the needs of others besides their own capacities and abilities. our own goal must be to end discrimination and safeguard their rights. it is important that discrimination against people with disabilities is tackled as a mainstream issue. the four amendments presented by my colleague, mr andrews, were fully supported by the rapporteur, mrs lynne, and endorsed by the committee on employment and social affairs. they are now amendments nos 20, 45, 47 and 48. i have nothing else to add, only to congratulate mrs lynne on her excellent report and on the very touching speech she gave to us in the presentation of the report.

- mr president, as regards the european strategy to promote quality in work and social policy, it is not enough to state and approve intentions: it is essential that quality employment with rights is created and that concrete objectives are set for the effective fulfilment of all social rights. it is not enough to create jobs: it is essential that it should not be precarious, that wages should allow people to live with dignity and be paid on time, and not as is happening in portugal, where, despite the low official unemployment rate, the lowest wages in the european union are maintained, if precariousness worsens, cases of companies with backward wages return and the development of areas entering crisis is hindered. a quality-oriented policy at the social level requires that priority be given to employment with rights, with particular attention to women and young people, including their training, but also to remuneration and contractual ties, and also special attention to the elderly and to improving pensions and pensions, especially the minimum, which implies that the european union cannot continue to insist on the absolute priority of the monetary policies of the stability pact, as is still the case.

in writing. - (pt) i congratulate our colleague mr foster on his excellent report, which i fully support. his work reveals a great deal of technical knowledge in the approach to a subject which is of vital importance for the future of civil aviation and in relation to which i would like to underline the following points: - the need for member states to commit themselves to the implementation of an integrated policy for the financing of air transport safety, which should remain the sole responsibility of governments, since the threats to air transport are a threat to the states themselves; - the need to provide for a number of common standards in the field of civil aviation, following the european civil aviation conference, in order to increase safety at airports in the european union.
in writing. - (pt) europe must cooperate more effectively in the fight against terrorism, both at home and internationally. we do not need to say that terrorism is one of the greatest threats to democracy, the free exercise of human rights and economic and social development. terrorism is not a recent phenomenon, but in recent years there has been a resurgence of terrorist activities, with a profound change in the nature of terrorist offences (new threats have emerged) and with the use of new and more sophisticated instruments, making the actual or potential effects of these attacks increasingly devastating and deadly. the terrible events of 11 september have painfully demonstrated this reality and the urgency of an effective response to this scourge. it is therefore vital that our member states have effective criminal legislation to combat terrorism and that measures are taken to strengthen police and judicial cooperation, as well as cooperation at international level. the first of these initiatives proposes the adoption of a common definition of terrorist acts and the levels of sanctions (which should be effective, proportionate and dissuasive), reflecting the seriousness of such acts in all member states. at present, there are wide differences between member states. only six member states have specific legislation on terrorism (one of these is portugal), so harmonisation is essential at this level, to avoid that terrorists can take advantage of these differences in legal treatment between states or exploit legal loopholes arising from the geographical limits of investigations. the second initiative is based on the principle of mutual recognition of judicial decisions. it aims to replace traditional extradition procedures (extradition being a long and complex aspect of the procedure, it is no longer suited to an area without borders) with a simple system of transferring criminals on the basis of a european arrest warrant. under this system, an arrest warrant issued by a member state will have to be executed throughout the union, but will always, in all cases, have to respond to the concerns of european citizens regarding the guarantee of individual rights. the adoption of these proposals will thus be clear evidence of the willingness of the member states to strengthen their cooperation at all levels in the fight against terrorism.
in writing. - (pt) it is difficult to sum up in such a short speech what should be emphasized about the dangerous negative aspects of these commission proposals for framework decisions, which are incomprehensibly supported by the portuguese government. under the pretext of fighting terrorism, the european commission is putting forward proposals that it had already prepared, with commissioner antónio vitorino as the protagonist, who, going far beyond the necessary cooperation between states, is seeking to advance the supranationalisation of essential aspects of justice. the concept of 'terrorism' proposed by the commission makes it possible to 'criminalize' the actions of those who fight for national liberation and for the transformation of society, the struggles and actions of workers and the population, with obvious consequences for the full guarantee of democratic rights and fundamental freedoms. the proposal for a 'european arrest warrant', which aims to abolish the principle of double incrimination - even if it is limited - is the real 'trojan horse' of a strategy which will require more and more progress, justified by the 'necessities' of the instruments created previously. despite the amendments concerning certain negative aspects of the commission's proposals, the report follows the same logic. that is why i voted against it.
in writing. - (pt) i voted in favour of this proposal because it is part of a coherent set of measures which can and must help to enable europe to cooperate more effectively in the fight against terrorism, both at home and at international level. during this meeting, we welcomed the initiatives concerning the adoption of a common definition of terrorist acts and levels of sanctions, as well as the replacement of traditional extradition procedures by a simple system of transfer of criminals, based on a european arrest warrant. the proposal on eurojust aims to effectively improve cooperation between the judicial authorities of the member states (by ensuring the protection of fundamental rights), since the fight against terrorism and other forms of crime is only possible through the strengthening of cooperation between the judicial authorities and also the police (with particular importance in relation to the role to be played by europol). this is an important and ambitious project, because crime knows no borders. it is therefore important to promote this judicial cooperation, as well as the exchange of information between member states. the citizens of the european union have the right to demand that the union effectively address the growing threats to their freedoms and rights posed by crime. this requires the adoption of a new strategy, which consists in coordinating all efforts to combat and prevent crime throughout the territory of the member states.
in writing. - (pt) the report is part of the strategy of those who count on the next intergovernmental conference to take a new qualitative and significant step in the federalistic integration of the eu, under the domination of the great powers, which will inevitably call into question the sovereignty of countries like portugal. he insisted on speeding up the preparation of a new treaty to deepen federalist integration, 'forgetting' the lessons and political consequences of the irish people's rejection of the treaty of nice. among many other aspects which i criticise, this report defends the creation of a 'convention' - which some see as a 'constituent assembly' - which it positions as an instrument to condition the exercise of the exclusive competence of national governments and parliaments to revise the treaties, since it guarantees, as regards its composition, only the participation of the largest national parties, by reducing the participation of parties with different political positions on europe, calling into question the pluralism and the genuine independent and democratic participation of national parliaments. that is why i am voting against.
in writing. - (pt) i support the prohibition at european level of the creation of human embryos by cloning (for therapeutic and reproductive purposes) for two reasons: 1) we cannot afford to ignore the emergence of techniques which present the advantages of manipulating human embryos without the ethical disadvantages. the legislator would be wrong if, in order to give the green light to this technique, he were to renounce his obligation to legislate firmly for many years (which is even more necessary in a field that is changing so rapidly), by telling scientists and industrialists that they must opt for the development of techniques, some of which are still underdeveloped today and very expensive (such as the manipulation of adult cells), but for which there is still a wide field of exploration. 2) the creation of embryos by the transfer of the cell nucleus would facilitate cloning for reproductive purposes (which everyone considers to be aberrant). it is for this reason that the english constitutional court has just this week rejected the law which authorised therapeutic cloning. however, this prohibition must allow for three (strictly controlled and regulated) exceptions: - research on surplus embryos (created for in vitro fertilisation but no longer able to be implanted in the genetic parents, which are therefore frozen and destined to be destroyed) should be allowed; - research on the 64 cell lines already in existence should be allowed; - research on embryonic or foetal stem cells from spontaneous or therapeutic abortions should be allowed.

in writing. - (pt) some progress in the creation of the european food authority is positive, although i continue to maintain that it cannot serve as a panacea for solving food safety problems or a smokescreen for public opinion. there is a need to ensure that the food safety risk assessment is carried out by an authority based on sound scientific advice; the competent authorities in each member state which cannot resign from their responsibilities or replace the commission at the level of risk management of community food policy, including the competent authorities in each member state. the fundamental principle of all food law is the protection of human health; it is therefore essential to introduce the precautionary principle in risk management where scientific data are insufficient or inconclusive. however, it is worth asking whether such a structure, which is burdensome from an administrative and budgetary point of view, is necessary, while stressing the need to ensure the participation of one member from each member state in the administrative board. in any case, it must be reaffirmed that the food problem can only be solved by means of a fundamental change in the cap and a genuine food policy which is not based on the interests of the multinational agro-food industries or on the liberalisation of agricultural trade.
in writing. - (pt) the launch of the growth and jobs initiative is a very positive step towards increasing and improving employment prospects in the eu. in recent years, smes and micro-enterprises have created the largest number of new jobs. the report presented by the commission provides an objective analysis of the development of the three financial instruments for smes: start-up support (ste), european joint undertaking (ejn) and sme guarantee mechanism. the critical remarks in the report by our colleague mrs bushill-mathews, which i share and for which i voted, are however very relevant. i would first of all stress the need for the commission to make clear in future the extent to which the job creation forecasts have been achieved. secondly, the commission will have to carry out a thorough analysis of the reasons why some countries have a high rate of use of the various programmes and others a low or no rate. in this connection, i would remind you that portugal is one of the member states not covered by the mte start-up support programme, which is intended for early stage investments in high-technology areas. the rate of utilization of the sme guarantee mechanism is already very low for the period under review, 31/12/2000. according to preliminary data provided by financial intermediaries, only 8 portuguese smes benefited from a guarantee under this mechanism, compared to 2,812 in spain. this analysis will be a highly important indicator for the definition and adoption of adjustment measures, in particular as regards transparent access to information, simplification of the application procedures and the speed of the allocation of the amounts.
the commission has also taken the initiative of drawing up a report on the implementation of the budget, which is to be submitted to the council in the near future. in view of the balanced outcome, it is right to distinguish the work of the members of the committee on budgets, the essential contribution of the rapporteurs of the other committees, the responsible involvement of the political groups, assessment of the results achieved should distinguish between the establishment of better conditions for continuing to improve the implementation of the budget and ensuring greater effectiveness. the joint declarations between the european parliament, the council and the commission are very important. for the first time, the council accepted amendments adopted at the first reading by parliament in the context of the common agricultural policy, which opens up interesting prospects for cooperation. new needs arising from the non-fulfilment of the fisheries agreement with morocco and the impact of neighbouring countries. i would also stress the strengthening of the capacity for action in the field of e-learning and the special emphasis given to responding to the plight of refugees. support for the reform of the commission and progress in preparing for enlargement are also covered. the respect of the commitments made in the past, both in the context of agenda 2000 on the common agricultural policy and the structural funds, on programmes resulting from codecision and in the field of external action policy, are also matters to be considered. of course, new challenges are bound to arise. i would point out that the appropriations required for expenditure in categories iii - internal policies, iv - external policies and v - administrative expenditure are under enormous pressure and the fact that the situation is under control for the time being should not mislead us. the problems will become more and more complex in the coming years and the solutions will become more and more difficult. in this context i note and deplore the rigidity of the budgetary structure resulting from the current financial perspective, which leads to the existence of budgetary imbalances in some categories and an increasingly complicated situation in others. who can understand that, with the difficulties in various areas, such as external action, there was a balance of 11 billion euro in 2000, about 14% of total expenditure? let us have the courage to adopt the solutions which are so obvious in this case in time. at the same time it must be reaffirmed that the division of budgetary expenditure into compulsory and non-compulsory is completely anachronistic and, in the light of the most elementary principles of democracy, it is unacceptable that parliament's actual competence is limited to non-compulsory expenditure (about half the budget) and incomprehensible that it has no competence as regards the revenue system. the 2002 budget is there and i believe it deserves the approval of the members. as for the tasks for the future and the new role of the european parliament in the budgetary process, we will be here to tackle them. thank you all again!
- madam president, we are concerned about the decisions taken at the world trade organisation meeting. its short and medium-term repercussions could be disastrous for the people of many countries, especially the least developed countries, but also some of the european union, notably portugal. for my country, the opening up of agriculture and textiles is particularly worrying, including the bilateral agreements being negotiated by the european union, such as the one with pakistan, without taking into account the specific features of portugal. the essential thing has been achieved for the economic and multinational groups: the opening of negotiations for greater liberalization of world trade, which were postponed in seattle and clearly called into question this year by world organizations, particularly those present at the world social forum in porto alegre and genoa. the declarations of joy of unice (the european employers' organization) demonstrate this well when it says that the basic has been achieved, even if not all its demands have been completely met. the final wto resolution itself, aiming at liberalisation in particularly sensitive areas such as textiles, important aspects of agriculture, services and others, including in the field of intellectual property, leaves little doubt, although its practical implementation may still be influenced by the struggles that will develop. even some small positive advances in the field of intellectual property, after the pressures that countries such as brazil and south africa have developed to obtain generic drugs to combat aids and malaria, demonstrate that it is important to intensify the path of the fight against capitalist globalization in its various aspects, whether political, economic, social or environmental. the trade union organisations in the european union have already condemned the opening of negotiations in the wto without at least ensuring real progress in defending compliance with the ilo conventions and minimum standards for the protection of workers' rights, since there can be no sustainable development without social and environmental development. the liberalization of international trade favours rich countries and multinational companies, widening the gap between developed and less developed countries, as has been shown by un studies, and intensifying the exploitation of people and workers by multinational companies which dominate international trade and are only interested in maximum profit, even if it is at the expense of child labour, food security, the environment, family farming and multifunctional agriculture. this is why it is important to mobilise for the fight for an alternative to sustainable development which gives priority to poverty reduction and social expansion, which promotes the revision of the cap, which is more coherent, more supportive and less isolated from the countries of the south.
- madam president, commissioners, colleagues, it is too early to say who won or lost in doha on the agricultural cap plan, because all that was at stake was an agreement on an agenda and a timetable for negotiations. the winners have so far been the developing countries, whose main demands have been accepted in the text, including those of the acp countries. the cairns group and the united states saw the principle of continuing agricultural reforms, improving market access and phasing out export subsidies enshrined in the text. the european union has ensured that this reduction in export subsidies is not automatic and has seen some of its main complaints about strengthening the non-trade components of the agreement come to pass. despite the relative optimism of the community negotiators, the agreement did not enshrine some of our fundamental aspirations. the text does not explicitly mention agricultural multifunctionality and the strengthening of the protection of geographical indications is explicitly mentioned only for wines. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and so it will not be difficult to conclude that the next reform of the cap, before 2006, will have to be more profound than one might expect. not only by the reduction in guarantee prices which this will entail, but especially by the qualitative change in the instruments of support for farmers so that, even without market support, they continue to have an income safety net. doha was just a starting point and much is still open. but let there be no illusions. today our trade rivals are fighting for the end of the subsidies which are supposed to be the most distorting of international competition, but tomorrow they will want to impose an end to all kinds of subsidies to agriculture, that is to say, the old story of rings, fingers and then the rest. it would be unfair to end without a special salute to our commissioners for the courage and steadfastness they have shown in these negotiations.
- madam president, commissioners, members of the european parliament, the president of the commission was very optimistic this morning about the future of europe. 'the great europe is taking shape', said mr prodi. the question is: is a europe that is great in spirit and great in will taking shape? a europe with new aims, a europe able to play its role in the world better? i doubt it. it's okay to doubt. the world trade organization conference in qatar alone justifies a degree of mitigated optimism. the action taken by the european union through its representatives, namely commissioners pascal lamy and franz fischler, was difficult but frankly positive. european diplomacy has in fact made a decisive contribution to the successful outcome of the conference and thus to ending the paralysis of the world trade organisation and restoring its credibility after the disastrous failure of the seattle conference. the commitments made by the european union are far from meeting our main priorities, particularly in the environmental and social fields. the european representatives were faced with a dilemma: the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. there are, therefore, reasons for hope, but also reasons for disappointment. the positive aspect of the negotiations on agriculture is underlined, as it finally gives impetus to a thorough reform of the cap which the european union was unable to carry out internally. the results achieved in terms of drug prices and the undeniable victory over the selfishness of the multinational pharmaceutical companies are also commended. the doha conference represents only a small step on the arduous road to reconciling liberalism and regulation, a small step towards globalisation contributing to world progress and in particular to the reduction of poverty, hunger, disease and violence. finally, if the vote is not taken, the president will interrupt the speaker.
- mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, we are just over two years after tampere and the entry into force of the treaty of amsterdam. it is important to point out that enormous progress has been made, but that on the other hand there are still many obstacles to overcome. the union is rightly proud of its constant advocacy for respect for human rights in the world and within the community. the recently proclaimed charter of fundamental rights has reinforced our commitment to this and the area of freedom, security and justice only makes sense with the three components, without any of them affecting the others. the tragic events of 11 september and the increased attention paid to security measures must not undermine this fundamental approach. the european union must not be allowed to become a victim of terrorism, but must be able to take action to combat it. the commitment of the member states to building an area of freedom, security and justice was reaffirmed once again at the last justice and home affairs council. it is essential that within this space citizens can enjoy the right to express themselves, demonstrate and assemble freely and peacefully, provided that they do so in a way that does not constitute a threat to their own safety, the safety of other citizens and property. we must not overdo it here either. any individual or group of persons who wish to participate in legitimate demonstrations should not be blocked at the borders, that is to say, they cannot be denied the right to cross borders, that is to say, they cannot be denied their right to freedom of movement, which is a fundamental right conferred on all citizens of the union by the treaty. it's that security is only an instrument to ensure freedom. security loses its meaning when it compromises freedom. security without freedom is tyranny. at christmas time, i wish commissioner vitorino well for the continuation of his excellent work.
- mr president, commissioner, we have just heard from three personalities who have been awarded the sakharov prize. we were moved by the talks and encouraged by the example, but we also felt a sense of unease. the european parliament has been able to identify ways of preventing and resolving conflicts and to identify, even before others, those who can promote them, but we have lacked the capacity to influence these processes. i remember attending a meeting chaired by mrs fontaine earlier this year with commander massoud. at the time, few were interested in the visit and we were unable to mobilize the support needed to bolster commander massoud's position and perhaps prevent some of the great tragedies that followed. we therefore welcome the commission's communication and acknowledge its work and merit. we fully agree with the strategic principle stated by the commission, and i quote, 'development policy and other cooperation programmes are the most powerful instruments available to the community to deal with the causes of conflict'. we support the aims of the document in general; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we believe that a strong and serious effort towards interinstitutional cooperation is a priority. as for the specific aspects, they are listed quite fully by the rapporteur, whom we congratulate. but i would like to emphasize one point: the role of the commission's delegations should be strengthened, which also relates to the issue of the union's external representation. the commission document speaks well of joint training of diplomats when it refers to the euro-mediterranean partnership. and i ask: when will we have joint training programmes for diplomats within the european union itself?

- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, this debate on the european union's fisheries agreement with mauritania, together with the vote we held yesterday on the fisheries agreements with cape verde and guinea-bissau, is a good opportunity for political reflection on international fisheries agreements. and i would like to make three brief remarks in this connection. the first is the economic and social importance of these agreements for the european union. as is well known, these fisheries agreements with third countries account for 25% of the supply of the community market and represent some 50 000 jobs. and it is proved, as was done in the efremer study, that they are highly advantageous for the european economy. the second point concerns the importance of agreements for the economies of developing countries: it is clear that the european union cannot paternalistically and arrogantly impose the headings where compensation should be applied, but little by little and with diplomacy we have been moving towards a situation where part of these financial compensations are applied in the fisheries sector and in important activities. thirdly, it is clear that there is a mutual advantage to these fisheries agreements for both parties, the european union and third countries. finally, i would like to refer to the gallagher report, since the agreement with mauritania is currently the most important agreement concluded with third countries. given the fact that the agreement with morocco has not been renewed, this agreement with mauritania is now, as the rapporteur has said, of particular importance for portugal and spain, but also for italy and france, which are countries that also fish in these waters. as is well known, the committee on fisheries has approved an amendment proposing that quotas be redistributed among the countries if those for whom they are intended do not use them. i must say that my group is in favour of this principle, provided that it is henceforth applied, of course, to all fisheries agreements and not just to agreements with acp countries. finally, i would like to thank mr gallagher for the excellent work he has done on this report.
in writing. - (pt) i would like to express my agreement with the new fisheries protocol signed between the european community and mauritania for the period from 1 august 2001 to 31 july 2006, which i consider to be of vital importance for the european community, not only for the fishing opportunities it offers and the financial amount involved, but above all for socioeconomic reasons, by allowing the relocation to new fishing areas of a large number of vessels operating in moroccan waters. as for mr gallagher's report, i would like to congratulate the author on his work and say that i agree with the conclusions he draws in his explanatory statement. finally, as regards amendment no 4 (article 3b), i would like to stress that the council only gives the commission a negotiating mandate for the conclusion of new fisheries agreements and not for the renewal of implementing protocols, provided that they do not change the substance of the framework agreement. i therefore advocate a new wording of the rapporteur's proposal to bring it into line with community law on the subject.
